When the choir sings “Glory,” and “Now and,” the Priest, having vested in the phelonion and bowed unto the holy table, makes the entrance with the censer, reciting within himself the entrance prayer: “O good and man-befriending King...” etc., and having come through the northern doors before the royal doors, bows unto the holy table, and blessing the incense with three fingers, saith: “Blessed is the entrance of Thy holy ones, O Lord...” etc., and censes the holy table, the icon of the Savior and of the Mother of God. Upon the conclusion of the sticheron he opens the royal doors, makes the sign of the cross with the censer, and proclaims: “Wisdom! Stand upright!” (*Премꙋ́дрость про́сти!*) Thereafter, having entered into the holy sanctuary, at the steps he bows unto the holy table; and while the choir sings “Gladsome Light,” the Priest censes around the holy table, and from the royal doors the icon of the Savior, of the Mother of God, and the people. After this the Priest hands over the censer and departs unto the high place. When the choir hath finished singing “Gladsome Light,” the Priest proclaims: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” Whereunto the prokimenon is sung. After the prokimenon the Priest proclaims: “Wisdom!” (*Премꙋ́дрость!*) whereupon the reader begins the preface of the paremia, for example: “The reading from Genesis,” or “The reading from Exodus”; and the Priest again proclaims: “Let us attend!” (*Во́ньмемъ*) and the reader reads the paremia. Upon the conclusion of the readings, the Priest goes up by the northern side unto the holy table, but first shuts the royal doors, and here loudly proclaims the litany: “Let us all say...” etc., with the Exclamation: “For a merciful God...” etc. After this litany the choir reads: “Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this evening,” and afterwards the Priest recites the second litany: “Let us complete our evening prayer...” etc., with the Exclamation: “For a good God...” etc. Further: “Peace be to all,” “Bow your heads unto the Lord,” and the Exclamation: “Blessed and glorified be the majesty...” etc. After this the choir comes forth into the midst of the church and sings the Litiya stichera (3) with “Glory,” and “Now and.” The Priest with the censer comes forth around the holy table out of the sanctuary by the northern doors before the royal doors, saying nothing, and censes the holy table, the icon of the Savior and of the Mother of God. After this, having handed over the censer, he goes, with candle-bearers preceding, unto the prepared tetrapod in the midst of the church, whereupon 4 vessels are set cruciformly. — The dish with 5 loaves

=== LEAF p89 ===
[Book Page 65]

stands on the side toward the sanctuary (lit. 'from the mountain'), and below (toward the west) stands the vessel with wheat; the wine stands on the side where the icon of the Most Holy Theotokos is, and the oil on the side where the icon of Jesus Christ is. And there he awaits the end of the singing. When the singing is concluded, he loudly proclaims the litany: “Save, O God...” etc.,[^77] with the Exclamation: “Hear us, O God.” After this litany follows: “Peace be to all,” “Bow your heads,” etc., and the prayer which the Priest loudly recites: “O most merciful Master...” etc. And immediately the choir sings the aposticha stichera (3) with “Glory,” and “Now and,” after which “Now lettest Thou.” Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the Exclamation. Upon the conclusion of this, first the Priest himself sings the proper troparion, and the choir thereafter repeats it, and that in such wise: if it be a Feast of the Lord or of the Mother of God, then the troparion of the feast is sung thrice; if, however, it be the feast of a Saint, then his troparion is sung twice, and “O Theotokos Virgin, rejoice” once. During the singing of the troparia the Priest cruciformly censes the tetrapod whereupon the aforesaid offerings are prepared for blessing. Upon the conclusion of the singing the Priest loudly proclaims: “Let us pray unto the Lord,” and reads the prayer: “O Lord Jesus Christ our God, Who didst bless the five loaves in the wilderness and didst satisfy five thousand men: do Thou Thyself now, O Master, bless these loaves, wheat, wine, and oil,” with a single blessing[^78] he blesses the loaves, wheat, wine, and oil. Upon the conclusion of this prayer, when the choir hath sung “Amen,” the Priest censes the set-forth gifts and sings: “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (*Буди имя Господне*) once; and the choir repeats the same twice. After this the Priest departs before the royal doors, and the choir reads the psalm: “I will bless the Lord at all times” (*Благословлю Господа*) even unto “Shall not want any good thing” [Ps. 33:1–11], and adds: “In the name of the Lord, bless, Father!” whereunto the Priest answers: “Glory to the holy...” etc., and Matins follows.

This Vespers among us is celebrated on great feasts in the morning, if Little Vespers hath previously taken place.

##### 2) Extraordinary Vespers

§. 29.

Order of Vespers for the Departed

This Vespers is daily without entrance. It is celebrated thus: The Priest in a black epitrachelion before the royal doors

=== LEAF p90 ===
[Book Page 66]

having bowed, makes the usual beginning (“Blessed is our God,” etc.). After “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) the choir reads the Introductory Psalm, while the Priest recites the evening prayers. After “Alleluia” (thrice), “Glory to Thee, O God,” the Priest chants the litany for the departed: “In peace let us pray unto the Lord”[^79] with the Exclamation: “For Thou art the resurrection...” etc. And immediately the kathisma, after which “Lord, I have cried” (*Господи воззвахъ*) is sung. Stichera at “Lord, I have cried” (*Господи воззвахъ*) on 6, namely: 3 martyria from the Oktoechos, and 3 for the departed. “Glory,” for the departed; “Now and,” Theotokion from the Oktoechos according to the tone. There is no entrance, but the Priest from his place proclaims: “Wisdom! Stand upright!” (*Премꙋ́дрость про́сти!*) and the choir sings: “Gladsome Light.” After this the Priest loudly proclaims: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” and the choir instead of a prokimenon sings: “Alleluia” (4 times) in Tone 8 with verses for the departed,[^80] and immediately reads: “Vouchsafe, O Lord.” During this time the Priest comes unto the royal doors, and here loudly proclaims the litany: “Let us complete our evening prayer...” etc., as at other times without any change. After this the aposticha stichera are sung, for the departed (3) with “Glory,” and “Now and,” and immediately “Now lettest Thou.” Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the Exclamation: “For Thine is the kingdom...” etc. After the Exclamation the troparia for the departed are sung (“With depth of wisdom,” “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Thee we have as a rampart” (*Тебѐ и҆ стѣ́нꙋ...*)). During the singing the Priest comes forth, and upon the conclusion of the singing loudly proclaims the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,”[^81] (as at a Panakhyda) with the Exclamation: “For Thou art the resurrection...” etc. Having finished the litany, he makes the Small Dismissal. Namely: “Wisdom”; the choir: “More honorable,” etc. At the end, however: “May He Who hath dominion over the quick and the dead...” etc. Blessing, and “Blessed repose and eternal memory.”

This Vespers is celebrated on Friday evening on the so-called Memorial Saturdays.[^82]

§. 30.

Order of Vespers in the Holy Great Fast

(α) On Sunday[^83] evening such a Vespers is celebrated which up to the prokimenon: “Turn not away Thy face” (*Не отврати лица*)[^84] or “Thou hast given an inheritance,”

---

=== LEAF p91 ===
[Book Page 67]

is great with an entrance; from here, however, that is, from “Vouchsafe,” it is little without the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” instead of which there is “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times) with prostrations and with the least dismissal. The order of this Vespers is the following: The usual beginning: “Heavenly King,” the Trisagion, “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the Exclamation, “Come, let us worship,” Psalm: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” Litany: “In peace let us pray unto the Lord.” There is no kathisma on Sunday evening, unless on the following Monday there be a great feast. After this, the stichera at “Lord, I have cried” (*Господи воззвахъ*) on 10, namely: 4 penitential from the Oktoechos,[^85] 3 from the Lenten Triodion,[^86] and 3 from the Menaion unto the Saint. “Glory,” unto the Saint; “Now and,” Theotokion from the Menaion. During the Theotokion the Priest vests in the phelonion and makes the entrance with the censer. Great Prokimenon: “Turn not away,” or: “Thou hast given an inheritance” (*И далъ еси достояніе*) (4 times). After the prokimenon the Priest shuts the royal doors, divests of the phelonion, and only in the epitrachelion, having taken his stand before the royal doors, upon the completion of “Vouchsafe, O Lord,” chants the litany: “Let us complete our evening prayer.” And immediately in due order follow the aposticha stichera: 3 from the Lenten Triodion. “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion likewise from the Triodion.[^87] “Now lettest Thou.” Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the Exclamation. After the Exclamation the cantors and the rest of the people go forth into the midst of the church, and the troparion “O Theotokos Virgin” (*Богоро́дице Дѣ́во...*) is sung with a prostration; at “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): “O Baptist of Christ” (*Крестителю Христовъ*) with a prostration; at “Now and”: “Intercede for us” (*Молите о насъ*) with a prostration. After this the Priest reads: “Beneath Thy tender mercy” without a prostration. After this the choir: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times), “O Lord, bless.” The Priest: “Blessed and glorified is Christ our God.” The choir: “Amen.” The Priest reads: “Heavenly King,” etc.; and the choir: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” “In the name of the Lord, bless, Father.” The Priest: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and makes 3 prostrations with the prayer of St. Ephrem: “O Lord and Master of my life,”[^88] etc., and immediately the least dismissal from “Glory to Thee.”[^89]

(β) If in the Great Fast there falls on a fasting day (that is, from Monday unto Friday) a certain feast, either decretal, or church, or polyeleos, and if there be no Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts, then Vespers from the beginning even unto the prokimenon is

=== LEAF p92 ===
[Book Page 68]

great with an entrance, but from here, that is, from “Vouchsafe” (*Сподо́би*) even unto the very end, it is little, but with the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and with the small dismissal. — The order of this Vespers is the following: The usual beginning (“Heavenly King,” the Trisagion, “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the Exclamation, “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) 12 times), “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice), Psalm: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” Litany of peace. Kathisma. Stichera at “Lord, I have cried” (*Господи воззвахъ*): 3 of the Fast, and 5 unto the Saint; “Glory,” unto the Saint (if there be one); if not: “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion from the Menaion. Entrance. The prokimenon of the Fast is taken just as at other times. Readings: 2 of the Fast and 3 of the feast. After this the Priest puts off the phelonion and in the epitrachelion comes forth before the royal doors, during which time the cantor or reader reads “Vouchsafe,” and here he loudly proclaims the litany: “Let us complete our evening prayers,” etc. Aposticha stichera of the feast: 3; “Glory,” unto the Saint; “Now and,” Theotokion as usual. “Now lettest Thou.” Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the Exclamation. Troparia of the feast (“O Theotokos Virgin,” “O Baptist of Christ,” and “Intercede” are omitted). Upon the conclusion of the festal troparia the Priest comes forth before the royal doors and chants the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God.” After this, 3 prostrations, but without the prayer of St. Ephrem: “O Lord and Master.” Festal dismissal, and that the small one, from “Wisdom.”

(γ) Daily Vespers on fasting days of the Holy Fast (except Wednesday and Friday) is joined with the Ninth Hour and with the Typika. The Ninth Hour is celebrated thus: The Priest, having come forth from the sanctuary in the epitrachelion before the royal doors, saith: “Blessed is our God,” whereunto the choir answers: “Amen,” and immediately: “Heavenly King,” the Trisagion, “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the Exclamation, “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). After this the Priest enters into the sanctuary, and the cantor reads the psalms, which are also found in the Molitvoslov.[^90] “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Alleluia” (thrice), “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” the Kathisma of the day.[^91] After the kathisma, the troparion: “He Who at the ninth hour” with the verses, and “Deliver us not up” (*Не предаждь насъ*) is read unto the end. After this, the Trisagion; “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the Exclamation. Troparia: “Seeing the thief,” “In the midst of two thieves,” “The Lamb and Shepherd and Savior of the world,” which are only read. Upon the conclusion of these, “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times); and the Priest comes forth from the sanctuary before the royal doors, and reads the prayer: “Thou Who at every time.” After this: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable.” And the Priest proclaims: “God be bountiful unto us,”

=== LEAF p93 ===
[Book Page 69]

and makes 3 prostrations. After the prostrations the Typika begins in such wise that the Priest enters into the holy sanctuary, and the choir sings: “In Thy kingdom.” Upon the conclusion of this it sings: “Remember us, O Lord,” etc., “Remember us, O Master,” etc., “Remember us, O Holy One,” etc., and the people make prostrations at each one. After this the choir reads: “The heavenly choir,” etc., “The choir of Saints,” etc., the Creed, “Remit, forgive,” “Our Father.” After this, the kontakion of the temple, of the day, and of the Saint. “Glory,” “With the Saints”; “Now and,” “Steadfast Protectress.” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). “Glory,” and “Now and”: “More honorable.” After this the choir: “In the name of the Lord, bless, Father,” whereunto the Priest proclaims: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers, O Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us,” and makes 3 prostrations. After the prostrations: the Trisagion, “Our Father,” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times) with the prayer: “O Master Christ God,” etc. Daily dismissal, in which also the Saint whose memory is observed is commemorated. After this begins Vespers, which is daily without entrance and without the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” but with 2 paremias. — It is celebrated thus: The Priest after the Typika proclaims: “Blessed is our God,” and the choir: “Amen,” and immediately: “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). After this, the psalm: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” Litany of peace. Kathisma. Stichera at “Lord, I have cried” (*Господи воззвахъ*) on 6 (3 from the Triodion and 3 unto the Saint). There is no entrance. There are two prokimena before the two paremias, but only before the 1st prokimenon doth the Priest proclaim: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” upon the conclusion of which “Vouchsafe, O Lord,” and the litany: “Let us complete our evening prayer,” etc. Aposticha stichera of the Fast, and immediately: “Now lettest Thou.” Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the Exclamation. After this the Priest comes forth before the royal doors, and the choir into the midst of the church, and they sing the troparia with prostrations: “O Theotokos Virgin,” “O Baptist of Christ,” “Intercede for us, holy Apostles.” Upon the conclusion of these troparia the Priest proclaims: “Beneath Thy tender mercy.” After this: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). — And the Priest proclaims: “Blessed is He Who is,” etc., and reads the prayer: “Heavenly King.” Further: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” and the cantor concludes: “In the name of the Lord,” etc., whereunto the Priest saith: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and makes 3 prostrations. After the prostrations the choir on ordinary days begins anew: “Trisagion. Our Father with the Exclamation. Lord, have mercy (12 times)”; and the Priest reads the prayer: “All-Holy Trinity” (*Всесвятая Троице*) etc. (from the Typika). Upon the conclusion of this prayer the choir sings: “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (*Буди имя Господне*) (thrice) with prostrations, and after this reads the psalm: “I will bless the Lord” (*Благословлю Господа*) [Ps. 33].[^92] And

=== LEAF p94 ===
[Book Page 70]

immediately the choir saith: “It is truly meet... unto... our God”; and the Priest loudly proclaims: “Wisdom,” etc. After this the Priest: “Glory to Thee, O Christ God,” etc.; the choir: “Glory to the Father,” etc., and the daily dismissal with blessing.

(δ) On Wednesday and Friday of the Great Fast, Vespers is celebrated not only with the Ninth Hour and with the Typika, but also with the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts.[^93] The Ninth Hour and the Typika precede Vespers, while the Liturgy follows Vespers. The Ninth Hour with the Typika is celebrated as was said above; only this is needful to note here, that when at the Typika the choir begins to sing “In Thy kingdom,” the Priest then enters into the holy sanctuary and here vests in all the priestly vestments, saying over each vestment: “Let us pray unto the Lord.” After this he takes his stand within the sanctuary before the Holy Table below, and here concludes the Typika with the dismissal as was said above. — Upon the conclusion of the Typika the Priest approaches the Holy Table, begins to pray: “Blessed is our God,” and having kissed the holy table and the cross, loudly proclaims: “Blessed is the kingdom,” whereunto the choir answers: “Amen,” and “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice), etc. And immediately the litany of peace. After the Exclamation the 18th kathisma begins to be read in the midst of the church. Before the 1st Antiphon the cantor proclaims: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory to the Father...”; and the reader saith: “Both now and,” and reads the 1st Antiphon. After the reading of the 1st Antiphon the Priest proclaims: “Again and again in peace let us pray unto the Lord,” and the cantor sings: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (twice), “To Thee, O Lord,” “Amen”; and the reader makes 3 prostrations in the midst of the church. Upon the conclusion of the 2nd Antiphon the Priest again proclaims: “Again and again,” etc., and the cantor again sings: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (twice), “To Thee, O Lord. Amen”; and the reader again makes 3 prostrations. Upon the conclusion of the 3rd Antiphon the Priest saith nothing, but the cantor himself of his own accord sings: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (twice), “To Thee, O Lord. Amen”; and the reader makes 3 prostrations and departs. And immediately the cantor begins to sing: “Lord, I have cried” (*Господи воззвахъ*) with the stichera, and the Priest censes the church. Stichera on 10: 3 penitential from the Oktoechos, 3 from the Triodion, and 4 unto the Saint (repeating the first sticheron);[^94] “Glory,” of the Saint, if there be one; if not: “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion from the Menaion. On Fridays: “Glory,” of the Saint; “Now and,” dogmaticon of the tone from the Oktoechos (for it is the apodosis of the tone). After the censing of the church, the Priest comes

=== LEAF p95 ===
[Book Page 71]

unto the holy table, takes the chalice out of the tabernacle, takes the Holy Lamb, places It upon the diskos, covers It with the star and the veil, and having censed It, carries It unto the prothesis with candle-bearers preceding. Here into the chalice he pours wine and water, saying: “Let us pray unto the Lord.” And after this, having prepared everything, he returns unto the Holy Table, and here at “Glory,” and “Now and” makes the entrance with the censer (and at times also with the Gospel), and before the royal doors proclaims: “Wisdom! Stand upright!” (*Премꙋ́дрость про́сти!*) After “Gladsome Light” (*Свѣте тихій*) and after “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” the 1st prokimenon is sung and there takes place the reading from the Book of Genesis. Upon the conclusion of this reading the choir at once sings the 2nd prokimenon, during which the Priest comes unto the holy table, and when the cantor proclaims: “Command!” the Priest with the three-branched candle and with the censer, looking toward the East, saith: “Wisdom! Stand upright!” (*Премꙋ́дрость про́сти!*) and having turned unto the people saith: “The light of Christ enlightens all.” Unto this the reader immediately begins the 2nd reading, saying: “The reading from the Proverbs,” whereunto the Priest proclaims: “Let us attend!” (*Во́ньмемъ*) and the reader begins to read the 2nd paremia. After the paremias there is sung: “Let my prayer arise,” during which singing, when the verse “Let my prayer arise” (*Да и҆спра́витсѧ*) is sung briskly, the Priest stands; when, however, the verses “Let my prayer arise,” “Lord, I have cried,” “Set a watch, O Lord,” and “Incline not my heart” are sung slowly, the Priest falls upon his knees. Upon the conclusion of this singing, 3 prostrations. (After this at times there follows also a prokimenon, Epistle, and Gospel, that is to say, if a certain polyeleos Saint is celebrated.) After the Gospel, if there be one, but if not, then after the singing of “Let my prayer arise,” the litany “Let us all say” (*Рцѣмъ вси*) is sung, and thus the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts is celebrated as the Liturgikon shows, concerning which word will be made in Section II. (From this Vespers likewise only one half is taken, and that up to the paremias.)

§. 31.

Order of Great Vespers with the Liturgy of St. Basil the Great

The Priest, having prepared himself as at other times for the celebration of the Liturgy, having come into the church, before the royal doors concludes the prayers that are to be celebrated before the Divine Liturgy. Then, having entered into the sanctuary, he vests in all the priestly vestments, saying the prescribed prayers, washes his hands, and immediately goes unto the holy table. Here, having bowed, he begins secretly: “Blessed is our God,” then “Heavenly King,” “Glory to

=== LEAF p96 ===
[Book Page 72]

God in the highest,” etc. (twice), and “O Lord, open Thou my lips,” etc. (once). After this, having kissed the holy table and the cross, he proclaims: “Blessed is the kingdom.” The choir answers: “Amen,” and reads: “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice), Psalm: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” The Priest concludes the evening prayers. After “Alleluia” (thrice), “Glory to Thee, O God,” the Priest loudly proclaims the litany: “In peace let us pray unto the Lord.” After this litany the choir sings: “Lord, I have cried,” during which singing the Priest departs unto the table of preparation and concludes the proskomedia. Stichera on 8 or on 10. After “Glory,” and “Now and,” entrance with the censer and the Gospel. “Gladsome Light.” Prokimenon of the day and 3 paremias, but at times even more.[^95] During the last reading the Priest ascends by the northern side unto the holy table and reads the prayer of the Trisagion already from the Liturgy (“O Holy God, Who restest in the holy,” etc.). Upon the conclusion of the readings and the little litany, the Priest proclaims: “For holy art Thou,” etc., and thus follows the Trisagion, or: “All of you that have been baptized into Christ.” And after this the Divine Liturgy of Basil is concluded according to the order that is prescribed in the Liturgikon, — concerning which word will be made in Section II.

Vespers with the Liturgy of St. Basil the Great is celebrated on the Paramony of the Nativity of Christ, and on the Paramony of Theophany, if it happen not on a Saturday or Sunday;[^96] likewise always on Holy and Great Thursday, and Great Saturday; furthermore, on the feast of the Annunciation with the Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom, if this feast falls in the Great Fast, Saturdays and Sundays excepted.

§. 32.

Order of Vespers of Holy and Great Friday with Procession

This Vespers is great with an entrance, with the Gospel, and with the great dismissal. It is renowned also for this reason, that it hath a procession around the church with the Shroud (*Plashchanytsia*). The Priest begins it as usual in a black epitrachelion and concludes it in due order. The usual beginning. No kathisma. Stichera on 6. Before the entrance the Priest vests not only in the epitrachelion and phelonion, but also in the sticharion, and thus makes the entrance with the censer and with the Gospel. After “Gladsome Light” (*Свѣте тихій*) the Priest proclaims: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” and the prokimenon is sung. After the prokimenon, 1 paremia, and again one

=== LEAF p97 ===
[Book Page 73]

prokimenon, and thus 2 paremias. After the paremias immediately: “Let us attend! Peace be to all!” and the prokimenon is sung, and the Epistle is read: to the Corinthians, pericope 125 [1 Cor. 1:18–2:2]. After the Epistle the Priest proclaims: “Peace be to thee,” whereunto the choir sings: “Alleluia” (thrice), and the Priest reads the Gospel from Matthew, pericope 110 [Matt. 27:1–38; Luke 23:39–43; Matt. 27:39–54; John 19:31–37; Matt. 27:55–61]. After this, the litany: “Let us all say.” “Vouchsafe, O Lord” (*Сподоби Господи*) is read, and the litany is sung: “Let us complete our evening prayer,” etc. Aposticha stichera. When the choir saith: “Now lettest Thou,” the Priest spreads out the Shroud (*Plashchanytsia*) upon the holy table and censes It. Thereafter, when the choir, upon the conclusion of “Now lettest Thou,” the Trisagion, and “Our Father,” begins to sing: “The noble Joseph,” the Priest places the Shroud upon his shoulders, and holding It with both hands upon his head, while other two men hold the other end of the Shroud, that is to say, from the feet, and thus the Priest, with the deacon censing and candle-bearers preceding, makes the procession around the church, and that only once. At the procession Gospels also are read from all four Evangelists, namely: 1. Gospel from Matthew, pericope 113 (“Now when the even was come... over against the sepulchre” [Matt. 27:57–61]). 2. Gospel from Mark, pericope 66 (“Joseph came... where He was laid” [Mark 15:42–47]). 3. Gospel from Luke, pericope 111 (“And a man... rested according to the commandment” [Luke 23:50–56]). 4. Gospel from John, pericope 62 (“At that time... laid they Jesus” [John 19:38–42]). Having returned into the church, the Priest lays the Shroud into the tomb, or upon another prepared place, and censes It. And the cantor sings at “Glory,” and “Now and”: “Unto the Myrrh-bearing Women.” After this, having put off the phelonion, he goes unto the pulpit and delivers the holy sermon. And after the sermon with genuflection he saith the prayer prescribed in the Trebnik. After the prayer, the great dismissal, namely: “Wisdom”; the choir: “Bless.” The Priest: “Blessed and glorified is...” After the blessing there takes place the kissing of the Shroud with prostrations.

§. 33.

Order of Great Vespers with the Liturgy of St. Basil and with the Blessing of Loaves on Holy and Great Saturday

It begins at the 9th hour. The Priest, having come into the church, as usual as before the Divine Liturgy concludes the prayers before the icons of the Savior and the Mother of God. After this, having entered into the holy sanctuary, he vests in all the priestly vestments, and those black, saying the prescribed prayers, and having washed his hands goes unto the holy table and begins quietly: “Blessed is our God. Heavenly King. Glory

=== LEAF p98 ===
[Book Page 74]

to God in the highest” (twice), and “O Lord, open Thou my lips,” etc. (once). After this he proclaims: “Blessed is the kingdom,” the choir: “Amen.” And immediately the cantor: “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice), Psalm: “Bless the Lord, O my soul,” “Alleluia” (thrice); during the singing the Priest reads the evening prayers, and after “Alleluia (thrice), Glory to Thee, O God,” the litany of peace. After the Exclamation the choir sings: “Lord, I have cried” (*Господи воззвахъ*) with the stichera on 8. And the Priest departs to celebrate the proskomedia. When “Glory,” and “Now and” approaches, the proskomedia must be concluded; during “Glory,” and “Now and” the entrance takes place with the censer and the Gospel. The Priest: “Wisdom! Stand upright!” (*Премꙋ́дрость про́сти!*) the choir: “Gladsome Light,” etc.; the prokimenon is not said, but 6 readings follow. And immediately the Priest at each one proclaims: “Wisdom!” (*Премꙋ́дрость!*) the choir: “The reading from Genesis”; thereafter “The reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah”; further “The reading from Exodus,” “The reading from the Prophecy of Jonah,” “The reading from Joshua,” “The reading from Exodus.” This 6th reading the reader concludes with the words: “Then sang Moses and the children of Israel this song unto the Lord, and spoke, saying,” whereunto the reader in Tone 5 begins to lead the chanting of the following verse: “Let us sing unto the Lord, for gloriously hath He been glorified.” The reader: “Glory,” and “Now and.” The choir: “Let us sing unto the Lord.” And again the reader concludes: “Let us sing unto the Lord,” etc. After this follow 9 readings. And that: the Priest: “Wisdom!” (*Премꙋ́дрость!*) the reader: “The reading from the Prophecy of Zephaniah,” “The reading from the Third Book of Kings,” “The reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah,” “The reading from Genesis,” “The reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah,” “The reading from the Sixth Book of Kings,” “The reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah,” “The reading from the Prophecy of Jeremiah,” “The reading from the Prophecy of Daniel.” And immediately the reader subjoins the prayer or Song of the Three Holy Children: “Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers, and praised...” etc. At the very end of this the reader sings the verse: “Praise the Lord and exalt Him above all forever,” and the choir sings it twice. And immediately the reader chants the verses: “Bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord, we praise,” and the choir after each verse answers: “Praise the Lord and exalt Him above all forever.” At the end the reader: “Glory... and now and ever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen”; the choir: “Praise the Lord and exalt Him,” etc. The reader: “Praise the Lord and exalt Him,” etc. After this follows the little litany with the Exclamation. During this singing the Priest from the high place enters unto the Holy Table and here quietly saith the prayer prescribed in the Liturgikon before the Trisagion, and proclaims: “For holy art Thou,” etc. Instead of the Trisagion: “All of you that have been baptized into Christ.” Afterwards the Priest: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” and the choir the prokimenon: “Let all the earth worship Thee,” etc. The Epistle (to the Romans, pericope 91 [Rom. 6:3–11]). After this the Priest: “Peace be to thee! Wisdom, let us attend!” whereunto the choir

=== LEAF p99 ===
[Book Page 75]

instead of the Alleluia[^97] saith: “Arise, O God, judge the earth, for Thou shalt inherit among all the nations” [Ps. 81:8] multiple times in Tone 7. During that time the Priest changes the black vestments into bright ones, and there follows the Gospel from Matthew, pericope 115 [Matt. 28:1–20]. After this everything follows in the order of the Divine Liturgy of St. Basil the Great. Instead of the Cherubic Hymn there is sung: “Let all mortal flesh keep silence,” and at the “Especially”: “Weep not for Me, O Mother” (*Не рыдай Мене мати*); the Communion Hymn: “The Lord awoke as one out of sleep, and is risen, saving us. Alleluia.” After the Divine Liturgy there is performed the blessing of loaves, wheat, wine, and oil, as at a Litiya. That is to say, after the Ambo Prayer the Priest comes into the midst of the church, and having taken his stand there before the tetrapod proclaims: “Let us pray unto the Lord,” and loudly recites the prayer: “O Lord Jesus Christ our God, Who didst bless the five loaves,” etc.; after the prayer he censes, and upon the conclusion of the singing of “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (*Буди имя Господне*) makes the dismissal.

§. 34.

Order of Vespers in Bright Week

(α) Order of Vespers on Easter Sunday with the Gospel. — For this Vespers the Priest vests in all the priestly vestments, and since throughout all Bright Week the royal doors are open, he censes, and comes unto the Holy Table, where with the censer he signs cruciformly and proclaims, saying: “Blessed is our God,” etc. After this the Priest loudly sings: “Christ is risen,” etc., once, and the choir twice. And immediately the Priest sings the following verses: “Let God arise,” etc., “As smoke vanishes,” and the rest, and the choir after each verse sings: “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*); at the end the Priest sings “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) once, and the choir twice. Then the Priest saith the litany: “In peace let us pray unto the Lord.” And immediately the stichera at “Lord, I have cried” (*Господи воззвахъ*) on 6. At “Glory,” and “Now and” the entrance takes place with the Gospel. At “Wisdom! Stand upright!” (*Премꙋ́дрость про́сти!*): “Gladsome Light.” “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” and immediately the Great Prokimenon. After the prokimenon: “That we may be accounted worthy,” etc., “Peace be to all,” etc., “Wisdom! Stand upright! Let us hear the Holy Gospel,” etc. (from John, pericope 65: “The same day at evening,” etc. [John 20:19–25]). The Gospel is read from the royal doors, and after it follows the holy sermon. After this the litany: “Let us all say,” etc., “Vouchsafe, O Lord,” etc., the litany: “Let us complete our evening prayer,” etc. After this litany the choir sings this aposticha sticheron: “Thy Resurrection,

=== LEAF p100 ===
[Book Page 76]

O Christ Savior,” etc.; and the Priest immediately, having taken the Gospel and the Cross, comes forth onto the ambo and sings: “Let God arise” (*Да воскреснетъ Богъ*) and the other verses. Unto this the choir after each verse sings the Paschal stichera: “A sacred Pascha,” etc., and concludes with “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) thrice. During the singing the people kiss the Cross, the Gospel, and the icon of the risen Christ. Likewise at that time the myrovania takes place and the blessed bread, or so-called antidoron, is distributed. After that the great dismissal takes place, that is to say: “Wisdom,” “Bless,” etc., “Christ is risen from the dead, trampling down death by death, and unto those in the tombs bestowing life, our true God,” etc., as in the Molitvoslov, p. 478. After the dismissal the Priest, having turned unto the people with the Cross, saith thrice: “Christ is risen!” and the people answer: “Truly He is risen!” Blessing. And thus he celebrates Vespers throughout the entire Bright Week. In Bright Week every blessing takes place with the Cross.

(β) Order of Vespers in Bright Week, if on a certain day a polyeleos Saint should occur. — The Priest, having put on all the vestments and censed the church, before the Holy Table begins from: “Blessed is our God.” After this: “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) thrice with the verses. At the end the Priest sings: “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) once, and the choir twice. And immediately the litany of peace. (There is no kathisma in Bright Week.) At “Lord, I have cried,” 6 stichera, namely 3 of the Resurrection, and 3 unto the Saint. “Glory,” of the Saint; “Now and,” Theotokion-dogmaticon, that is, the first of the tone. Entrance with the censer. Prokimenon of the day. Readings of the Saint. At the aposticha, one sticheron of the Resurrection, and the rest the stichera of Pascha. (Veneration of the Cross.) “Glory,” of the Saint; “Now and,” “The Day of Resurrection,” etc., “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) thrice. And immediately “Glory,” Troparion unto the Saint; “Now and,” Theotokion according to the tone of the Saint. Paschal dismissal with the Cross. Blessing with the Cross throughout the entire week.

Note. Concerning Vespers from Thomas Sunday until Ascension, it is necessary to note the following regarding these: (α) Sunday Vespers, sung on Saturday evening, is celebrated thus: After “Blessed is our God” (*Благословенъ Богъ нашъ*) the Priest sings “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) once, and the cantor twice. (The verses are not sung.) And immediately the Trisagion.[^98] “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the Exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times). “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). The Introductory Psalm: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” Litany of peace. Kathisma. At “Lord, I have cried,” 10 stichera.[^99] Great entrance.

---

=== LEAF p101 ===
[Book Page 77]

2 litanies. At the aposticha, one sticheron of the Resurrection in order from the Flowery Triodion, and immediately the stichera of Pascha are sung (except on Thomas Sunday): “Let God arise,” etc., “A sacred Pascha,” etc. “Glory,” of the Saint, if there be one; “Now and,” “The Day of Resurrection,” and once: “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*); and thus “Now Thou lettest depart.” (When the stichera of Pascha are sung, the Priest opens the royal doors, comes forth onto the ambo, and gives the Cross unto the people to kiss.) After “Now Thou lettest depart” the troparia are sung and immediately the dismissal of Pascha with the Cross. — (β) Concerning daily Vespers, it is necessary to note that the beginning of this Vespers is the same as that of Sunday Vespers. It is celebrated in order. After the litany, the kathisma of the day; the stichera at “Lord, I have cried” (*Господи воззвахъ*) on 6, and that also unto the Saint. There is no entrance. The stichera at the aposticha are of the Resurrection from the Flowery Triodion (and not of Pascha). “Glory,” unto the Saint; “Now and,” Theotokion. After “Now Thou lettest depart,” all in order. (The stichera of Pascha at daily Vespers are taken only on Tuesday evening at the Apodosis of the Feast of Pascha.) The dismissal is without the Cross, that is to say: “Wisdom”; “He Who rose from the dead,” etc.

§. 35.

Order of Vespers on the Descent of the Holy Spirit

This Vespers on Sunday of the Descent of the Holy Spirit is celebrated immediately after the Divine Liturgy, and that together with the kneeling prayers for the invocation of the Holy Spirit. The order of this Vespers is as follows: After “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (*Буди имя Господне*) the Priest, having entered into the holy altar, begins: “Blessed is our God,” etc., and saith “Heavenly King,” the Trisagion, and the rest of the ordinary beginning, and the Psalm: “Bless the Lord, O my soul,” etc. After “Alleluia” (thrice), “Glory to Thee, O God,” the Priest chants the litany of peace, unto which after the petition: “For the people here present” he appends also the special litany “For the invocation of the help of the Holy Spirit” found in the *Sluzhebnik*, which he recites with kneeling. After the exclamation: “For unto Thee is due,” the choir sings: “Lord, I have cried” (*Господи воззвахъ*) and the stichera. (There is no kathisma.) “Glory,” and “Now and.” Entrance with the censer. Great Prokimenon: “Who is a God as great as our God?” etc. After the prokimenon the Priest ascends onto the ambo and proclaims: “Again and again, having bowed our knees, let us pray unto the Lord,” and having bowed his knees, recites the 1st prayer: “O undefiled, unblemished,” etc. After this, having arisen, he saith the small litany: “Help us, save us,” etc., and then the litany: “Let us all say.” — After the litany he proclaims: “Again and again, having bowed our knees,” etc., and recites the 2nd prayer:

=== LEAF p102 ===
[Book Page 78]

“O Lord Jesus Christ,” etc. After this prayer he chants again the small litany: “Help us, save us,” etc., and immediately the choir reads: “Vouchsafe, O Lord.” After this the Priest again saith: “Again and again, having bowed our knees,” etc., and recites the 3rd prayer: “O ever-flowing, life-creating,” etc. After this: “Help us, save us,” etc., and the litany: “Let us complete our evening prayer,” etc. Stichera at the aposticha. “Now Thou lettest depart.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. Troparion of the Holy Trinity, and “Glory,” and “Now and,” of the Feast. Proper festal dismissal.

§. 36.

Order of Vespers on the Paramony of the Nativity of Jesus Christ, if it should occur on a Saturday or on a Sunday

On the Paramony of the Nativity of Jesus Christ, the Royal Hours conclude in order in the morning together with Vespers and the Divine Liturgy of St. Basil the Great. But if the Paramony should fall on a Saturday or on a Sunday, the celebration is divided, and that thus: the Royal Hours are celebrated beforehand on Friday; Vespers is celebrated on the Paramony itself (that is, on Saturday or on Sunday) in the evening at the 3rd hour with the evening paremias, with the troparia, and with the Epistle and Gospel of the Paramony; while the Divine Liturgy of St. Basil is celebrated on the very day of the Nativity of Christ. — Concerning this Great Vespers we note that it hath the ordinary beginning. Stichera at “Lord, I have cried” (*Господи воззвахъ*) of the Nativity on 8. Entrance with the censer and with the Gospel. After “Gladsome Light” (*Свѣте тихій*) the Priest proclaims: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” and the prokimenon of the day is sung, after which there are 8 readings with troparia. These troparia are inserted at the 3rd and after the 6th paremia. At the conclusion of the 8th reading, the small litany, and immediately: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” and the prokimenon of the Epistle is taken: “The Lord said unto Me” [Ps. 2:7]. — The Epistle is read, pericope 303 [Gal. 3:15–22]. Alleluia and the Gospel from Matthew, pericope 53. Then “Let us all say,” “Vouchsafe, O Lord,” and “Let us complete our evening prayer,” and immediately the great dismissal. — (There are no stichera at the aposticha.)
Note. In like manner the celebration of the Paramony of Theophany is divided, if it should occur on a Saturday or Sunday. There, in addition, it is also necessary to note that this Vespers in this case is not only similar to this one, but in such a case it is also combined with the blessing of water. — That is to say,

=== LEAF p103 ===
[Book Page 79]

after the readings, after the Epistle and Gospel and after both litanies, the Priest comes forth into the midst of the church, where water is prepared, and the choir begins to sing: “The voice of the Lord is upon the waters,” and the blessing of water is celebrated, concerning which mention will be made in Article III.

ARTICLE II.

§. 37.

On Complines

Ordinary Complines are divided: (α) into small, and (β) into great. And of the extraordinary complines the most notable are: (α) Small Compline without a canon; (β) Complines in the Great Fast; (γ) Compline with Litiya.
Small Compline among us is celebrated publicly in church only on Holy Wednesday, Friday, and Saturday, and on the Descent of the Holy Spirit; but privately at home throughout the entire year, even in the Great Fast on Saturday and on Sunday (that is, for Sunday and for Monday). Great Compline, however, is celebrated publicly: (α) on Tuesday and on Thursday (that is, for Wednesday and for Friday) of Cheesefare Week; (β) on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday (with the exception of Wednesday of the 5th week), Thursday, and Friday (with the exception of Friday of the 5th week) of all weeks of the Great Fast; (γ) on Monday and Tuesday of Passion Week; (δ) on the Paramony of the Nativity of Christ and of Theophany; (ε) on the Paramony of the Annunciation, if that feast should occur on Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, Friday, or Saturday of the first 6 weeks of the Great Fast, or if it should occur on Tuesday, Wednesday, or Thursday of Passion Week.

1) Ordinary Complines.

§. 38.

Order of Small Compline

If this Divine Service is sung in church, then the Priest in epitrachelion before the royal doors makes the ordinary beginning, that is to say, proclaims: “Blessed is our God,” etc., unto which the choir answers: “Amen.”

=== LEAF p104 ===
[Book Page 80]

And immediately: “Heavenly King.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times). “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). After this the Priest enters into the holy altar, and the choir reads the Psalms, namely the 50th (“Have mercy on me, O God” (*Поми́луй мя́, Бо́же*) [Ps. 50]), the 69th [Ps. 69], and the 142nd (“O Lord, hear my prayer” [Ps. 142]); and after the Psalms it reads: “Glory to God in the highest.” The Creed. After this there is sung the daily canon in honor of the Most Holy Theotokos from the Octoechos.[^100] (Katavasia is not sung after each ode.) After the 3rd ode no litany is sung, but there is said: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” and the Sessional Hymn. After the 6th ode likewise: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” Kontakion and Ikos. At the conclusion of the 9th ode both choirs together sing: “It is truly right.”[^101] After this: The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. On Saturday, the Hypakoe of the tone; if it be a feast or a commemorated Saint, his Kontakion; but if it be a simple day, the troparion of the temple, of the day, and the present one: “O God of our Fathers,” etc. (*Molitvoslov*, p. 271). “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). During this the Priest comes forth and before the royal doors recites the prayer: “Thou Who at all times,” etc. The choir: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” etc., “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaims: “God be merciful unto us,” etc. And after “Amen” he proclaims the prayer: “O undefiled, unblemished,” etc., and subjoins the prayer: “And grant unto us,” etc., with “O most glorious,” and “My hope,” etc. After this the troparia are sung: “Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us,” etc. “Glory,” “O Lord, have mercy on us,” etc. “Now and,” “Open unto us the doors of compassion,” etc. After these, the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc., “Again we pray that this city may be preserved...” and after the exclamation: “Hear us, O God our Savior...” The smallest dismissal from “Glory to Thee, O Christ God.” After this, the commemorations.[^102] If the morrow be a Sunday, a feast, or a commemorated Saint, then the Priest after “For the abundance of the fruits of the earth” doth not commemorate the departed, but only the living, and therefore immediately follows: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and thus the blessing takes place.

§. 39.

Order of Great Daily Compline

Great Compline is divided into 3 parts: (α) thanksgiving, (β) penitential, and (γ) doxological.

=== LEAF p105 ===
[Book Page 81]

Part I. The Priest in epitrachelion makes the ordinary beginning, saying: “Blessed is our God,” etc. The choir: “Amen.” And immediately: “Heavenly King.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). During this the Priest enters into the holy altar, and the cantor begins to read the Psalms: 4, 6, 12. “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). “Glory,” and “Now and.” Again the Psalms: 24, 30, 90. “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). After the Psalms the Priest comes forth and loudly chants the hymn: “God is with us, understand, all ye nations, and submit yourselves, for God is with us”; the choir chants the same twice. After this the Priest chants the verses, and the choir repeats after each verse: “God is with us.” At the conclusion of this singing the Priest enters, and the cantor reads: “The day being past,” etc. “The bodiless.” The Creed. “Most Holy.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation, and immediately the troparia are sung. After the troparia the choir: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times) (and the Priest comes forth). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “More honorable.” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaims: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and with the prayer: “O Lord, Lord,” etc., concludes the 1st part, having entered into the altar.
Part II of Great Compline begins with the invitation: “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice); the Psalms: 50, 101. After the Psalms the Priest comes forth and recites the prayer of King Manasses: “O Lord Almighty.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. Kontakion of the feast or of the day. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “More honorable.” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaims: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and with the prayer: “O Master, Lord Almighty,” etc., concludes the 2nd part, and goes into the holy altar.
Part III. This part begins with the invitation: “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice); the Psalms: the 69th (“O God, come to my assistance” [Ps. 69]) and the 142nd (“O Lord, hear my prayer” [Ps. 142]). After this: “Glory to God in the highest” (*Слава во вышнихъ Богу*) is only read. A canon, sometimes even two.[^103] After the canon there is sung the hymn: “It is truly right.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. After this follows the troparion: “O Lord of hosts,” etc., with Psalm 150: “Praise God in His saints” [Ps. 150:1], which is sung in such a manner that unto each verse of that Psalm there is appended as a refrain: “O Lord of hosts”; and at “Glory” (*Сла́ва*) there is sung: “O Lord, if we had not Thy saints,” etc., and at “Now and”: “A multitude of transgressions,” etc. After this: “O Most Holy Theotokos.” “All my hope.” After this follows “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). And the Priest comes forth and recites the prayer: “Thou Who at all times,” etc., and the choir:

=== LEAF p106 ===
[Book Page 82]

“Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” etc., “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And immediately the Priest proclaims: “God be merciful unto us,” etc. After “God be merciful unto us” (*Боже ущедри ны*) immediately the prayer: “O undefiled, unblemished,” etc., and “And grant unto us,” “O most glorious,” etc., “My hope,” and the rest, likewise as at Small Compline.

2) Extraordinary Complines.

§. 40.

Order of Small Compline without a Canon

The beginning of this Compline is ordinary, with the Psalms and with “I believe in one God,” etc. — After the Creed there is no canon, but immediately “It is truly right,” and immediately the Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. After this the troparion of the temple, of the day, and “O God of our Fathers.” After the troparia: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times), “Thou Who at all times.” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “More honorable,” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaims: “God be merciful unto us,” etc. After this: “O undefiled,” and “And grant unto us,” “O most glorious,” “Our hope.” Troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord,” etc. Litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc. Dismissal. Commemorations.
There is no canon at Compline: (α) on the days of Meatfare and Cheesefare Weeks, except Wednesday and Friday of Cheesefare Week; (β) on the day of the Annunciation, if it should occur in the Great Fast. Then in the morning Great Compline with Litiya is celebrated, and in the evening Small Compline without a canon;[^104] (γ) on the days of Bright Week; (δ) on the days from Monday of the Holy Spirit even unto the Sunday of All Saints.
Note. The Compline of Bright Week is given in the *Flowery Triodion*.

§. 41.

Order of Complines in the Holy Great Fast

(α) Order of Small Compline with Prostrations. — This Compline the Priest must read in his own house on Sundays of the Great Fast. It is the Compline of Monday.[^105] It is celebrated thus: Ordinary beginning: “Blessed is our God,” etc. Come,

=== LEAF p107 ===
[Book Page 83]

let us worship (thrice). The Psalms: 50, 69, and 142. After this: “Glory to God in the highest,” the Creed. The Theotokion canon. After the canon: “It is truly right.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. And immediately: the troparia of the temple, of the day, and the present one: “O God of our Fathers.”[^106] After the troparia the choir saith: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times); the Priest, having come forth, saith the prayer: “Thou Who at all times.” The choir: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” The Priest proclaims: “God be merciful unto us,” etc., and makes 3 great prostrations with the prayer of St. Ephrem (“O Lord and Master of my life” (*Господи и Владыко живота моего*)). After this, the Trisagion (thrice), “O Most Holy Trinity,” “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times). The prayer: “O undefiled,” etc., “And grant unto us,” “O most glorious,” “Our hope,” etc. The smallest dismissal from “Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee.” The commemorations, and thus the blessing. (The troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us,” etc., and the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” are omitted.)
(β) Order of Great Compline in the First Week of the Holy Great Fast. — Part I. The Priest in epitrachelion begins, saying: “Blessed is our God,” unto which the choir: “Amen.” And immediately: “Heavenly King.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). Then the Priest enters into the holy altar, and the cantor reads the 69th Psalm (“O God, come to my assistance” [Ps. 69]). After the Psalm, the Great Canon of St. Andrew of Crete.[^107] After the canon “It is truly right” (*Достойно есть*) is not sung, but instead of it the 9th ode is repeated, and one prostration even unto the ground is made. After this the Psalms: 4, 6, 12. “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). “Glory,” and “Now and,” and immediately the Psalms: 24, 30, 90. “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). And the Priest, having come forth, chants the hymn: “God is with us,” the choir twice. The Priest chants the verses, the cantor appends after each verse: “God is with us.” After this hymn the Priest enters into the holy altar, and the cantor reads: “The day being past,” etc., “The bodiless nature,” etc., “Most Holy Virgin.” The Creed. “O Most Holy Lady, pray to God for us sinners” — twice; while the 1st choir recites, the other makes a prostration, — and while the other choir arises and answers, the 1st choir makes a prostration. Likewise also at the other verses.[^108] After these petitions, the Trisagion, “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. After this on Monday and Wednesday the troparia: “Enlighten mine eyes,”

=== LEAF p108 ===
[Book Page 84]

and on Tuesday and Thursday: “Mine unseen enemies.”[^109] After these troparia: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” etc., “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest before the royal doors proclaims: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and recites the prayer: “O Lord, Lord, Who deliverest us,” etc., and herewith the 1st part of this Compline is concluded. — Part II begins with “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). After this the Psalms: 50, 101 (“O Lord, hear my prayer” [Ps. 101]). After these Psalms the Priest recites the prayer: “O Lord Almighty,” etc. After this: The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. And immediately we sing the troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord,” etc. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “More honorable.” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaims: “O Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us,” and recites the prayer: “O Master, God the Father,” etc., with which at once he also concludes the 2nd part. Part III begins with “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). After this, since Psalm 69, “O God, come to my assistance,” is omitted, immediately the 142nd Psalm is taken (“O Lord, hear my prayer” [Ps. 142]). After the Psalm: “Glory to God in the highest.” Then: The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. After this is sung loudly and slowly: “O Lord of hosts, be with us,” with the verses at the Praises. “Glory,” “O Lord, if we had not,” “Now and,” “A multitude of transgressions.” Then: “O Most Holy Theotokos.” “All my hope.” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). “Thou Who at all times.” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “More honorable.” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaims: “God be merciful unto us,” etc., and makes 3 prostrations with the prayer of St. Ephrem. After the prostrations, the Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times), and the prayers (kneeling): “O undefiled,” and “And grant unto us,” “O most glorious,” “Our hope.” After this the smallest dismissal from: “Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee.” The choir: “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Bless.” The Priest, however, instead of the dismissal saith the Litiya prayer: “O most merciful Master.” At the conclusion of this prayer immediately follow the commemorations and the blessing.
(γ) Order of Great Compline in the Remaining Weeks of the Great Fast. — This Compline is similar to that previously described, differing from it only in that the canon is recited at the end. It is celebrated thus: Part I. “Blessed is our God,” and the rest of the ordinary beginning. After “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice), the Psalms: 4, 6, 12. “Glory,” and “Now and.” The Psalms: 24, 30, and the 90th. “Glory,” and

=== LEAF p109 ===
[Book Page 85]

“Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). “Glory,” and “Now and.” The singing: “God is with us.” The petitions: “The day being past, I give thanks,” etc. “Glory,” “The day being past, I glorify,” etc. “Now and,” “The day being past, I hymn,” etc. “The bodiless.” “Most Holy Virgin.” The Creed. “O Most Holy Lady.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation, and immediately the troparia, on Monday and Wednesday: “Enlighten mine eyes,” on Tuesday and Thursday: “Mine unseen enemies.” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “More honorable.” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaims: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and recites the prayer: “O Lord, Lord,” with which he concludes the 1st part. Part II begins with “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). After this the Psalms: 50 and the 101st. The prayer of King Manasses: “O Lord Almighty,” etc. The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. Troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord,” etc. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “More honorable.” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaims: “O Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us,” etc., and with the prayer: “O Master, God the Father,” etc., concludes the 2nd part.
Part III begins from “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). After this the Psalms: 69 and the 142nd. “Glory to God in the highest.” The canon in honor of the Most Holy Theotokos. “It is truly right.” When the choir concludes: “It is truly right,” the Priest, having come forth before the royal doors, makes one prostration (except on Friday). After this: The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “O Lord of hosts,”[^110] with the verses at the Praises. “Glory,” “O Lord, if we had not.” “Now and,” “A multitude of transgressions,” and the rest. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (50 times). “Thou Who at all times.” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “More honorable.” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father,” and the Priest proclaims: “God be merciful unto us,” etc., and makes the prostrations. After the prostrations, the Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times), and the Priest, having bowed his knees, saith the prayers: “O undefiled,” “And grant unto us,” “O most glorious,” “Our hope,” and after this immediately saith the smallest dismissal from “Glory to Thee, O Christ God,” etc. The choir: “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Bless,” unto which the Priest saith instead of the dismissal the Litiya prayer: “O most merciful Master,” etc. After the prayer, the commemorations and the blessing.
Note. It must be noted that at the end of Compline the troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord,” etc., and the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and “Again we pray that this city may be preserved,” etc., in the Great Fast are taken neither at

=== LEAF p110 ===
[Book Page 86]

Small nor at Great Compline, except in Passion Week on Wednesday, Friday, and Saturday at Small Compline, as is our custom.
(δ) Order of Great Compline on the Fridays of the Great Fast.[^111] This Compline is without prostrations, for in the Greek Church there are no prostrations on Saturday. It is celebrated thus: Part I. Ordinary beginning with the Psalms, with “God is with us,” and with the other petitions even unto “Our Father.” After this the troparion is taken, namely: on Friday of the 1st week, the troparion of the Holy Martyr Theodore; on Fridays of the 2nd, 3rd, and 4th weeks of the Great Fast, the troparion to the Martyrs and for the Departed; on Friday of the 5th week there is Small Compline with the troparia of the Akathist and of the Great Canon; on Friday of the 6th week there is the troparion of St. Lazarus. After the given troparion follows: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” and the Priest proclaims: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and with the prayer: “O Lord, Lord,” concludes the 1st part. Part II is celebrated in the ordinary manner even unto “Our Father.” After this the troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord,” etc. Then: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable.” The Priest proclaims: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and with the prayer: “O Master, God,” etc., concludes the 2nd part.
Part III begins from “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). After this the Psalms: 67 and the 142nd. “Glory to God in the highest.” The canon for the departed of the current tone, sometimes even two canons. “It is truly right,” but without prostrations. The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “O Lord of hosts”[^112] we do not sing, but instead of “O Lord of hosts” the kontakia are taken, namely: on Friday of the 1st week, the kontakion of the Holy Martyr Theodore; on Fridays of the 2nd, 3rd, and 4th weeks, the kontakion for the departed: “With the Saints give rest” (*Со Святыми упокой*); on Friday of the 5th week and on Wednesday of the 5th week, since then there is Small Compline, the kontakia of the Saturday of the Akathist and of the Great Canon; on Friday of the 6th week, the kontakion of St. Lazarus. After the kontakion: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times), “Thou Who at all times,” “More honorable,” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaims: “God be merciful unto us,” etc., but makes no prostrations. After the prostrations 

=== LEAF p111 ===
[Book Page 87]

there follow (the Trisagion, “Our Father,” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) [12 times] are omitted) the prayers: “O undefiled, unblemished,” and “And grant unto us,” “Our hope,” but without kneeling. (The prayer “O most merciful Master” is omitted.) The smallest dismissal, commemorations, and the blessing.

---


§. 42.

Order of Great Compline with Litiya.

The Priest in phelonion and epitrachelion censes the holy sanctuary, opens the royal doors, censes the whole church, stands before the royal doors, and when the chanter saith: “Command!” he begins: “O Lord, bless!” And he proclaims: “Glory to the holy...” — and thus follows: “Come, let us worship.” The Priest, having entered after this into the holy sanctuary, shuts the royal doors, and having divested of the phelonion, in the epitrachelion comes forth before the royal doors. The chanter meanwhile begins Part I of Great Compline, saying after “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) 3 psalms. The Priest goes into the holy sanctuary, and the chanter reads Psalms: 4, 6, 12 [Pss. 4, 6, 12]. “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Alleluia” (thrice), “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and.” And immediately Psalms: 24, 30, and the 90th [Pss. 24, 30, 90]. “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Alleluia” (thrice), “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and.” And immediately the Priest comes forth and sings once: “God is with us.” The choir repeats this 1st verse twice. After this the Priest, standing before the royal doors, reads the verses: “Unto the ends of the earth,” etc., and the choir after each verse sings: “God is with us,” etc. At the end the Priest again sings this first verse once, and the choir repeats it twice. After this canticle of the Prophet Isaiah there follows: “The day being past,” etc., “The bodiless nature,” “O Most Holy Virgin,” “I believe,” “O Most Holy Trinity,” each verse twice. After these: The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. The troparion of the feast (for instance, of the Nativity of Christ, the Theophany, or the Annunciation), and that once. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” “In the name of the Lord,” etc. And the Priest proclaims: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and with the prayer: “O Lord, Lord of hosts,” concludes Part I, having entered into the sanctuary. Part II begins from “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice), after which follow Psalms: 50 and the 101st [Pss. 50, 101]. The prayer of King Manasses, which the Priest reads before the royal doors. The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. The kontakion of the feast. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” “In the name of the Lord,” etc., and the Priest proclaims: “O Lord Jesus Christ,” etc., and with the prayer: “O Master, God the Father,” etc., concludes Part II, having entered into

=== LEAF p112 ===
[Book Page 88]

the holy sanctuary. Part III begins from “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). After this Psalms: 69 and the 142nd [Pss. 69, 142]. “Glory to God in the highest” (*Слава во вышнихъ Богу*) is read. — Meanwhile the Priest vests in the phelonion, opens the royal doors, blesses the censer, and in the usual manner, as at the entrance, goes forth by the northern door, censes the entire church, and after the censing goes unto the table or *Tetrapod* in the midst of the church, where there are already prepared 5 loaves or prosphora, wheat, wine, and oil. The arrangement of the *Tetrapod* is thus: on the right side is placed the wine, on the left the oil, in the middle the wheat, and upon it 5 loaves (prosphora), and that four prosphora are placed cruciformly, and the fifth lies upon them touching all four. After this the choir after “Glory to God in the highest,” etc., sings the stichera at the Litiya (and that 5 with “Glory,” and “Now and”), and upon the conclusion of these the Priest chants the litany at the Litiya: “Save, O God, Thy people,” etc. After this, the stichera at the aposticha. Next: “Now Thou lettest depart.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. The troparion of the feast (thrice). (When the Priest sings the troparion, he at the same time also censes.) After the troparion the Priest proclaims: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and reads the prayer over the blessing of the loaves: “O Lord Jesus Christ,” etc., taking first the prosphora lying on top, and having then set it back in its place, with outstretched fingers he signs the entire oblation at the words: “Bless these loaves, the wheat, the wine, and the oil” with a single sign of the cross. After this prayer follows: “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (*Буди имя Господне*) (thrice), while the Priest censes the oblation, — and after this Psalm 33 [Ps. 33] is read up to “Shall not want any good thing.” The Priest goes unto the royal doors, and the chanter upon the conclusion of the psalm saith: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father”; and the Priest standing before the royal doors, at the exclamation of the choir: “In the name of the Lord bless, Father,” begins Matins, saying: “Glory to the holy...” etc.[^113]

It is necessary to note that if Compline is combined with Litiya, then from Compline everything beginning from the canon is omitted.

### ARTICLE III.

§. 43.

On the Midnight Offices.

The ordinary Midnight Office is threefold: (α) daily, (β) Saturday, and (γ) Sunday.

=== LEAF p113 ===
[Book Page 89]

The daily Midnight Office is read on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, and Friday. — The Saturday one, however, only on Saturday; and the Sunday one on Sundays of the whole year whereon there is no vigil.

Besides the ordinary Midnight Offices there are also extraordinary ones. Such are: (α) in the Great Fast; (β) on feasts; (γ) on Holy and Great Saturday.

### 1) Ordinary Midnight Offices.

§. 44.

Order of the Daily Midnight Office.

On weekdays it is usually read in private, but sometimes it is also sung in church before Matins. — If we sing this Midnight Office in church, the Priest in the epitrachelion before the royal doors makes the usual beginning, proclaiming: “Blessed is our God,” etc. After this: “O Heavenly King,” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times), “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). And the Priest enters into the holy sanctuary. Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. The 17th Kathisma, which hath 3 antiphons. Before the 2nd and 3rd antiphons: “Alleluia” (thrice), “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and.” After this: “I believe.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. Troparia: “Behold, the Bridegroom,” “Glory,” “That day,” and “Now and,” “Thee, the invincible rampart”; and immediately “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). After this the Priest comes forth and reads the prayer: “Thou Who at all times.” After this: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” “In the name of the Lord,” etc., and the Priest immediately proclaims: “God be merciful unto us,” etc., whereunto the choir answers: “Amen.” After this the Priest with the prayer: “O Master, God the Father,” etc., concludes Part I.

Part II begins from: “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice), and immediately Psalms: 120 and the 133rd [Pss. 120, 133]. “Glory,” and “Now and.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. After the exclamation follow the troparia for the departed: “Remember, O Lord,” “With profound wisdom,” “Glory,” “With the Saints give rest,” “Now and,” “All generations bless thee,” etc. After this the chanter saith: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times), whereunto the Priest reads the prayer: “Remember, O Lord.” There are added also the troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord,” etc. Litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc. (*Molitvoslov*, p. 47). After these, the smallest dismissal. Commemorations: “Let us pray for our most holy Hierarch,” etc. Furthermore after the commemorations the Priest proclaims aloud the prayers: “Remit, forgive,” “Those who hate,” etc., and gives the blessing.

=== LEAF p114 ===
[Book Page 90]

Note: In our *Molitvoslov* there are after the prayer “For the departed” also the troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord,” etc., and the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” further “Remit, forgive,” etc., and “Those who hate and wrong us,” etc., but all this is not contained in the Horologia.[^114]

§. 45.

Order of the Saturday Midnight Office.

The Saturday Midnight Office is celebrated in the same manner as the daily one, except that: (α) instead of the 17th Kathisma the 9th Kathisma is taken, that is to say, Psalms: 64, 65, 66, 67, 68, and the 69th [Pss. 64, 65, 66, 67, 68, 69]; (β) instead of the troparia: “Behold, the Bridegroom,” etc., there are read the troparia in honor of the Most Holy Trinity: “Uncreated nature” (*Несозда́нное естество̀*); finally (γ) instead of the prayer: “Those who hate,” etc., the prayer of St. Eustratius is taken: “Magnifying, I magnify Thee,” etc.

§. 46.

Order of the Sunday Midnight Office.

If it be celebrated in church, then: The Priest, having put on the epitrachelion, makes before the royal doors the usual beginning: “Blessed is our God,” etc. The choir: “Amen,” and immediately: “O Heavenly King,” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times), “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). During this the Priest enters into the holy sanctuary, and the chanter begins to read Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. After this (instead of the kathisma), the canon to the Most Holy Trinity, which is found in the Octoechos under each tone. After the canon, first the Triadicon Megalynarion, consisting of several troparia (“It is truly meet to praise Thee, Who art God,” “The Chief of the Cherubim,” “Unto the unoriginate Father and God,” “Let us all sing,” “With radiant,” “Before Whom tremble,” “It is truly meet reverently,” “Receive tears,” “Glory,” “O Father Almighty,” and at the end “Now and,” “From the dead”). After this is sung the Megalynarion of the Theotokos: “It is truly right,” and thus: The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation and (among us) the Troparia:[^115] “Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us,” and the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc., with the exclamation: “Hear us, O God.” After the litany the smallest dismissal, and that

=== LEAF p115 ===
[Book Page 91]

the resurrectional one (“Christ, our true God, Who rose from the dead,” etc.). Commemorations. (The commemoration for the departed is omitted.) The prayer: “Remit, forgive,” etc., and the prayer to the Most Holy Trinity: “All-powerful and life-creating,” and thus the blessing.

If, however, the Sunday Midnight Office is read in private, then there is read the general Triadicon canon, recorded in the *Molitvoslov*, and at the end after the troparia “Have mercy on us, O Lord” (*Помилуй насъ Господи*) the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc., is not said, but instead of it: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Lord, bless,” and the Dismissal. And thus the prayer: “All-powerful and life-creating,” etc.

### 2) Extraordinary Midnight Offices.

§. 47.

Order of the Daily Midnight Office in the Holy Great Fast.

It is celebrated in the same manner as the daily Midnight Office at other times, except that it is necessary to note that at the end of Part I, after “God be merciful unto us,” etc., and before the prayer: “O Master, God the Father,” there are prostrations with the prayer of St. Ephrem: “O Lord and Master,” etc. And that is: (α) three great prostrations with the prayer of St. Ephrem. (A great prostration is when a man falls upon his knees and bows his head unto the earth as far as he can.) (β) 12 lesser prostrations (with the verses: “O God, be merciful unto me a sinner” (*Бо́же, ми́лостивъ бꙋ́ди мнѣ̀ грѣ́шномꙋ*); “O God, cleanse my sins and have mercy on me”; and “Without number have I sinned, O Lord, forgive me”). After the prayer: “O Master, God the Father,” again one great prostration. The rest in the usual manner.

§. 48.

Order of the Festal Midnight Office.

If a certain feast of the Lord, or a feast of the Mother of God, or a feast of a great Saint, or the Afterfeast of the Nativity of Christ and the Theophany fall upon weekdays whereon Litiya is not celebrated: then the Midnight Office is celebrated in the same manner as the daily Midnight Office, except that: (α) the troparion: “Behold, the Bridegroom,” etc., is omitted, and instead of these the troparion of the feast is sung; (β) the troparion and prayer for the departed are omitted, and

=== LEAF p116 ===
[Book Page 92]

instead of them the kontakion of the feast is sung. Upon the conclusion of the kontakion there are sung among us the Troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord,” etc., and the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc., with the exclamation: “Hear us, O God.”[^116] Dismissal. Commemorations. (The commemoration for the departed is omitted.) Blessing. (The last two prayers are omitted.)

§. 49.

Order of the Midnight Office on Holy and Great Saturday.

Before the Resurrection of Christ, likewise before midnight, there is to be celebrated the Midnight Office, or the so-called “Burial Service” (*Nadhrobne*). And that in the following order: The Priest, vested in a black epitrachelion, before the royal doors begins the Midnight Office with: “Blessed is our God.” After this the choir: “Amen,” “O Heavenly King,” “Our Father,” “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. And there follows the Matins canon of Great Saturday: “By a wave of the sea.” After the canon, instead of “It is truly right,” the Heirmos: “Weep not for Me, O Mother.” And immediately: The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. Troparia: “When Thou didst descend,” “The noble Joseph,” and “Unto the Myrrhbearers.” After these the Priest goes from the royal doors unto the Tomb, and having bowed, censes the Shroud (*Plashchanytsia*), and after this transfers It unto the holy table, where he spreads It out and again censes It.
