After this he saith the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc., as at the Sunday Midnight Office. Dismissal.

### ARTICLE IV.

§. 50.

On Matins.

Ordinary Matins is divided into: (α) Small, that is to say, daily without the Great Doxology; and (β) Great, with the Great Doxology.

Daily Matins is celebrated on weekdays if there occur on them one or more minor Saints.

=== LEAF p117 ===
[Book Page 93]

Great Matins, however, is celebrated on feasts and on Sundays. And in accordance with this it is: Festal and Sunday. But since feasts are of two kinds: those with the Doxology and those with the Polyeleos, so also festal Matins services are of two kinds, that is to say: (α) with the Doxology, but without the Polyeleos; (β) with both the Doxology and the Polyeleos.

Besides these ordinary Matins services there are also extraordinary ones, whereof the most notable are the following: (α) Matins for the departed; (β) Matins in the Great Fast; (γ) Matins with the Akathist; (δ) Matins in Passion Week; (ε) Matins with the Stases on Holy and Great Saturday; (ζ) Matins in Bright Week; (η) Matins on the Exaltation of the Precious Cross.

### 1) Ordinary Matins Services.

§. 51.

Order of Small Daily Matins.

If there be no Midnight Office in church,[^117] then the Priest, having entered into the church, makes three bows before the royal doors, and having kissed and put on the epitrachelion, begins in the usual manner: “Blessed is our God.” The choir: “Amen,” and immediately: “O Heavenly King,” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times), “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). Psalms: 19 and the 20th [Pss. 19, 20], after which: “Glory,” and “Now and.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. And after this the reader saith the troparion: “Save, O Lord, Thy people,” etc., “Glory,” “Thou Who wast voluntarily lifted up upon the Cross,” etc., “Now and,” Theotokion: “O fearsome defense,” etc. Upon the conclusion of these troparia the Priest proclaims the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc., “Furthermore let us pray for our most devout Emperor,” etc., unto which petitions the choir answers: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (6 times). Then the exclamation: “For a merciful and man-befriending God art Thou,” etc. Whereunto the choir answers: “Amen,” and immediately proclaims aloud: “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaims: “Glory to the holy, consubstantial, and life-creating...” etc. The choir: “Amen,” and begins first: “Glory to God in the highest” (*Слава во вышнихъ Богу*) (thrice), after this: “O Lord, open Thou my lips,” etc. (twice). And in order it saith the Six Psalms (that is to say, Psalms: 3, 37, 62, 87, 102, and the 142nd [Pss. 3, 37, 62, 87, 102, 142]) in a quiet voice. After the recitation of three Psalms: “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Alleluia” (thrice), “Lord,

=== LEAF p118 ===
[Book Page 94]

have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and.” Upon the completion of the Six Psalms: “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Alleluia” (thrice), “Glory to Thee, O God” (the third time with chanting). After this the Priest, standing before the royal doors, saith the litany of peace: “In peace let us pray to the Lord.” After its exclamation the Priest sings: “God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us,” etc., and the chanter repeats it 4 times (according to the verses). The Priest, having bowed, withdraws unto his place, and there secretly recites the morning prayers; and the chanter begins to sing the troparia, and that: the troparion of the Saint (twice); “Glory,” and “Now and,” the dismissal Theotokion according to the tone of the Saint (or as the typikon prescribes). After the troparia follows: Kathisma 1. The 1st Sessional Hymn with the Theotokion. Kathisma 2. The 2nd Sessional Hymn. (Before the kathisma, both the 1st and the 2nd, the choir saith: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) [thrice] and “Glory,” and the reader continues from “Now and.” After the kathisma: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) [twice], “To Thee, O Lord,” “Amen.”) After the 2nd Sessional Hymn and after “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” the chanter reads Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. After this the canons, usually 3 (two from the Octoechos, and one of the Saint). The troparia in the canons are read with the appropriate refrains, and that: let there be 14 troparia in all with “Glory,” and with “Now and.” The heirmoi are taken from the first canon. The heirmoi from the canon of the Saint are taken as the Katavasia, but only after the 3rd, 6th, 8th, and 9th odes. After the 3rd ode there is the small litany and the Sessional Hymn from the Common Menaion, that is to say, of the Saint. — After the 6th ode, the small litany, the kontakion and ikos of the Saint. Towards the end of the 8th ode instead of “Glory” (*Сла́ва*) we say: “We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord”; before the Katavasia the choir saith in the tone of the canon of the Saint: “We praise, we bless, we worship,” etc., and they sing the Heirmos of the 8th ode of the canon of the Saint as Katavasia. After the 8th ode the Priest in the sanctuary proclaims: “The Theotokos and Mother of Light in hymns let us magnify,” whereunto the choir loudly sings: “My soul doth magnify the Lord,” etc., chanting unto the verses: “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc. After the Katavasia of the 9th ode: “It is truly right.” The small litany and the Exaposteilarion, and that of the day; “Glory,” of the Saint; “Now and,” Theotokion, or if it be Wednesday or Friday: Stavrotheotokion. And immediately Psalms: 148, 149, and the 150th [Pss. 148, 149, 150] are read (“Praise the Lord from the heavens,” “Sing unto the Lord a new song,” and “Praise God in His Saints”). Upon the conclusion of Psalm 150, the Priest proclaims from the sanctuary: “Glory to God Who hath shown us the light,” and the choir reads the Doxology in order: “Glory to God in the highest.”[^118]

=== LEAF p119 ===
[Book Page 95]

After this the Priest comes forth from the sanctuary and before the royal doors saith the litany: “Let us complete our morning prayer.” After the 2nd exclamation of this litany the Priest enters into the sanctuary, and the choir begins to sing the stichera at the aposticha (from the Octoechos) with the usual refrains. (Before the 1st there is no verse; before the 2nd and 3rd: “We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy,” and “Let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us” [Ps. 89:14, 17]). At the end: “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion.[^119] After this the choir reads: “It is good to give praise,” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. After this the troparion of the Saint. “Glory,” and “Now and,” the dismissal Theotokion according to the tone of the Saint. The Priest after this comes forth from the sanctuary and before the royal doors chants the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God.” The daily dismissal, and that the small one, and the blessing.

§. 52.

Order of Festal Matins Services.

(α) Matins with the Great Doxology. This Matins is celebrated on minor feasts, that is to say, when the feast or the Saint hath stichera on 6 and the Great Doxology. Beginning from the start, up unto the Doxology, it is small,[^120] and only here towards the end of the Praises (*Ainoi*) the Priest vests in the epitrachelion and phelonion, and stands before the Holy Table, awaiting the end of the stichera at the Praises. — When the chanter hath concluded these, then he opens the royal doors and proclaims: “Glory to God, Who hath shown us the light,” and goes forth onto the ambo and there with the chanter sings the Doxology.[^121] After this The Trisagion (thrice). And immediately the troparion of the feast with the Theotokion, after which the Priest enters unto the Holy Table and there chants the litanies: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and “Let us complete.”

=== LEAF p120 ===
[Book Page 96]

The great dismissal, and having given the blessing, he shuts the royal doors. (There are no stichera at the Aposticha.)[^122]

(β) Matins with the Polyeleos, with the Gospel, and with the Great Doxology. It is celebrated on weekdays if there occur a Saint having a polyeleos, or a feast of the Lord, or a feast of the Mother of God. The order of this Matins is as follows: The Priest, having put on the epitrachelion, comes before the royal doors, and having made a bow there, saith: “Blessed is our God.” The choir: “Amen.” Then (if there was no Midnight Office): “O Heavenly King.” After this also the rest of the usual beginning. After “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice), Psalms: 19 and the 20th [Pss. 19, 20]. And immediately The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. After this the troparia are read: “Save, O God, Thy people,” etc., after which the litany for the Emperor with the exclamation. Unto the exclamation the choir answers: “Amen,” and immediately proclaims: “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” Unto this the Priest: “Glory to the holy...” etc. The choir: “Amen,” and immediately: “Glory to God in the highest,” etc. (thrice), “O Lord, open Thou my lips,” etc. (twice). And there follows the Six Psalms. (The Priest enters into the holy sanctuary and recites the priestly morning prayers.) After the Six Psalms the Priest comes forth and the litany of peace is chanted. “God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us,” etc. (4 times). Festal troparia. Kathisma 1. The 1st Sessional Hymn of the Saint. Kathisma 2. The 2nd Sessional Hymn of the Saint.[^123] After this: “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion, and instead of the 3rd kathisma the choir sings the Polyeleos (that is to say, verses from the 19th Kathisma: “Praise the name of the Lord,” etc.). After this: The Megalynarion with the verses. After this the small litany is said and the choir sings the 3rd Sessional Hymn (the polyeleos one), “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion. Then the Gradual Hymns of the 4th Tone, the 1st Antiphon: “From my youth,” which are sung to 4 verses, chanting unto the 3rd verse “Glory,” and unto the 4th “Now and.” During the singing of the 3rd and 4th verses, that is to say, at “Glory,” and “Now and,” the Priest vests in the phelonion, ascends unto the Holy Table, and proclaims: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” Then the choir sings the daily Prokimenon. After the Prokimenon the Priest opens the royal doors, saying: “Let us pray to the Lord.” Unto this the choir: “Lord, have mercy,” and immediately the Priest saith the exclamation: “For holy art Thou,” etc. The choir: “Amen.” And again the Priest: “Let every breath praise 

=== LEAF p121 ===
[Book Page 97]

the Lord,” and censes the Holy Table and from the royal doors the people. After the choir sings: “Let every breath praise the Lord” (*Вся́кое дыха́нїе да хва́литъ Го́спода*) twice, the Priest [saith]: “That we may be accounted worthy to hear the holy Gospel,” etc. Then upon the choir’s response the Priest [saith]: “Peace be to all!” The choir: (“And to thy spirit” (*И҆ дꙋ́хови твое́мꙋ*)). And thereafter: “Wisdom, let us attend, let us hear the holy Gospel,” etc., and he reads the holy Gospel, and that standing before the Holy Table. After the Gospel hath been read, immediately Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. (The Priest, after reading the holy Gospel, goes forth and gives the holy Gospel to be kissed upon the ambo.) — After Psalm 50: “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): “Through the prayers of the holy Archangel [or: holy Apostle, or: Prophet],” etc. ([Name]), in whose honor the service is celebrated. At “Now and”: “Through the prayers of the Most Holy Theotokos,” etc., is sung. At the end the refrain: “Have mercy on me, O God,” etc., and the sticheron of the Saint.[^124] After this the Priest, having returned unto the Holy Table, chants the litany: “Save, O God, Thy people,” etc., whereunto the choir [responds]: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). And immediately the Priest: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), whereunto the choir again: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). And immediately the Priest [saith] the exclamation: “Through the mercy and compassions...”[^125] etc. After this exclamation the Priest shuts the royal doors, divests of the phelonion, and the chanter begins to sing the canon on 14. In the canon all things are taken in order. Before the Katavasia of the 8th ode the choir proclaims the refrain: “We praise, we bless, we worship,” etc., and sings the Katavasia. After the Katavasia the Priest proclaims: “The Theotokos and Mother of Light,” etc., and the magnification of the feast is sung (that is, the Heirmos of the 9th ode with the refrain) or “More honorable than the Cherubim.”[^126] After the 9th ode: the small litany and the Exaposteilarion. Stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*): and that we set 4; “Glory,” of the Saint; “Now and,” Theotokion. At this the Priest vests in the phelonion, opens the royal doors, and goes forth onto the ambo, and proclaims: “Glory to God, Who hath shown us the light”; they sing the Great Doxology. The Trisagion. The troparion of the Saint and Theotokion. The Priest, having entered into the sanctuary, saith before the Holy Table the litanies: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and “Let us complete.” The great dismissal, the blessing, and he shuts the royal doors.

---


=== LEAF p122 ===
[Book Page 98]

§. 53.

Order of Sunday Matins.

It is celebrated thus: The beginning is the usual one, as at daily Matins without any exception. After the Six Psalms, when the choir sings: “Alleluia” (thrice), “Glory to Thee, O God,” the Priest, standing before the royal doors, saith the litany of peace. After this: “God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us,” etc., which chant is sung in the current tone. The resurrectional (Sunday) troparion (twice); “Glory,” of the Saint; “Now and,” Theotokion from the Octoechos according to the tone of the Saint. After the troparia the choir immediately saith: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), and “Glory” (*Сла́ва*); the reader continues: “Now and,” and reads Kathisma 1. After the kathisma: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (twice), “To Thee, O Lord,” “Amen”; and immediately the 1st Sessional Hymn from the Octoechos is sung, first plain, and thereafter unto the refrain: “Arise, O Lord my God,” etc., and at the end with “Glory,” and “Now and.” After the 1st Sessional Hymn the choir: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory” (*Сла́ва*); the reader: “Now and,” and reads the 2nd Kathisma. After this: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (twice), “To Thee, O Lord,” “Amen”; and immediately the 2nd Sessional Hymn with “Glory,” and “Now and,” whereunto its Theotokion is sung. After the 2nd Sessional Hymn, as before, the reader reads the 3rd Kathisma (this is the 17th: “Blessed are the undefiled”), or [the choir] sings the Polyeleos.[^127] But instead of the 3rd Sessional Hymn the troparia after the Evlogitaria (*Neporochny*) are sung: “The angelic host,” with the refrain: “Blessed art Thou, O Lord: teach me Thy statutes.” After this: the small litany. The Hypakoe of the tone. The Gradual Hymns (*Stepenny*). After the Gradual Hymns the Priest, vested in the phelonion, proclaims from the Holy Table: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” and the choir sings the Prokimenon. After the Prokimenon the Priest opens the royal doors, saying: “Let us pray to the Lord.” Whereunto the choir answers: “Lord, have mercy,” and immediately he proclaims: “For holy art Thou, O our God,” etc. Again after this exclamation the Priest saith once: “Let every breath praise the Lord,” and the chanter [saith it] twice. After this the Priest begins to cense the Holy Table and the people from the royal doors, and proclaims: “That we may be accounted worthy,” etc.; further: “Peace be to all!” The choir: “And to thy spirit.” Then the Priest: “Wisdom, let us attend, let us hear the holy Gospel,” the reading from the holy Gospel according to Mark (or Matthew, or Luke, or John), and he reads the holy Gospel before the Holy Table. Upon the conclusion of the Gospel the Priest reads before the Holy Table: “Having seen the Resurrection of Christ.” The chanter after this

=== LEAF p123 ===
[Book Page 99]

reads Psalm 50 [Ps. 50], during which the Priest comes forth from the sanctuary onto the ambo and gives the holy Gospel to be kissed. After Psalm 50 the chanter sings: “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): “Through the prayers of the holy Apostles,” etc.; “Now and”: “Through the prayers of the Most Holy Theotokos,” etc. Then unto “Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great [mercy],” etc., the resurrectional sticheron[^128] is sung in the 6th Tone (“Jesus, having risen from the tomb”). And immediately the Priest saith the litany: “Save, O God, Thy people,” etc. After this: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). Exclamation: “Through the mercy and compassions,” etc. After this the Priest shuts the royal doors, and the choir sings the canons; and that: the Resurrectional, Cross-Resurrectional, and Theotokion from the Octoechos.[^129] Only the heirmoi and katavasiai are sung, while the other troparia are read. The troparia of the canons are read on 14 with the proper refrains in order, as was stated. — Before the Katavasia of the 8th ode the chanter proclaims aloud: “We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord, singing and exalting Him unto all ages.” After the 8th ode the Priest proclaims: “The Theotokos and Mother of Light,” etc.,[^130] and the choir sings: “My soul doth magnify [the Lord],” refraining: “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc., with the verses. After the 9th ode the small litany, and immediately the Priest, having come forth before the royal doors, proclaims: “Holy is the Lord our God” (*Святъ Господь Богъ нашъ*) (thrice). The choir likewise answers thrice. After this the Exaposteilarion. At the Praises (*Ainoi*) the choir sings: “Let every breath praise the Lord; praise Him in the highest; to Thee song is due, O God”; and the second verse of Psalm 148 [Ps. 148] with this ending according to the current tone. The other verses of the psalm are read from the 148th and the 149th [Pss. 148, 149]. We set 8 verses from the last verse of Psalm 149 [Ps. 149]: “This glory shall be to all His saints.” And we sing the resurrectional and Anatolian (*vostochny*) stichera with the refrains taken from Psalm 150 [Ps. 150], and with the two resurrectional ones: “Arise, O Lord my God,” etc., and “I will give thanks unto Thee, O Lord,” etc. After this: “Glory,” the Gospel sticheron; “Now and,” “Most blessed art thou.” The Priest vests in the phelonion, goes forth before the Holy Table, and makes a bow before the Holy Table. Upon the conclusion of the singing he opens the royal doors, goes forth onto the ambo, makes a bow, and proclaims aloud: “Glory to God, Who hath shown us the light.” The chanter goes unto the midst of the church, and together with the congregation the Great Doxology is sung. After the Doxology the Priest sings the Trisagion once, and the chanter twice. Then the resurrectional troparion by both, and that: if it be the 1st, 3rd, 5th, 7th

=== LEAF p124 ===
[Book Page 100]

tone: “Today salvation is come unto the world” (*Дне́сь спасенїе мїрꙋ*); but if it be the 2nd, 4th, 6th, and 8th Tone, then: “Having risen from the tomb” (*Molitvoslov*, p. 424). After this the Priest, having bowed, enters into the holy sanctuary, and takes the Gospel from the *Tetrapod*, if it was there. After this the Priest, standing before the Holy Table, saith the litanies: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and “Let us complete our morning [prayer],” etc. At the end he makes the great dismissal, commemorating also the Saint in whose honor the service is sung, and gives the blessing. After the dismissal he shuts the royal doors.

Note: At Sunday Matins there are taken in order 11 resurrectional Gospels, 11 Gospel stichera, and 11 resurrectional Exaposteilaria, which agree in meaning. Besides this, such are the characteristics of this Sunday Matins, that for the most part it hath no Polyeleos, but hath the resurrectional troparia after the Evlogitaria (*Neporochny*); at it “Having seen the Resurrection of Christ” (*Воскресеніе Христово*) is read after the Gospel; and at the end it hath “Holy is the Lord our God.”

### 2) Extraordinary Matins Services.

§. 54.

Order of Matins for the Departed.

This Matins is small, without the Gospel and without the Great Doxology. It is celebrated thus: The beginning is the usual one, only that after the Six Psalms, instead of the litany of peace, the litany for the departed is taken (*Molitvoslov*, p. 328). Instead of: “God is the Lord,” etc., “Alleluia” (4 times), and immediately there are sung in the 8th Tone the troparia: “With profound wisdom,” “Glory,” and “Now and”: “Thee, the rampart.” After the troparia, the usual 1st Kathisma, and the 1st Sessional Hymn from the Octoechos. Immediately after the 1st Sessional Hymn: “Blessed are the undefiled” is sung in two stases; and that the first stasis with the refrain: “Blessed art Thou,” etc., and at the end of this stasis the verse: “Unless Thy law had been,” etc. (thrice). After this the small litany for the departed (“Again and again in peace let us pray to the Lord”; “Furthermore let us pray for the remission of sins of the servants of God departed in blessed memory,” etc. And the exclamation: “For Thou art the resurrection,” etc. [See *Molitvoslov*, p. 355]), and immediately the second stasis begins to be sung: “I am Thine.” After these two stases follow the troparia: “The choir of the Saints,” with the refrain: “Blessed art Thou, O Lord.” After the troparia, the litany for the departed,[^131] and thus only the 2nd Sessional Hymn (“Give rest, O our Savior” (*Оу҆поко́й, Спа́се на́шъ...*)). After

=== LEAF p125 ===
[Book Page 101]

the 2nd Sessional Hymn, Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. After this the canon of the temple with the Heirmos on 6, and the canon from the Triodion on 8. After the 3rd ode of the canon the usual litany, and not for the departed. After the 6th ode the litany for the departed, and the kontakion: “With the Saints.” After the 9th the usual litany, and not for the departed, and the Exaposteilarion: “Ruling over the living and the dead,” etc. All the rest in order. At the Praises (*Ainoi*) 4 stichera from the Triodion; “Glory,” and “Now and,” likewise from the Triodion. The Small Doxology. After this the litany: “Let us complete our morning [prayer],” etc. Stichera at the aposticha for the departed with verses for the departed, and with “Glory,” and “Now and.” Psalm: “It is good to give praise.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. The troparion for the departed: “With profound wisdom”; “Glory,” and “Now and”: “Thee, the rampart.” The litany for the departed (*Molitvoslov*, p. 355). The Dismissal.[^132] The blessing, and thus the Priest proclaims: “In blessed falling asleep, eternal rest...” etc. The choir: “Memory eternal.” — This Matins is celebrated on All Souls Saturdays.

§. 55.

Order of Matins Services in the Holy Great Fast.

(α) Order of Daily Matins in the Holy Great Fast with the First Hour (excepting Saturdays and Sundays). It begins: “Blessed is our God” (*Благословенъ Богъ нашъ*); but if there was no Midnight Office in church, then: “Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.” “O Heavenly King.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times). “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice).[^133] Psalms: 19 and the 20th [Pss. 19, 20], and all the rest in order. “God is the Lord” (*Богъ Господь*) is not sung, but instead of that “Alleluia” (thrice) with 4 verses. (On Saturday, Sunday, and on feasts “God is the Lord” (*Богъ Господь*) is sung.) Instead of the troparia, the 3 Triadica are sung slowly, and that in such a manner that at the 2nd verse “Glory” (*Сла́ва*) is taken, and at the 3rd “Now and.” — Let it be noted that at the end of the 1st Triadicon of all the 8 tones the endings of the day are said.[^134] (These endings are given in the *Horologion*, and in the *Molitvoslov*, p. 107.) After the Triadica, Kathisma 1. The 1st Sessional Hymn. After this: Kathisma 2. The 2nd Sessional Hymn. (These ses-

=== LEAF p126 ===
[Book Page 102]

sional hymns are the penitential ones of the Octoechos which are found at the end of the Menaion, and the martyr hymn (*Martyrikon*) with the refrain: “Wondrous is God in His Saints.” Besides this, let us also note that in the Great Fast neither after the 1st nor after the 2nd Kathisma is the small litany said, but the choir saith: “Alleluia” (thrice), “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and.”) After the 2nd Sessional Hymn the chanter saith: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” and thus reads Psalm 50 [Ps. 50], and immediately the Priest comes forth before the royal doors and saith the litany: “Save, O God, Thy people,” etc., with the exclamation: “Through the mercy and compassions.” After this litany follows the canon combined with the Biblical Odes (*Canticles of Moses*).[^135] In the Great Fast on weekdays there are 2 canons: of the Octoechos for the day with the Heirmos on 8, and of the Menaion for the Saint on 6. In whichever odes the Triodion (*Triodion-canon / Trepesnets*) occurs, we omit the canon of the Octoechos with the Heirmos, and sing the canon of the Saint with the Heirmos on 6, and the Triodion on 8. At the end the Katavasia is sung, and that the Heirmos of the Triodion canon.[^136] The Katavasia on weekdays throughout the entire Forty Days is sung thus: On Monday after the 1st, 3rd, 6th, 8th, and 9th ode. — On Tuesday after the 2nd, 3rd, 6th, 8th, and 9th ode. — On Wednesday after the 3rd, 6th, 8th, and 9th ode. — On Thursday after the 4th, 6th, 8th, and 9th ode. — On Friday after the 3rd, 5th, 6th, 8th, and 9th ode. — On Saturday after the 3rd, 6th, 7th, 8th, and 9th ode. After the 3rd ode, the Sessional Hymn of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion. After the 6th, the kontakion and ikos of the Saint. (If, however, the Saint hath no kontakion in the Menaion, then here the martyr sessional hymn printed at the end of the Lenten Triodion is said.) After the 8th ode, at the exclamation of the Priest: “The Theotokos and Mother of Light,” etc., “My soul doth magnify [the Lord]” and “More honorable than the Cherubim.” After the 9th ode: “It is truly right” (*Достойно есть*) is sung with one bow. The small litany. The Lenten Exaposteilaria according to the 8 tones with the proper endings (*Molitvoslov*, pp. 137 and 106).

=== LEAF p127 ===
[Book Page 103]

The Praises (*Ainoi*) are read.[^137] After this the Priest saith the exclamation: “To Thee glory is due,” etc., and immediately: “Glory to Thee Who hast shown us the light.” At this “Glory to God in the highest,” etc., is read. After this the Priest before the royal doors proclaims the litany: “Let us complete.” Stichera at the aposticha from the Triodion with the usual refrains, and the martyr hymn (*Martyrikon*); “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion.[^138] After this: “It is good to give praise” (*Бла́го є̂́сть*) (twice), etc. The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. The troparion: “Standing in the temple of Thy glory,” is read. After this (instead of the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc.): “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times), “Lord, bless,” during which the Priest comes forth before the royal doors and proclaims: “Blessed and glorified is Christ our God,” and saith the prayer: “O Heavenly King.” After this: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” and “In the name of the Lord bless, Father”; and the Priest proclaims: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and makes 3 prostrations with the prayer of St. Ephrem. — And immediately the First Hour begins from “Come, let us worship,” whereof mention will be made later.

(β) Order of Festal Matins in the Great Fast, if a feast fall upon weekdays (excepting Saturdays and Sundays). — This Matins is great up unto the Doxology (which is small), and after the Doxology it is small. It begins from “Blessed is our God,” and is celebrated in the usual manner. After the litany of peace: “God is the Lord” (*Богъ Господь*) (and not “Alleluia”). The troparia of the feast. Kathisma 1. The 1st Sessional Hymn. Kathisma 2. The 2nd Sessional Hymn. Instead of the 3rd Kathisma: the Polyeleos and Megalynarion. The 3rd Sessional Hymn. The Gradual Hymns (*Stepenny*: “From my youth” (*Ѡ̂тъ ю́ности моея́*)). Festal Prokimenon. Festal Gospel. (“Having seen the Resurrection of Christ” (*Воскресеніе Христово*) is omitted. Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]) with the stichera of the feast. After which the Priest proclaims the litany: “Save, O God, Thy people,” etc., with the exclamation: “Through the mercy and compassions.” After the litany the canon of the feast is combined with the Triodion. The Heirmos of the feast, and the Katavasia of the order. At “The Theotokos and Mother of Light,” the magnification of the feast. Upon the conclusion of the canon, the Exaposteilarion of the feast, and immediately the stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*) of the feast. Before the Doxology the Priest proclaims: “To Thee glory is due,” etc.; after this he saith: “Glory to Thee Who hast shown us the light,” and the chanter reads the Doxology. After this the Priest in the epitrachelion comes forth before the royal doors and chants aloud the litany: “Let us complete our morning [prayer],” etc. Stichera at the apos-

=== LEAF p128 ===
[Book Page 104]

ticha from the Lenten Triodion. After this: “It is good to give praise” (*Бла́го є̂́сть*) (twice), etc. The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. The troparion of the feast. During this the Priest comes forth from the sanctuary and saith the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God.” After this litany 3 prostrations. And immediately the First Hour begins from “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). After this Psalms: 5, 89, and the 100th [Pss. 5, 89, 100]. “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Alleluia” (thrice), “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). Kathisma of the day. “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): Troparion of the feast; “Now and”: “What shall we call thee?” “Order my steps,” further: “Let my mouth be filled,” etc. The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. The kontakion of the feast. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times), during which the Priest comes forth and before the royal doors proclaims aloud the prayer: “Thou Who at all times.” After this: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), etc. “More honorable,” etc. “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaims: “God be merciful unto us,” etc., and makes 3 prostrations. After this again the Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times). And the Priest saith the prayer: “O Christ, the true Light,” and after this the kontakion: “To thee, the champion leader,” etc. The smallest dismissal from “Glory to Thee, O Christ God,” etc.

(γ) Matins with the Canon of St. Andrew of Crete. — This Matins is celebrated on Wednesday evening of the 5th week of the Holy Great Fast. It is a small Matins without the Polyeleos and without the Great Doxology. It is celebrated thus: “Blessed is our God.” “O Heavenly King,” etc., in the usual manner. After the litany of peace, instead of “God is the Lord” (*Богъ Господь*): “Alleluia” (thrice). The Triadica from the Lenten Triodion. Only one, and that the 8th Kathisma, and only one Sessional Hymn from the Triodion. Upon the conclusion of this, Psalm 50 [Ps. 50], and after this the Priest before the royal doors saith the litany: “Save, O God,” etc., and the exclamation: “Through the mercy and compassions,” etc. (In the midst of this litany “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) [thrice] is omitted.) After this the Canon of St. Andrew of Crete begins, and that in such a manner that the chanter begins to sing the Heirmos: “A helper and a protector,” etc.; upon the conclusion of this the choir sings the refrain: “Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me,” and the Priest begins to read the troparia: “Whence shall I begin to weep,” etc., the people making a bow at each. When, however, the canon of the Venerable Mary comes, he saith unto the troparia the refrain: “Venerable Mary, pray unto God for us.” Unto the troparia of the Venerable Andrew: “Venerable Father Andrew, pray unto God for us.” At the 3rd ode the Katavasia is sung, and that the Heirmos of the same ode: “Establish, O Lord”; and the Sessional Hymn is read with the small litany. At the 6th ode the Katavasia: the same Heirmos of the Great Canon. After the 6th ode the small litany; the kontakion: “My soul,” is sung, and the ikos is read; further is read: “In Thy kingdom when Thou comest.” Before the Katavasia of the 8th ode is sung: “We praise, we bless,

=== LEAF p129 ===
[Book Page 105]

we worship,” etc.; upon the conclusion of the Katavasia: “The Theotokos and Mother of Light,” etc., and the chanter sings: “My soul doth magnify [the Lord],” “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc. After this follows the 9th ode twice, as Heirmos and as Katavasia.[^139] Upon the conclusion of this there is the small litany, and the chanter reads the Triadicon Exaposteilarion of the tone, and immediately the stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*). After this the Priest proclaims: “To Thee glory is due,” etc. (*Molitvoslov*, p. 142), whereunto the choir [saith]: “Amen,” and further the Priest saith: “Glory to Thee Who hast shown us the light.” At this the chanter reads the Doxology, during which the Priest comes forth in the epitrachelion, and upon its conclusion chants the litany: “Let us complete.” Stichera at the aposticha from the Lenten Triodion. — After this: “It is good to give praise” (*Бла́го є̂́сть*) (twice). The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. The troparion: “Standing in the temple.” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times), “Lord, bless.” The Priest comes forth before the royal doors, proclaims: “Blessed is He Who is,” etc., and reads the prayer: “O Heavenly King.” After this: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and.” And the Priest proclaims: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and makes 3 prostrations. After the prostrations the First Hour begins, which is celebrated in order without a kathisma. After the Trisagion we say the kontakion: “My soul.”

§. 56.

Order of Matins with the Akathist.

This Matins is celebrated on the Saturday of the 5th week of the Holy Great Fast. It is Matins with the Great Doxology. It is celebrated thus: The beginning is the usual one. After the litany of peace: “God is the Lord,” etc., is sung, for in the Great Fast on Saturday and on Sunday it is always taken. After “God is the Lord” (*Богъ Господь*) the troparion: “The command secretly given” (twice); “Glory,” and “Now and,” the same. After this follows Kathisma 1 (the 16th), and the small litany, after which the kontakion: “To thee, the champion leader.” And the Priest, vested in the phelonion, opens the royal doors and before the Holy Table sings from the Akathist 3 kontakia, ending them with “Alleluia,” and 3 ikoi, ending them with: “Rejoice, Bride unwedded,” alternately. The chanter repetitively sings unto a kontakion: “Alleluia,” and unto an ikos: “Rejoice, Bride unwedded.” (It is necessary to note that during this singing we do not sit, but stand.) Upon the conclusion of the 3rd ikos follows the 2nd Kathisma (the 17th): “Blessed are the undefiled,” and immediately the small litany. After this the choir sings the kontakion: “To thee, the champion leader,” while the Priest, standing before the Holy

=== LEAF p130 ===
[Book Page 106]

Table, saith the other 3 kontakia and ikoi. The choir answers as was stated above. Upon the conclusion of this: Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. After this the canons: of the temple with the Heirmos on 6, and the Theotokion on 6. Katavasia: “I will open my mouth.” (If, however, the temple be dedicated to the Mother of God, then the Theotokion canon is sung on 12.) After the 3rd ode the small litany and kontakion, and again the Priest sings another 3 kontakia and 3 ikoi. At the 6th ode the canon of the temple ceases, and the canon of the Theotokos is sung with the Heirmos on 6, and in addition also the Triodion from the Triodion. Katavasia from the Triodion. After the 6th ode the small litany and the kontakion: “To thee, the champion leader,” and immediately the Priest reads the remaining 3 kontakia and ikoi from the Akathist; at the end the choir again sings: “To thee, the champion leader.” After the 8th ode: “More honorable than the Cherubim” (*Честнѣйшую*) is sung. Stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*) from the Triodion 4. The Great Doxology. The Trisagion (thrice). The troparion of the Theotokos once. 2 litanies. The great dismissal.

§. 57.

Order of Matins Services in Passion Week.

(α) Order of Matins on Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday of Passion Week. These Matins services are small with the Gospel, but without the Great Doxology. They are celebrated thus: The beginning is the usual one. After the Six Psalms the litany of peace and “Alleluia” (thrice) in the 8th Tone. Troparion: “Behold, the Bridegroom” (*Се женихъ*) (thrice) aloud. Then the usual kathismata and sessional hymns from the Triodion. Upon the conclusion of these the Priest, having put on the phelonion, opens the royal doors and saith: “That we may be accounted worthy,” etc., “Wisdom, let us attend, let us hear the holy Gospel,” etc., as at other times, and reads the holy Gospel. After the Gospel the chanter reads Psalm 50 [Ps. 50], and the Priest chants aloud the litany: “Save, O God, Thy people,” etc. After this litany, the Triodion canon from the Triodion (and on Tuesday, the Two-Ode Canon [*Dvoopesnets*]). After the 8th ode “More honorable than the Cherubim” (*Честнѣйшую*) is not sung, but the Heirmos. (From Lazarus Saturday until New Sunday “More honorable than the Cherubim” (*Честнѣйшую*) is not sung, but instead of that the Heirmos of the 9th ode is taken.) After the 9th ode the Priest comes forth before the royal doors for a bow. After the 9th ode the Exaposteilarion: “Thy bridal chamber” (*Черто́гъ тво́й*) (thrice). The rest in order. After the aposticha: “It is good to give praise,” and the rest in the usual manner with prostrations. And the First Hour begins. Dismissal: “The Lord, coming unto His voluntary passion.”

(β) Order of Matins on Thursday of Passion Week. This Matins is small without the Great Doxology. The beginning is the usual one. The litany of peace, after this “Alleluia” (thrice) in 

=== LEAF p131 ===
[Book Page 107]

Tone 8. Troparion: “When the glorious disciples” (*Егда славніи*) (thrice), and after this the Priest, vested in the phelonion, immediately opens the royal doors, proclaiming: “That we may be accounted worthy.” “Wisdom, let us attend, let us hear the holy Gospel,” etc., and immediately reads the Gospel. (Kathismata and Sessional Hymns there are none.) After the Gospel: Psalm 50 [Ps. 50], and immediately the canon from the Triodion is sung. (The litany: “Save, O God,” etc., is not said.) The rest in the usual manner, as on Monday. At the end of Matins: “It is good to give praise.” Troparion: “When the glorious disciples,” etc. Litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc. After the litany immediately the First Hour from “Come, let us worship,” wherein we say the troparion: “When the glorious disciples.” “Glory,” and “Now and”: Troparion of the prophecy from the Triodion: “He Who was hanged for the sake of mankind,” etc. After this: the Prokimenon, the Old Testament reading (*Paroemia*), and immediately the Prokimenon. And so: “Order my steps.” “Let my mouth be filled.” After the Trisagion: the kontakion of the Triodion: “Having taken the bread,” etc. And the dismissal.

---


Note: Since the prostrations already cease on Wednesday at the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts, therefore this Matins is without prostrations.

(γ) Order of Matins on Holy Great Friday.[^140] This Matins is small. It begins from: “Blessed is our God.” After this: “O Heavenly King.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times). “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice), etc., in the usual manner. “Glory to the holy,” etc. The Six Psalms. The litany of peace. Instead of “God is the Lord,” there is sung: “Alleluia” (thrice). Troparion: “When the glorious disciples,” and that thrice. During this the Priest enters into the holy sanctuary, and vests in the phelonion, and having opened the royal doors, censes the Holy Table, and upon the conclusion of the troparion the chanter sings: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (twice), “To Thee, O Lord,” “Amen,” whereunto the Priest proclaims: “That we may be accounted worthy,” etc., “Peace be to all,” etc., “Wisdom, let us attend, let us hear the holy Gospel,” etc., and reads the holy Gospel at the royal doors. The choir before the Gospel saith: “Glory to Thy passions, O Lord,” and after the Gospel: “Glory to Thy long-suffering, O Lord.” After the Gospel the Priest shuts the royal doors, and the chanter sings 3 antiphons from the Triodion. After the 3rd antiphon, the Sessional Hymn; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): its kontakion; “Now and”: the very same. After this the Priest opens again the royal doors, censes, and again saith: “That we may be accounted worthy,” and the rest—as before. After the Gospel he shuts the royal doors. As after the 1st, so also after the 2nd, 3rd, 4th, and after the 5th Gospel (there are therefore then 12 Gospels) there are three antiphons and one Sessional Hymn. After the 6th Gospel there are the Beati-

=== LEAF p132 ===
[Book Page 108]

tudes (*Blazhenny*), which are read, and after which the Priest from the sanctuary proclaims: “Let us attend, peace be to all, wisdom, let us attend,” and the Prokimenon is sung. After the Prokimenon: “That we may be accounted worthy,” etc., and the 7th Gospel is read. After the 7th Gospel Psalm 50 [Ps. 50], “Have mercy on me, O God,” is read, after which immediately: “That we may be accounted worthy,” and the 8th Gospel is read. (Here the censing ceases.) After the 8th Gospel: the Three-Ode Canon (*Triodion*). In the Three-Ode Canon before the 9th ode of the canon: “We praise, we bless,” etc., and the Priest proclaims: “The Theotokos and Mother of Light,” etc., whereunto “More honorable than the Cherubim” (*Честнѣйшую*) is not sung, but the Heirmos. After the 9th ode there is a litany, the Exaposteilarion. After the Exaposteilarion: “That we may be accounted worthy,” etc., and the 9th Gospel is read; and after the 9th Gospel, the stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*). After this, the 10th Gospel. After the 10th Gospel immediately the Priest proclaims: “Glory to God Who hath shown us the light,” and the Small Doxology is read. After this the Priest comes forth in the epitrachelion before the royal doors and chants the litany: “Let us complete.” After the litany: “That we may be accounted worthy,” and the 11th Gospel is read, and the stichera at the aposticha with their refrains. After the aposticha, the 12th Gospel. After the 12th Gospel: “It is good to give praise,” etc. (twice). Further: The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. Troparion: “Thou hast redeemed us.” After this the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” before the royal doors. The small dismissal. The Priest: “Wisdom”; the choir: “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc. — (The First Hour is not joined unto this Matins.)

§. 58.

Order of Matins on Holy Great Saturday.

This Matins is great with stases (*statii*). The Priest in a black epitrachelion makes the usual beginning: “Blessed is our God,” “O Heavenly King,” and the rest in the usual manner. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times), and immediately the Priest proclaims: “Glory to the holy,” etc. (From “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) [thrice] even unto “Glory to the holy,” the psalms, troparia, and litany found there are omitted.) After “Glory to the holy,” there is said: “Glory to God in the highest,” etc. (thrice), and “O Lord, open Thou my lips,” etc. (twice). The Six Psalms. The litany of peace. At “God is the Lord,” the troparion: “The noble Joseph” (*Благообразный Іѡсифъ*); “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): “When Thou didst descend” (*Егда снизшелъ еси*); “Now and”: “Unto the myrrh-bearing women.” After this the small litany, and there is sung: “Blessed are the undefiled” (Kathisma 17) with refrains in 3 stases, and that in such wise that the chanter begins to sing the refrains,[^141] while the Priest, vested in

=== LEAF p133 ===
[Book Page 109]

the phelonion before the holy tomb, sings the troparia. The 1st and 2nd stasis is sung in Tone 5, and the 3rd in Tone 3. After the 1st and 2nd stasis there is the small litany. After the 3rd stasis there are the troparia after the Evlogitaria (*Neporochny*): “The angelic host.” The small litany and the Sessional Hymn. After this Psalm 50 [Ps. 50], and immediately the canon: “Unto Him Who of old drowned,” wherein before the 9th ode at the exclamation of the Priest: “The Theotokos and Mother of Light,” there is sung: “Weep not for Me, O Mother.” After the 9th ode, instead of the Exaposteilarion, there is sung: “Holy is the Lord our God” (*Святъ Господь Богъ нашъ*) (thrice). Stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*) 4. After this the Priest vests in the phelonion and in all the priestly vestments, and from the sanctuary comes forth before the royal doors onto the ambo, proclaiming: “Glory to God Who hath shown us the light.” Whereunto the Great Doxology is sung. — After the Doxology, the Trisagion. After the Trisagion: “Come, let us bless Joseph.” During which the Priest enters into the holy sanctuary, and with the Gospel makes the entrance around the Holy Table, candle-bearers going before, and coming before the royal doors, proclaims: “Wisdom, let us attend,”[^142] while the choir sings: “The noble Joseph,” and “He Who holds the ends [of the earth].” During this the Priest goes unto the high place, and there proclaims: “Let us attend, peace be to all, wisdom, let us attend,” and they sing the Prokimenon: “Arise, O Lord,” etc. The reading from the Prophecy of Ezekiel. After the Old Testament reading (*Paroemia*), the Prokimenon, and the reading from the Epistle of the holy Apostle to the Corinthians, pericope 133. After the Epistle, the Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 114. Then the litanies: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and “Let us complete,” etc. The great dismissal. (After the dismissal there takes place the kissing of the Shroud [*Plashchanytsia*], when the chanters sing the stichera in Tone 5: “Come, let us bless Joseph.”)

§. 59.

Order of Matins Services in Bright Week.

(α) Order of Matins on the Sunday of Pascha. This Matins is without the Great Doxology, instead whereof the “Paschal Stichera” are sung. It is celebrated thus: The Priest, vested in all the priestly vestments, having taken the holy cross, opens the royal doors, and goes outside of the church, and makes a circuit of the entire church, candle-bearers going before, while singing: “Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior,” etc. After this, when all have stood before the closed church doors, the Priest, holding the cross in his hands, proclaims: “Glory to the holy,” etc. After this he sings: “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) once, and the choir twice. After

=== LEAF p134 ===
[Book Page 110]

this the Priest sings the verses: “Let God arise,” and the rest, and unto each the choir sings the troparion: “Christ is risen,” in a higher voice, and repeats the words: “And unto those in the tombs bestowing life”—when he opens with the holy cross the church doors, and all enter into the church, singing: “And unto those in the tombs bestowing life.” When the Priest hath entered into the holy sanctuary, he there at the Holy Table chants the litany: “In peace let us pray to the Lord,” and there follows the Canon of St. John of Damascus on 16 (in our cathedral church on 6). After all the odes there are small litanies, when the Priest censes the Holy Table, the icons, and makes a circuit with the censer among the people, and censes the people also, greeting each person: “Christ is risen.” The exclamation is said by the Priest within the sanctuary. At the 9th ode the refrain: “The angel cried.” Exaposteilarion: “Having fallen asleep in the flesh,” etc. (thrice). After the canon they sing: “Let every breath” (*Всякое дыханіе*) in Tone 1, and there follow 4 stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*), and the Paschal Stichera: “Today a sacred Pascha,” with their refrains. When the stichera begin to be sung, the Priest comes forth with the Gospel and with the cross onto the ambo, and the people kiss the cross, the Gospel, the *Artos*, etc. These stichera conclude with: “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) (thrice). After this the litanies: “Have mercy on us, O God,” “Let us complete,” and so the Paschal dismissal with the cross.[^143] Before the blessing the Priest, having turned unto the people, exalts the cross, saying thrice: “Christ is risen,” whereunto the people answer: “Truly He is risen,” and only after this doth he give the blessing with the cross.

(β) Order of Sunday Matins, if on any day in Bright Week there fall a Saint with the Polyeleos. This Matins is great, yet in such wise that instead of the Great Doxology the Paschal Stichera are sung. The Priest begins from: “Glory to the holy.” Immediately: “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) (thrice) and with the verses: “Let God arise.” “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): Troparion to the Saint; “Now and”: Theotokion. After this the litany: “In peace let us pray to the Lord.” After the litany (“God is the Lord” (*Богъ Господь*) is not taken) the Sessional Hymns to the Saint without kathismata. The Polyeleos. The Megalynarion. The 3rd Sessional Hymn to the Saint. Gradual Hymns (*Stepenny*): “From my youth,” etc. Prokimenon to the Saint and the rest, as at other times. The Gospel to the Saint; after the Gospel: “Having seen the Resurrection of Christ,” etc. (thrice). After this (Psalm 50 [Ps. 50] is not taken) “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): “Through the prayers of the Saint,” etc.; “Now and”: “Through the prayers of the Theotokos,” etc.; unto the refrain: “Have mercy on me, O God,” etc., the sticheron to the Saint. And immediately the Priest saith the litany: “Save, O God,” etc., with the excla-

=== LEAF p135 ===
[Book Page 111]

mation. The Canon of Pascha on 8, and to the Saint on 6. The Katavasia is the Heirmos: “The Day of Resurrection,” etc. After the 3rd ode the kontakion: “Though Thou didst descend into the grave,” etc., and the ikos. After the 6th ode the kontakion and ikos to the Saint. At the 9th ode (instead of “More honorable than the Cherubim” (*Честнѣйшую*)) there is sung: “The angel cried.” After the 9th ode the Exaposteilarion: “Having fallen asleep in the flesh.” “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): to the Saint; “Now and”: “Having fallen asleep in the flesh.” At the Praises (*Ainoi*) the Sunday stichera 3, to the Saint 3, and the Paschal Stichera with the refrains (instead of the Doxology), during which there takes place the kissing of the cross and of the Gospel. “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): to the Saint; “Now and”: “The Day of Resurrection,” and immediately: “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) (thrice). “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): Troparion to the Saint; “Now and”: Theotokion. After this the litanies: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and “Let us complete,” and the Paschal dismissal with the cross, in which the Saint also is commemorated.

Notes: (α) As regards Sunday Matins services from Thomas Sunday unto the Ascension of Christ, concerning these it must be noted that they begin from: “Glory to the holy.” — After this there is sung: “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) (thrice). (The verses are omitted.) And immediately: “Glory to God in the highest” (*Слава во вышнихъ Богу*) (thrice), etc. The usual Six Psalms. After the litany of peace the troparia at “God is the Lord.” The kathismata with the sessional hymns. (On Thomas Sunday the Polyeleos and Megalynarion of the feast.) The troparia after the Evlogitaria (*Neporochny*). The Hypakoe of the tone. The Gradual Hymns (*Stepenny*). The Prokimenon. The Gospel. “Having seen the Resurrection of Christ” (*Воскресеніе Христово*) (thrice), etc., in order. The prescribed canon. The Katavasia of Pascha. At the 9th ode the Priest proclaims: “The Theotokos and Mother of Light,” and the chanter sings: “The angel cried.” Stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*), after which there is the Great Doxology, the Trisagion, etc. 2 litanies, and the Paschal dismissal with the cross.

(β) Concerning daily Matins services, we note that these Matins services begin and end in the same manner as the Sunday ones, only it must be noted that after the 2nd Kathisma and sessional hymn the Priest comes forth before the royal doors and reads: “Having seen the Resurrection of Christ” (*Воскресеніе Христово*) once. And immediately Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. After this the appointed canon (of the day and of the Saint). The Katavasia of the order, only after the 3rd, 6th, 8th, and 9th ode. At the 9th ode there is sung: “More honorable than the Cherubim.” The stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*) are always from the Pentecostarion (*Flowery Triodion*). The Small Doxology, after which the Priest comes forth before the royal doors and proclaims aloud the litany: “Let us complete.” Stichera at the aposticha from the Pentecostarion. After: “It is good to give praise,” the troparion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast of Pascha. After this the Priest again comes forth before the royal doors and chants the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God.” The dismissal without the cross: “He Who rose from the dead.”

=== LEAF p136 ===
[Book Page 112]

§. 60.

Order of Matins on the Exaltation of the Precious Cross.

This Matins is great festal with the Polyeleos, with the Gospel (after which is also “Having seen the Resurrection of Christ” (*Воскресеніе Христово*)), and with the Great Doxology. Here it is only needful to note that when the chanters after the Great Doxology sing the Trisagion, the Priest, vested in all the priestly vestments, goes unto the Holy Table, censes the precious cross displayed there, and carries it around the Holy Table, taking it upon his head, before the royal doors, candle-bearers going before, and standing there, awaits the end of the Trisagion. Upon the conclusion thereof he proclaims: “Wisdom, let us attend,” whereunto the choir sings the troparion: “Save, O Lord, Thy people,” etc. After this the Priest goes unto the appointed table, and places upon it the precious cross, and censes it from four sides, and having bowed unto it, takes it into his hands, and stands before the table, facing the east, and proclaims the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc., whereunto the choir sings: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times) in a lower and higher voice, during which singing the Priest lowers and exalts the holy cross, blessing the people therewith. — After this he withdraws unto the right side, facing north, and continues the litany: “Again we pray for our most devout Sovereign,” etc., whereunto the choir again sings 12 times: “Lord, have mercy,” as before, during which the Priest again lowers and exalts the precious cross; further he goes unto the 3rd side, and turning unto the west, continues the litany: “Again we pray for our God-loving Bishop,” etc., whereunto the choir again sings: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) 12 times, higher and lower, while the Priest during the very singing lowers and exalts the holy cross, and makes a blessing therewith. Finally he goes unto the 4th side, facing south, and chants: “Again we pray for the people here present,” etc., and the choir again sings: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times), and all takes place as before. After this the Priest, standing before the table, chants the exclamation, upon the conclusion of which he places the precious cross upon the Tetrapod, and there is sung: “Thy Cross do we adore, O Master,” (thrice) with prostrations. After this the Priest kisses the holy cross, and withdraws unto the Holy Table, and the people during this kiss the holy cross, and the stichera of the holy cross are sung: “Come, ye faithful,” etc., until the kissing is completed. After this the Priest, standing at the Holy Table, chants the litany: “Let us complete,” etc. The great dismissal.

Note: From this Matins the Matins on the “Sunday of the Adoration of the Cross” differs in the following: After the Great Doxology, when the choir sings the “Trisagion,” the Priest goes unto the Holy Ta-

=== LEAF p137 ===
[Book Page 113]

ble, and having censed the cross there, carries it around the Holy Table unto the royal doors, and here proclaims: “Wisdom, let us attend,” whereunto the choir immediately sings: “Save, O Lord, Thy people,” etc. During this singing the Priest places the holy cross upon the prepared table, and there is sung: “Thy Cross do we adore, O Master,” (thrice) with prostrations. Upon the conclusion of this singing the Priest, having kissed the holy cross, leaves the Tetrapod and goes unto the Holy Table, whereupon the people kiss the cross. During this time the choir sings: “Come, ye faithful,” etc. Upon the conclusion of this the Priest at the Holy Table saith the litanies: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and “Let us complete,” etc. The great dismissal.

### ARTICLE V.

§. 61.

Concerning the Canonical Hours and the Typika [*Obidnytsia*].

The ordinary Hours are: (α) simple and (β) royal. The simple are celebrated daily, that is, on all feasts and simple days; while the royal are on the Eve of the Nativity of Jesus Christ and of Theophany, likewise also on Great Friday.

Of extraordinary Hours we cite: (α) the Hours in the Holy Great Fast; (β) the Hours in Bright Week.

The Typika [*Obidnytsia*] is celebrated either separately, when there is no Holy Liturgy; when, however, there is a Holy Liturgy, it is then combined with the Ninth Hour, and then only certain prayers are taken from it for completion.

### 1) Ordinary Hours.

§. 62.

Order of Simple Hours with the Typika [*Obidnytsia*].

The First Hour. If the First Hour is combined with Matins, then upon the conclusion of Matins the chanter immediately begins: “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). If, however, it is celebrated separately without Matins, then the Priest in the epitrachelion before the royal doors proclaims aloud: “Blessed is our God.” “O Heavenly King.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times). “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). (The Priest enters into the holy sanctuary.) The chanter begins to read Psalms: 5, 89, and 100 [Pss. 5, 89, 100]. “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). “Glory,” and “Now and”: Troparion

=== LEAF p138 ===
[Book Page 114]

of the Sunday, or of the feast, or of the Saint, if there be a great or middle Saint; if, however, there be a small Saint, then “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): Troparion of the day; “Now and”: “What shall we call thee?” After this: “Order my steps.” “Let my mouth be filled.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. The kontakion of the Sunday, or to the Saint, or of the feast. If, however, there be a small Saint, then the kontakion of the day is taken. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). During this the Priest comes forth and reads before the royal doors the prayer: “Thou Who at all times.” After this the chanter saith: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and”: “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc., and “In the name of the Lord bless, Father”; whereunto the Priest proclaims: “God be bountiful unto us,” etc., and reads the prayer: “O Christ, the true Light,” etc. After this prayer there immediately begins:

The Third Hour,[^144] in such wise that the chanter saith: “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). Psalms: 16, 24, 50 [Pss. 16, 24, 50]. “Glory,” and “Now and.” (The Priest enters into the sanctuary.) “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). Troparion of the Sunday, of the feast, or to the great Saint. “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): Troparion to the small Saint; “Now and”: “O Theotokos, thou art the vine”; and there is read: “Blessed is the Lord God,” etc. After this: The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. The kontakion of the Sunday, of the feast, or to the great Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Kontakion to the small Saint. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). After this the Priest comes forth and reads the prayer: “Thou Who at all times,” etc. After this the chanter saith: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and”: “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc., and “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” Whereunto the Priest proclaims: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and saith the prayer: “O Master God.” Upon the conclusion of the 3rd Hour there begins:

The Sixth Hour, and that in such wise that the chanter saith: “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). (The Priest enters into the sanctuary.) Psalms: 53, 54, 90 [Pss. 53, 54, 90]. 

=== LEAF p139 ===
[Book Page 115]

“Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): Troparion of the temple; “Now and”: “Seeing that we have not”; and there is read: “Let Thy compassions quickly go before us,” etc. The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. The kontakion of the temple. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). And the Priest comes forth and reads: “Thou Who at all times.” After this the chanter: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and”: “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc., “In the name of the Lord,” etc., and the Priest proclaims: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and saith the prayer: “O Lord and God of hosts.” Upon the conclusion of the 6th Hour there begins:

The Ninth Hour,[^145] and that in such wise that the chanter saith: “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). (The Priest enters into the sanctuary.) Psalms: 83, 84, 85 [Pss. 83, 84, 85].

“Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). Kathisma of the day. “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): Troparion, as at the 1st Hour; “Now and”: “He Who for our sake,” etc., and there is read: “Deliver us not up.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. The kontakion, as at the 1st Hour. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). After this the Priest comes forth from the sanctuary and reads: “Thou Who at all times,” etc. After the prayer the chanter saith: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc., “In the name of the Lord,” etc., and the Priest, if all the Hours are celebrated together, proclaims: “Through the prayers,” etc. (if, however, they are not: “God be bountiful unto us,”[^146] etc.), and reads the prayer: “O Master, Lord Jesus Christ,” etc., and the Priest enters into the holy sanctuary. And he makes a dismissal only then if he wishes to celebrate the Typika [*Obidnytsia*] separately and apart.

The Typika [*Obidnytsia*] is celebrated then if there will be no Liturgy, and it is celebrated thus: The Priest having given the blessing, the chanter makes the usual beginning and reads the Psalms with the refrain: “Bless the Lord, O my soul,[^147] blessed art Thou, O Lord.” “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): “Praise the Lord, O my soul” (*Хвалѝ, дꙋшѐ моя̀, Го́спода...*); “Now and”: “Only-begotten Son” (*Е҆диноро́дный Сы́не...*) (is sung). The Beatitudes (*Blazhenny*). Stichera at the Beatitudes from the Octoechos according to the 8 tones. After this the Priest, having put on the phelonion, opens the royal doors and proclaims: “Let us attend, peace be to all, wisdom, let us attend,” and the chanter sings the Prokimenon of the day. After this follows: the Epistle (*Apostol*). During the reading of the Epistle the Priest censes the sanctuary, and after it saith: “Peace be to thee, wisdom, let us attend,” and immediately: “Wisdom, let us attend,” etc., and reads the holy Gospel. After the Gospel he shuts the royal doors. After this the choir sings: “Remember us, O Lord.” “The heavenly choir.” The Creed (*Viruyu*). “Pardon, remit.” “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. The kontakion of the temple, of the day, and to the Saint. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). “Glory,” and “Now and”: “More honorable than the Cherubim.” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father”; whereunto the Priest, having come in the epitrachelion before the royal doors, saith: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and reads the prayer: “All-Holy Trinity.” After this the chanter sings: “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (*Буди имя Господне*) (thrice). “I will bless the Lord.” “It is meet

=== LEAF p140 ===
[Book Page 116]

and right... unto “the Mother of our God.” After this: “Wisdom.” “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc. The small dismissal.

If, however, the Typika [*Obidnytsia*] is celebrated with the Ninth Hour before the Holy Liturgy, then all things are omitted from the Typika that will be taken at the Liturgy, namely: “Only-begotten Son.” The Prokimenon. The Epistle (*Apostol*). The Gospel. The Creed (*Viruyu*). “Blessed be the name of the Lord.” “I will bless the Lord,” etc., and the remaining parts are joined unto the Ninth Hour for completion.

§. 63.

Order of the Royal Hours.

They are celebrated in the same manner as the ordinary Hours. — Here it is only needful to note that at all the Hours, instead of the usual psalms, other psalms are prescribed. Besides this, after the troparia 3 stichera are sung with “Glory,” and “Now and.” After this the Priest from the sanctuary saith: “Let us attend, peace be to all, wisdom, let us attend.” Whereunto the chanter sings the Prokimenon. After this there is one reading from the Old and one from the New Testament. After the readings the Priest opens the royal doors, proclaiming: “Wisdom, let us attend,” etc., and at the royal doors reads the Gospel. After the Gospel he shuts the royal doors. The chanter reads: “Order my steps” (*Стопы̀ моя̀ напра́ви...*) at the 1st, “Blessed is the Lord God” (*Господь Богъ благословенъ*) at the 3rd, “Let Thy compassions quickly go before us” at the 6th, and “Deliver us not up” (*Не предаждь насъ*) at the 9th Hour. All the rest are concluded in order, as at the simple Hours, that is, when “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) is said 40 times, the Priest comes forth before the royal doors in the epitrachelion, and there saith all things as at other times. After the prayer of the Ninth Hour there are also taken from the Typika [*Obidnytsia*] certain parts for completion, and that: (α) On the Eve of the Nativity and of Theophany: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): “Praise the Lord, O my soul.” “Now and”: “Only-begotten Son.” After this: “In Thy kingdom.” “Glory,” and “Now and”: “Remember us.” “The heavenly choir.” The Creed (*Viruyu*). “Pardon, remit.” “Our Father.” The kontakion of the feast. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times), and “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., when the Priest comes forth before the royal doors, and there reads the prayer: “All-Holy Trinity.” After this: “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (*Буди имя Господне*) (thrice). “I will bless the Lord at all times.” “It is meet and right,” and the dismissal. (If, however, there is a Liturgy, then after the prayer: “All-Holy Trinity,” there is immediately the dismissal. “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (*Буди имя Господне*) [thrice] and “I will bless the Lord at all times” (*Благословлю Господа*) are taken at the end of the Liturgy.)

(β) On Great Friday, however, from the Typika [*Obidnytsia*] there is taken: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” “Glory,” and “Now and”: “Praise the Lord, O my soul.” “In Thy kingdom 

=== LEAF p141 ===
[Book Page 117]

remember us, O Lord.” “Glory,” and “Now and”: “Remember us, O Lord.” “The heavenly choir.” The Creed (*Viruyu*). “Pardon, remit.” “Our Father.” The kontakion. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). “Glory,” and “Now and”: “More honorable than the Cherubim” (*Честнѣйшую*); and thus “Through the prayers [of our holy Fathers],” etc. The Priest, standing before the royal doors, immediately saith the prayer: “All-Holy Trinity.” After this: “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (*Буди имя Господне*) (thrice). “I will bless the Lord at all times.” “It is meet and right,” and the dismissal.

---


### 2) Extraordinary Hours.

§. 64.

The Hours in the Great Fast.

The First Hour is celebrated for the most part with Matins, and begins only from: “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice). (The Priest enters into the sanctuary.)[^148] The chanter reads the three appointed Psalms. “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). Kathisma of the day.[^149] Instead of the troparion there is said: “In the morning hear my voice, O my King and my God.” “Glory,” and “Now and”: “What shall we call thee, O full of grace?” “Order my steps” (*Стопы̀ моя̀ напра́ви...*)[^150] “Let my mouth be filled.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. Instead of the kontakion there is taken: “Let Thy compassions quickly go before us.” “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): “Wisdom.” “Now and”: “All-holy [Theotokos].” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). And the Priest, having come forth from the sanctuary, saith the prayer: “Thou Who at all times.” After this the chanter saith: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and”: “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc., “In the name of the Lord,” etc., and the Priest proclaims: “God be bountiful unto us, and bless us, and show the light of His countenance upon us, and have mercy on us,” and makes 3 prostrations. After this the chanter: The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times). Whereunto the Priest saith the prayer: “O Christ, the true Light.” Upon the conclusion of which the chanter sings the kontakion of the Annunciation: “To thee, the champion leader,” etc., and if this Hour is combined with Matins, he makes the smallest dismissal.

The Third Hour, if it is celebrated together with the 1st Hour, begins from: “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice), when the Priest departs from the royal

=== LEAF p142 ===
[Book Page 118]

doors. If, however, it is celebrated separately, then from: “Blessed is our God.” “O Heavenly King.” “Our Father,” etc. After this, following “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice), the appointed Psalms. “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). Kathisma of the day. After the kathisma the troparion of the Fast: “O Lord, Who at the third hour didst send down Thy Most Holy Spirit upon Thine Apostles, take Him not away from us, O Good One, but renew Him in us who pray unto Thee.” “Glory,” and “Now and”: “O Theotokos, thou art the true vine”; and there is read: “Blessed is the Lord God,” etc. After this: The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. Instead of the kontakion there is sung the troparion of the Fast: “Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God,” with “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). And the Priest, having come forth from the sanctuary before the royal doors, saith the prayer: “Thou Who at all times.” After this the chanter: “Glory,” and “Now and”: “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc., “In the name of the Lord bless, Father,” and the Priest proclaims: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and makes 3 prostrations. After this: The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times), and the Priest reads the prayer: “O Master God,” etc., and immediately:

The Sixth Hour from “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice), when the Priest enters into the holy sanctuary. The chanter reads the three psalms. “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). Kathisma of the day. Troparion of the Fast: “Thou Who on the sixth day and hour didst nail to the Cross the sin which Adam daringly committed in paradise,” etc. “Glory,” and “Now and”: “Seeing that we have not boldness,” etc. “Let Thy compassions quickly go before us” is read. After this: The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. Instead of the kontakion the troparion of the Fast: “Thou hast wrought salvation,” with “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times), when the Priest comes forth from the sanctuary and reads the prayer: “Thou Who at all times.” “Glory,” and “Now and.” “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc., “In the name of the Lord bless, Father,” whereunto the Priest proclaims: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and makes 3 prostrations. After this: The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times), and the prayer is said by the Priest: “O God and Lord of hosts,” and so forth.

The Ninth Hour, if it is celebrated together with the 6th Hour, begins with: “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice); if, however, this Hour is celebrated separately, then after “Blessed is our God” (*Благословенъ Богъ нашъ*) there is said also “O Heavenly King,” etc., as usual. Three psalms. “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). Kathisma of the day. Troparion: “Thou Who at the ninth hour for our sake didst taste of death in the flesh,” etc. “Glory,” and “Now and”: “He Who for our sake,” etc. “Deliver us not up” (*Не предаждь насъ*) is read. After this: The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. Instead of the kontakion the troparion of the Fast: “When the thief beheld,” with “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). And the Priest, having come forth, on the ambo saith the prayer: “Thou Who at all times.” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice). “Glory,” and “Now and”: “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc., “In the name of the Lord bless, Father,” and so forth, whereunto the Priest before the royal doors saith: “God be bountiful unto us,” etc., if this Hour is celebrated separately; if, however, not:

=== LEAF p143 ===
[Book Page 119]

then: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and he makes three prostrations,[^151] and there begins:

The Typika [*Obidnytsia*]. Here it is needful to note that it is celebrated either together with Vespers, or also with the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts. (If there be any feast, then first we say Ps. 102 [Ps. 102]. “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): “Praise the Lord, O my soul.” “Now and”: “Only-begotten Son.”) If it is celebrated together with Vespers, then the Priest stands before the royal doors, and the choir sings: “In Thy kingdom.” (If there be any feast, then “In Thy kingdom” is only read.) After this they sing: “Remember us, O Lord,” and all make a bow. “Remember us, O Master,” and likewise they make a bow. Finally: “Remember us, O Holy One,” and they make a bow. After this there is read: “The choir of the holy Apostles.” The Creed (*Viruyu*). “Pardon, remit.” “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. The kontakion of the temple, of the day, and to the Saint. “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): “With the Saints,” “Now and”: “O Protection of Christians.” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc. “In the name of the Lord,” etc., and the Priest, standing before the royal doors, proclaims: “Through the prayers of the holy Fathers,” and makes 3 prostrations. The Trisagion. “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times), and then the prayer of the 9th Hour: “O Lord Jesus Christ,”[^152] etc. The smallest dismissal. The remaining parts of the Typika [*Obidnytsia*] are taken at the end of Vespers. (See p. 74.)

If, however, the Typika [*Obidnytsia*] is joined not only with Vespers, but also with the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts, then it is celebrated as was said above, only that it is needful to note that in this case, of the remaining parts, the prayer: “All-Holy Trinity” (*Всесвятая Троице*) is omitted, and the other parts are taken at the end of the Liturgy.

§. 65.

The Hours of Bright Week.

They are celebrated in such wise as they are arranged in the *Molitvoslov* on pages 478–480, that is: after “Blessed is our God” (*Благословенъ Богъ нашъ*) there is taken: “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) (thrice). “Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ” (*Воскресе́нїе Хрїсто́во*) (thrice). “They who preceded the dawn.” “Though Thou didst descend into the tomb.” “In the tomb

=== LEAF p144 ===
[Book Page 120]

bodily.” “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “More honorable than the Cherubim.” “In the name of the Lord.” The Priest: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers.” “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) (thrice) and the dismissal. It is needful to note this, that all 4 Hours are concluded uniformly. — The Typika [*Obidnytsia*] is also celebrated as it is in the *Molitvoslov*, p. 480. That is, if there will be no Liturgy: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) (thrice). “Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ” (*Воскресе́нїе Хрїсто́во*) (once). “They who preceded the dawn” (once). “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): “Though Thou didst descend into the tomb.” “Now and”: “Only-begotten Son.” After this: The Beatitudes (*Blazhenny*), and that: from the Canon, Odes 3 and 6. The Prokimenon. The Epistle (*Apostol*), the Gospel. “Remember us, O Lord.” The Creed (*Viruyu*). “Pardon, remit.” “Our Father.” Kontakion: “Though Thou didst descend into the tomb.” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (40 times). “All-Holy Trinity.” “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) (thrice). Psalm 33 [Ps. 33]: “I will bless the Lord,” and the Dismissal.

§. 66.

Concerning the All-Night Vigil.

The All-Night Vigil consists of Great Vespers with Litiya and of Matins with the 1st Hour,—or of Great Compline with Litiya and of Matins with the 1st Hour. We do not intend to describe the order of this divine service here yet again, for each one will already know it from the things spoken.
