holiness becomes Thy house, O Lord, unto length of days.” After this he places the Diskos over the Chalice and covers it with the larger veil, saying: “Let us pray to the Lord.” “Lord, have mercy,” and the prayer: “Thy virtue, O Christ, hath covered the heavens, and the earth is full of Thy praise.” At the last, he covers both with the Aer and saith: “Let us pray to the Lord.” “Lord, have mercy,” and the prayer: “Shelter us, O God, under the shadow of Thy wings, and drive away from us every enemy and adversary; make our life peaceful, O Lord; have mercy on us and on Thy world, and save our souls, for Thou art good and the Lover of mankind.” After this, having taken the censer, he censes the oblation thrice and saith at each censing: “Blessed is our God Who was thus well pleased, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.” And then: “For the precious gifts offered, let us pray to the Lord.” “Lord, have mercy.” After this, the priest with outstretched hands saith the prayer: “O God, our God, Who didst send forth the Heavenly Bread, the food of the whole world, our Lord and God Jesus Christ, as a Savior and Redeemer and Benefactor, blessing and sanctifying us: do Thou Thyself bless † (he blesses crosswise) this oblation, and receive it upon Thy most heavenly altar. Remember, as Thou art good and the Lover of mankind, those who have offered it and those for whom they have offered it; and preserve us uncondemned in the sacred celebration of Thy Divine Mysteries; for hallowed and glorified is Thy most honorable and majestic name, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.”

After this the priest makes the Dismissal, saying: “Glory be to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory be to Thee!” Glory... Now and forever... Lord, have mercy (thrice). Lord, bless. And if it be Sunday, he saith: “He Who rose from the dead, Christ our true God,” etc. On another day: “Christ our true God, through the prayers of His most pure Mother, by the power of the precious and life-giving Cross, of the holy, glorious, and all-praised Apostles, and of our Father among the Saints John Chrysostom, Archbishop of Constantinople, of our Venerable and God-bearing Fathers, and of all the Saints, have mercy on us and save us, for He is good and the Lover of mankind.” For the departed, however, this Dismissal: “Christ our true God, Who hath dominion over the living and the dead, through the prayers of His most pure Mother, of the holy, glorious, and all-praised Apostles, and of our Father among the Saints John Chrysostom, Archbishop of Constantinople, of our Venerable and God-bearing Fathers, and of all the Saints: establish the soul of His servant departed from us (or the soul of His handmaid departed from us, or the souls of His servants

=== LEAF p206 ===
[Book Page 182]

or handmaids departed from us: [N.]), in the tents of the righteous, and give it rest in the bosom of Abraham, and number it among the righteous, and have mercy on us and save us, for He is good and the Lover of mankind. Amen.” If, however, it be a Feast and hath a special Dismissal, then the Dismissal of the Feast is said; but one must always insert the Saint whose temple it is.[^186]

After the Dismissal, the priest departs unto the holy Altar-Table and, censing the Altar-Table, saith: “In the tomb bodily, in hades with Thy soul as God, in paradise with the thief, and on the throne wast Thou with the Father and the Spirit, O Christ, filling all things, Thyself uncircumscribed.” And having censed the holy Altar-Table round about crosswise, he goes forth by the northern door onto the ambo, because the Royal Doors are closed, reciting Psalm 50, and censes first from the ambo the stationary icons [of the iconostasis], and thereafter the entire church and the people, and enters into the holy Sanctuary [Altar], and laying aside the censer by the holy Altar-Table, begins the Divine Liturgy.

§. 140.

(b) The Order of the Liturgy of the Catechumens.

Upon the conclusion of the Proskomedia, the priest, having censed the church, stands before the holy Altar-Table with the deacons, and having bowed reverently and signed themselves with their right hand, they say secretly: “Blessed is our God.” After this, having joined hands at their breast, they say: “Heavenly King,” etc. And signing themselves again, they say: “Glory to God in the highest,” and that twice; after this: “O Lord, open Thou my lips,” once; and they kiss the holy Altar-Table and the Cross. The deacon, after this having received a blessing from the priest, goes forth from the holy Sanctuary by the northern door, holding the Orarion with three fingers of his hand, and standing upon the ambo directly before the holy Royal Doors, and having bowed with reverence, begins with a loud voice: “Bless, Master.” And the priest, having taken

=== LEAF p207 ===
[Book Page 183]

the holy Gospel Book into his hands, raises it crosswise, saying: “Blessed is the kingdom...”[^187] etc., unto which the choir answers: “Amen.” After this the priest kisses the Gospel Book and places it upon its customary place; the deacon then begins to chant the Litany of Peace aloud: “In peace let us pray to the Lord.” (If there is no deacon, the priest himself saith this litany.) Unto each petition the choir answers: “Lord, have mercy.” It is to be noted here that in this litany, after the petition: “For them that travel by sea or by land...” etc., if the Liturgy is celebrated for a special intention, the appropriate prescribed litanies are added, which are found in the *Sluzhebnik* on page 642; if, however, for the departed, this one: “For the servant of God...” etc., or “For the souls of all.” (This litany for the departed, however, is not added on Sunday, nor on any feast day.) After this: “That we may be delivered...”; this Litany of Peace ends with the exclamation: “For unto Thee is due...” etc., unto which the choir saith: “Amen.”[^188]

After the Litany of Peace the choir begins to sing the Psalms for the Antiphon, namely, when the 1st Antiphon (daily or festal) is sung, the priest saith the 1st prayer secretly: “O Lord our God...” etc. Upon the conclusion of this prayer he saith the Little Litany: “Again and again in peace let us pray to the Lord.”

When the choir sings the second Antiphon, the priest saith the second prayer secretly: “O Lord our God, save Thy people...”; but when the choir begins to sing: “Glory to the Father... Only-begotten Son...” etc., the priest, having signed himself with the cross, with outstretched hands saith secretly likewise this hymn: “Glory to the Father... Only-begotten Son...” It is to be noted that at the word “to become incarnate” the priest bows low. After this prayer the priest saith the Little Litany: “Again and again,” as before. — If there is a deacon, then standing before the Royal Doors during the 1st Antiphon he goes unto the icon of the Savior; thence, when the choir begins to sing the 2nd Antiphon, he goes unto the icon of the Theotokos; and at “Glory... Only-begotten...” he stands before the Royal Doors, which

=== LEAF p208 ===
[Book Page 184]

the subdeacon opens; at the word “to become incarnate” both deacon and subdeacon bow low and go unto the holy Altar-Table.

When the 3rd Antiphon begins to be sung, the priest, standing with the deacon and subdeacon at the holy Altar-Table, first saith the prayer: “Thou Who hast given us these common...” and after this “O Lord our God,” then makes a bow with the deacons and takes into his hands, or gives unto the deacon, the holy Gospel Book and makes the “Little Entrance,”[^189] and that thus: descending from the holy Altar-Table, he goes behind the altar-throne to the right and goes forth through the northern door before the iconostasis onto the ambo, candle-bearers preceding them (if there be such). And the priest holding his hands joined at the breast and having stood before the Royal Doors with the deacons, the deacon asks a blessing from the priest, who blesses the holy entrance with the sign of the cross, saying: “Blessed is the entrance of Thy Saints, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen,” and kisses the holy Gospel Book; the deacon, standing again before the Royal Doors, raises his hands slightly with the holy Gospel Book crosswise and exclaims: “Wisdom! Stand upright!” (*Премꙋ́дрость про́сти!*)[^190] Unto this the choir sings: “Come, let us worship...” etc.; the priest with the deacons enters into the holy Sanctuary [Altar], and the deacon, having bowed, places the holy Gospel Book upon the holy Altar-Table.

After this, the troparia and kontakia are sung according to custom; the priest, standing before the holy Altar-Table, reads the prayer of the Trisagion hymn, having his hands joined at his breast: “O holy God, Who restest in the saints...” etc. Having concluded this prayer, he exclaims: “For holy art Thou, our God, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, always, now and forever.” At the words “Now and forever” the priest turns toward the people, blessing them, and the deacon concludes the exclamation, saying: “And unto the ages of ages.” Unto this the choir, having sung “Amen,” begins to sing the “Trisagion.” The priest with the deacons say also themselves the “Trisagion,” making together three bows before the holy Altar-Table. At “Glory to the Father” they leave the holy Altar-Table and for the last time say the “Trisagion” already at the foot of the holy Altar-Table. After this they depart unto the High Place, but first the deacon and subdeacon change places, passing between the priest and the holy Altar-Table, and in that manner the deacon will be on the left, and the subdeacon on the right side. The departing

=== LEAF p209 ===
[Book Page 185]

priest on the right side departing unto the High Place quietly saith the prayer: “Blessed is He That comes in the name of the Lord; blessed art Thou upon the throne of the glory of Thy kingdom, Who sittest upon the Cherubim, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages.”

Upon the conclusion of the “Trisagion” the deacon exclaims: “Let us attend” (*Во́ньмемъ*); the priest: “Peace be unto all” (*Ми́ръ всѣ̑мъ*); and the subdeacon: “Wisdom! Let us attend!” (*Премꙋ́дрость, про́сти!*)[^191] After this the Prokimenon of the Sunday, of the Feast, or of various intentions is sung. After the Prokimenon the deacon exclaims: “Wisdom!” (*Премꙋ́дрость!*) and the subdeacon or reader saith the title of the holy Apostle (“The Reading from the Acts of the Holy Apostles,” or “The Reading from the Catholic Epistle of Peter,” or “The Reading from the Epistle of the Holy Apostle Paul to the Romans”), whereunto the deacon exclaims: “Let us attend!” (*Во́ньмемъ*) and the subdeacon or reader begins to read the holy Epistle, introducing it with either: “Brethren,” or: “In those days,” or: “Child Timothy,” which are appointed in the Apostle book for the apostrophe [address].[^192]

During the reading of the Epistle, the deacon, having taken the censer and asked a blessing from the priest, censes the holy Altar-Table and the holy Prothesis from the four sides. After the Epistle hath been read, the priest saith: “Peace be unto thee”; and the deacon, standing at the Royal Doors turned toward the people whom he censes, adds: “Wisdom! Let us attend!” (*Премꙋ́дрость, про́сти!*) (If there is no deacon, the priest himself saith it.) The choir unto this answers: “Alleluia,”[^193] during the singing of which the priest quietly saith the prayer: “Shine forth in our hearts, O Master Who lovest mankind, the undefiled light of Thy divine knowledge, and open the eyes of our mind to the understanding of Thy gospel proclamations; implant in us also the fear of Thy blessed commandments, that trampling down all carnal desires, we may enter upon a spiritual manner of living, both thinking and doing all things that are well-pleasing unto Thee. For Thou art the enlightenment of our souls and bodies, O Christ God, and unto Thee do we send up glory, together with Thine unoriginate Father, and Thy Most Holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.” The deacon or the priest then hands over the censer, and goes unto the holy Altar-Table.

After this, both deacons stand by the holy Altar-Table, and upon the conclusion of the “Alleluia” the senior deacon takes the Gospel Book into his hands and

=== LEAF p210 ===
[Book Page 186]

having set it upright upon the holy Altar-Table, exclaims: “Bless, Master, the good tidings of the holy, glorious, and all-praised Apostle and Evangelist, [N.]”; and the priest, standing at the High Place, blesses the deacon, saying with a loud voice: “May God, through the prayers of the holy, glorious, and all-praised Apostle and Evangelist, [N.], give unto thee a word with great power unto the proclamation of the Gospel of His beloved Son, our Lord Jesus Christ.”

While the priest saith this, the deacon goes forth from the holy Sanctuary, namely: the senior deacon holding the Gospel Book in his hands through the door on the right side, and the subdeacon through the door on the left side; and having come onto the ambo at the Royal Doors, they bow and, preceded by candle-bearers, go to the left before the icon of Saint Nicholas, and here the subdeacon exclaims: “Wisdom! Stand upright! Let us hear the Holy Gospel”; and the deacon continues thus: “The Reading from the Holy Gospel according to [N.],” unto which the choir answers: “Glory be to Thee, O Lord.” After this the subdeacon exclaims: “Let us attend!” (*Во́ньмемъ*) and the deacon, with the words: “At that time,” or “The Lord said,” or “The Lord said unto the Jews that came unto Him,” or “The Lord spake this parable,” begins to read the Gospel. After the Gospel hath been read, the choir sings: “Glory be to Thee, O Lord, glory be to Thee!” After this the subdeacon holding the Gospel Book in his hands, with the deacon preceded by candle-bearers, goes upon the ambo unto the Royal Doors, and here the subdeacon hands the Gospel Book opened unto the priest, who during this time comes hither from the High Place, and having kissed the Gospel Book, takes and carries it unto the holy Altar-Table. (If, however, the priest celebrates alone, he himself reads the Holy Gospel at the Royal Doors, signing himself with the cross at the beginning of the reading at “let us attend,” looking toward the people.)

Upon the conclusion of the Gospel the subdeacon goes unto the holy Altar-Table to the priest, and the deacon, standing again in the customary place before the Royal Doors, begins the Insistent Litany: “Let us all say,” etc.; while the priest, standing before the holy Altar-Table, saith secretly this litany with hands joined at his breast. It is to be noted that in this litany the priest may add also a special litany, if he hath a special intention.[^194] At the exclamation of the priest: “For a merciful...” the deacon enters by the right door, while the subdeacon goes forth by the left door and stands in the customary place, and begins the 2nd litany, which is called “For the Catechumens.”

Unto this litany the subdeacon summons the faithful to prayer 

=== LEAF p211 ===
[Book Page 187]

for the Catechumens, saying: “Pray unto the Lord, ye catechumens,” etc. (The priest saith this litany secretly.) When the subdeacon comes unto the words: “That He may reveal unto them the Gospel of righteousness,” then the priest, having kissed the Gospel Book, places it upon the right side. After the exclamation: “That they also with us may glorify Thine all-honorable and majestic name...” etc., the senior deacon—standing beside the priest—turns toward the people and exclaims: “All ye who are catechumens, depart”; and the subdeacon—standing upon the ambo—continues: “Ye catechumens, depart”; and thereafter the senior deacon saith again: “All ye who are catechumens, depart”; and the subdeacon continues: “Let none of the catechumens remain...” etc., and hereby the Liturgy of the Catechumens is concluded. And after this begins the III. Part of the Liturgy: The Liturgy of the Faithful.

---


§. 141.

(c) The Order of the Liturgy of the Faithful.

When the catechumens have been summoned by the deacon’s exclamation to depart the church, the priest unfolds upon the holy Altar-Table the folded Iliton, as it were for a sign that before the eyes of the faithful the greatest mystery instituted by Jesus Christ for our salvation is unveiled and accomplished. And after this the priest reads the 1st Prayer of the Faithful, saying secretly: “We give thanks unto Thee, O Lord God of Hosts...” etc. And while the priest reads the prayer, the deacon exclaims: “Help us, save us, have mercy on us...” etc., unto which the choir answers: “Lord, have mercy.” And immediately before the exclamation the deacon saith: “Wisdom!” (*Премꙋ́дрость!*) and the priest with a loud voice pronounces the exclamation: “For unto Thee is due all glory, honor, and worship...” etc. After this exclamation the deacon saith: “Again and again in peace let us pray to the Lord,” and the priest reads the 2nd Prayer of the Faithful. During the prayer of the priest the subdeacon saith again: “Help us, save us, have mercy on us, and protect us by Thy grace,” unto which the choir sings: “Lord, have mercy.” Before the exclamation: “That being ever guarded under Thy dominion...” etc., the subdeacon saith again: “Wisdom!” (*Премꙋ́дрость!*)[^195] and the priest makes the exclamation, in which at the words: “now and forever” he turns toward the people, blessing them.

After this the subdeacon enters into the holy Sanctuary [Altar], and having opened the Royal Doors, goes unto the priest; the senior deacon, having received the cen-

=== LEAF p212 ===
[Book Page 188]

-ser and a blessing from the priest, descends from the holy Altar-Table, censes the holy Altar-Table round about, likewise also the Table of Preparation [Prothesis], and coming unto the Royal Doors censes thence first the icon of the Savior, thereafter that of the Theotokos, and finally the people. After this he enters unto the Altar-Table, bringing the censer with him. (If there is no deacon, the priest himself censes, reciting Psalm 50.) After this the priest, standing at the holy Altar-Table, saith first the prayer: “None is worthy of them that are bound with carnal lusts and pleasures...” etc., and after this Psalm 50. At the last the priest, having taken the censer into his left hand from the deacon, with outstretched hands saith thrice, signing himself with the cross: “Let us who mystically represent the Cherubim, and sing the thrice-holy hymn unto the life-creating Trinity, now lay aside all earthly care...” etc. After each repetition he makes a bow, and after the third repetition, having bowed, he kisses the holy Altar-Table and the Cross. After this he goes forth unto the Royal Doors, and having censed the holy Sanctuary [Altar], he departs unto the Table of Preparation [Prothesis], the deacons preceding him, where he likewise censes the offered gifts, praying within himself: “O God, be merciful unto me a sinner!” (While the priest prays and the deacon censes, the choir sings: “Let us who mystically represent the Cherubim...”[^196])

Here at the Table of Preparation [Prothesis] the priest, having censed the gifts, gives the censer unto the subdeacon; and having taken the Aer, gives it likewise unto the subdeacon, who, having received it into both hands, holds it before his face and carries it, candle-bearers preceding them. Having taken the Diskos with the Asterisk [Star], he gives it unto the deacon, who, upon the conclusion of the singing of “Let us who mystically represent the Cherubim,” exclaims: “May the Lord God remember all you Orthodox Christians in His kingdom, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages.” (If the deacon is only a cleric, he then carries the censer.) The priest after this, having taken the holy Chalice into his hands, goes forth after the deacons through the northern door. (If, however, the priest celebrates alone, then, having placed the Aer upon his left shoulder and having given up the censer, he takes into his right hand the Diskos, upon which is the Asterisk and one veil, and into his left hand the Chalice covered with the lention and veil), chanting: “The Most Holy Universal Pontiff, [N.], our most faithful Sovereign, [N.], with all his court and his Christ-loving armed forces, our God-loving Bishop, [N.], and all the priestly and diaconal order, and the clerical rank; the blessed and ever-memorable founders and benefactors of this holy temple; and may the Lord God remember all you Orthodox Christians in His kingdom, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages.” And having come before the Royal Doors, at the words: “and all you...” etc., he turns toward the people,

=== LEAF p213 ===
[Book Page 189]

but blesses them not; after this he turns again unto the holy Altar-Table and thus concludes this prayer. Meanwhile the deacon and subdeacon, having entered into the holy Sanctuary [Altar], await the priest on both sides within the holy doors. At the conclusion of this Great Entrance the choir saith “Amen,” and sings: “That we may receive the King of all, escorted invisibly by angelic ranks.*) Alleluia.”[^197],[^198]

Having entered***)[^199] within into the holy Sanctuary [Altar], the priest sets the holy Chalice upon the holy Altar-Table upon the Iliton on the right side, having removed from it the lention and having covered it with the smaller veil; and upon the left side he places the Diskos, having the Asterisk [Star] standing over the Lamb. Then, taking the Aer and having censed it, he covers therewith the presented gifts, saying: “The noble Joseph, taking down Thy most pure Body from the tree, wrapped it in clean linen with sweet spices, and laying it in a new tomb, closed it...” etc. After this, taking the censer from the deacon, he censes them thrice, saying: “Do good, O Lord, in Thy good pleasure unto Zion, and let the walls of Jerusalem be built...” etc.

The priest having thus placed the Holy Gifts, the deacon bows low before the most holy Altar-Table and goes forth through the northern door onto the ambo, and standing in the customary place, saith: “Let us complete our prayer unto the Lord,” etc.; while the priest quietly saith this litany at the holy Altar-Table, and concludes with the exclamation: “Through the compassions of Thine Only-begotten Son, with Whom Thou art blessed, together with Thine all-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages,” unto which the choir answers: “Amen.”

When the Holy Gifts have been offered unto God in sacrifice, and when now the unbloody sacrifice of the New Testament itself is to be accomplished: the faithful are bound to prepare themselves for the accomplishment of this great Mystery, that this sacrifice may be pleasing unto God also with regard to the faithful. But since without faith it is impossible to please God, and faith without love is dead: therefore the faithful must manifest their love before the oblation of the sacrifice, and unanimously confess their faith.

=== LEAF p214 ===
[Book Page 190]

Unto the sign of that mutual love, the priest expresses it when after the exclamation: “Through the compassions” he saith unto the faithful the words: “Peace be unto all.” Unto this the choir answers: “And unto thy spirit.” After this the priest or deacon summons the faithful to manifest also by an outward sign their love, saying: “Let us love one another, that with one mind we may confess:”[^200] Unto this the choir answers: “The Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Trinity, one in essence and undivided.” During this singing the priest kisses the Gifts covered with the Aer: first the top of the Diskos, then the top of the Chalice, thereafter the edge of the holy Altar-Table before him, saying at each kissing: “I will love Thee, O Lord, my strength; the Lord is my foundation and my refuge.”

After this kissing the priest or deacon exclaims: “The doors! The doors! In wisdom let us attend!”[^201] unto which the choir sings: “I believe.” And the priest, having raised the Aer with both hands, holds it over the Holy Gifts, reciting secretly the Confession of Faith. (If there is a deacon, he during this time opens the Royal Doors.) When the priest comes unto the words: “and was incarnate of the Holy Spirit and Mary the Virgin,” he bows low; and upon the conclusion of the Symbol [Creed] he lowers the Aer, and having folded it in three parts, saith the Trisagion, and signing himself with the sign of the cross, kisses the Aer, and having folded it, places it upon the right side of the holy Altar-Table.

After the Confession of Faith, in order to make the faithful attentive unto the unbloody sacrifice, the deacon or priest exclaims: “Let us stand aright, let us stand with fear, let us attend, that we may offer the holy oblation in peace,” and immediately the deacon, having bowed, enters into the holy Sanctuary [Altar], and the priest exclaims, blessing the people: “The grace of our Lord Jesus Christ, and the love of God the Father, and the communion of the Holy Spirit, be with you all,” unto which the choir answers: “And with thy spirit.” After this the priest again admonishes the faithful to lay aside all earthly cares, saying: “Let us lift up our hearts,” lifting up together both his hands and his eyes, unto which the choir answers: “We lift them up unto the Lord.” After this the priest, having joined his outstretched hands at his breast and bowed his head, saith: “Let us give thanks unto the Lord,” and while the choir sings: “It is proper and right...” etc., he, with outstretched hands, reverently reads

=== LEAF p215 ===
[Book Page 191]

the prayer: “It is proper and right...” and pronounces only the conclusion of this prayer with a loud voice: “Singing the triumphant hymn, shouting, crying aloud, and saying:”[^202] and at these words takes the Asterisk [Star], and having bowed, strikes the four edges of the star upon the four sides of the Diskos, that is, depicts therewith upon the Diskos the image of the Holy Cross for a sign that the remembrance of Christ’s death upon the Cross draws near, and kisses it, and sets it aside.

When the choir sings the Angelic hymn, “Holy, Holy, Holy,” the priest—since he himself beforehand, having joined his hands at his breast, hath recited the hymn “Holy, Holy, Holy”—must renew with discernment and attentiveness the intention of his will toward the consecration of the Holy Mysteries, which he already formed at the Proskomedia, and that: briefly, quietly, in mind and not by words, saying: that he desires to consecrate the bread into the Body and the wine into the Blood; likewise he quietly repeats within himself for whom, or for what necessity, he desires to offer this sacrifice. And having awakened within himself perfect contrition and gathered all his thoughts, he outstretches his hands and with God-fearing attentiveness reads secretly this prayer: “With these blessed powers we also, O Master Who lovest mankind, cry aloud and say: Holy art Thou and all-holy, Thou and Thine Only-begotten Son, and Thy Holy Spirit...” etc.; and when he comes in this prayer unto the words: “taking bread,” he joins his hands; and after this takes the Diskos into his hands and lifts it up; and saying: “having given thanks,” he inclines his head, and makes with his right hand over the Diskos the sign of the cross, saying: “He † blessed, sanc † tified, and † broke, and gave unto His disciples, saying:” And immediately having bowed down, looking attentively upon the prepared bread, he pronounces the words that consecrate and transubstantiate the bread into the Body of Christ with a loud voice and distinctly: “Take, eat: this is My Body, which is broken for you for the remission of sins.” The choir: “Amen.” After this, having bowed low once, he uncovers the holy Chalice and, taking it with both hands, blesses it quietly, saying: “Likewise also the cup, † after supper, saying:” and immediately having bowed down and looking into the Chalice, he pronounces with a loud voice the words that consecrate and transubstantiate the wine into the Blood of Jesus Christ: “Drink of it, all of you: this is My Blood of the New Testament, which is shed for you and for many for the remission

=== LEAF p216 ===
[Book Page 192]

of sins.” When the choir sings: “Amen,” the priest again bows low before the holy Blood of Christ once, and after this quietly saith: “Remembering therefore this saving commandment and all those things which came to pass for us: the Cross, the Grave, the Resurrection on the third day, the Ascension into heaven, the Sitting at the right hand, and the second and glorious Coming again...” etc. Upon the conclusion whereof, having crossed his hands crosswise, he then takes the Diskos into his right hand, and the Chalice into his left, and raises them up from the holy Altar-Table and sets them down upon the holy Altar-Table crosswise, saying: “Thine own of Thine own we offer unto Thee, in all and for all,” unto which the choir sings: “We sing unto Thee, we bless Thee, we give thanks unto Thee, O Lord, and we pray unto Thee, our God.”[^203]

After this the priest, having placed the Holy Mysteries in their place, bows low, and with outstretched hands secretly reads the prayer: “Furthermore we offer unto Thee this reasonable and unbloody service...” etc. Upon the conclusion of this prayer he saith thrice in a quiet voice: “O Lord, Who didst send down Thy Most Holy Spirit at the third hour upon Thine Apostles: take Him not away from us, O Good One, but renew Him in us who pray unto Thee,” with the verses, and after each time bows low; and at the end blesses the consecrated bread, saying: “And make this bread † the precious Body of Thy Christ”; and after this blesses the consecrated wine, saying: “And that which is in this cup, † the precious Blood of Thy Christ”; and after this, signing both with the cross, saith: “Changing them † by Thy Holy Spirit.” And having bowed profoundly, he covers the Chalice and continues the initial prayer: “That they may be unto them that partake for the purification of soul, for the remission of sins, for the communion of Thy Holy Spirit, for the fulfillment of the kingdom of heaven...” etc.[^204]

After this the priest, having commended all the faithful unto God’s grace, commemorates the individual members of the Church of Christ, and that first of all the members of the Church Triumphant (*Ecclesia triumphans*), with outstretched hands quietly saying: “Furthermore we offer unto Thee this reasonable service for those who have fallen asleep in the faith: Ancestors, Fathers, Patriarchs, Prophets, Apostles, Preachers, Evangelists, Martyrs, Confessors, Ascetics, and every righteous spirit made perfect in faith...” etc. But in this prayer, when he comes unto the commemoration of the Most Holy Theotokos, he takes the censer and censes the Holy Mysteries, continuing the initial prayer, saying with a loud voice: “Especially for our Most Holy, most pure, most blessed and glorious Lady, the Theotokos and Ever-Virgin Mary,” unto which the choir sings: “It is truly proper to bless thee, the Theotokos, ever-blessed and blameless, and the Mother of our God...” After this the priest outstretches his hands and continues the commemoration of the Saints, quietly saying: “For the holy Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John,”

=== LEAF p217 ===
[Book Page 193]

etc. If there is a deacon, at “Especially” he opens the Royal Doors and censes the holy Altar-Table crosswise.

After this the priest, having invoked the Saints for their intercession, commemorates all the departed in the hope of resurrection (*Ecclesia patiens*), saying: “Remember, O Lord, the soul of Thy servant, [N.]...” etc., whereat he joins his hands, and quietly commemorates their names, and at the end outstretches his hands and concludes the commemoration of the departed with the words: “And make them to rest where the light of Thy countenance visits.”

After the commemoration of the dead follows the commemoration of the living (*Ecclesia militans*), quietly and with outstretched hands: “Furthermore we entreat Thee, remember, O Lord, all the episcopate of the Orthodox...” etc. Nevertheless, the commemoration of the supreme ecclesiastical hierarchs is made with a loud voice by this exclamation: “Among the first, remember, O Lord, the Most Holy Universal Pontiff, [N.], and our God-loving Bishop, [N.],” etc. After this exclamation, while the choir sings: “And all men and all women,” the priest, having his hands joined, continues the commemoration of the living whom he wishes, having commemorated also himself. At the end he outstretches his hands and quietly continues the commemorations, saying: “Remember, O Lord, this city wherein we dwell...” etc., and concludes with the exclamation: “And grant that with one mouth and one heart we may glorify and praise Thine all-honorable and majestic name, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages,”[^205] etc.

Having finished all these commemorations, he entreats God that the mercy of our Lord Jesus Christ, which flowed forth from His death upon the Cross as fruit, may be always with us, saying: “And may the mercies of our great God and Savior Jesus Christ be with you all.”

And hereby the core of the Divine Liturgy[^206] is concluded, that is, the oblation of the sacrifice, and now follows the preparation for Holy Communion.

At the exclamation: “And may the mercies...” the deacon closes the Royal Doors, and the subdeacon goes forth before the Royal Doors onto the ambo and chants the litany: “Having commemorated all the Saints, again and again in peace let us pray to the Lord...” etc., unto which the choir at each petition answers accordingly either “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) or “Grant this, O Lord.” The priest, having concluded the commemorations, quietly saith the litany and the prayer: “Unto Thee we commend our whole life and hope, O Master Who lovest mankind...” etc., and when the deacon concludes the litany, [the priest] with a loud voice pronounces

=== LEAF p218 ===
[Book Page 194]

the exclamation: “And make us worthy, O Master, that with boldness and without condemnation we may dare to call upon Thee, the heavenly God as Father, and to say:” etc., unto which the choir sings “Our Father.” During the singing thereof the priest, having his hands outstretched, likewise saith the Lord’s Prayer, but secretly—pronouncing with a loud voice only the exclamation: “For Thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages.” After this the priest greets the faithful with these words: “Peace be unto all.” Further he summons them, that in token of submission unto the Heavenly Father they should bow their heads, saying: “Bow your heads unto the Lord.” During the singing of “Unto Thee, O Lord,” the priest saith the prayer: “We give thanks unto Thee, O King invisible, Who by Thine immeasurable power hast created all things, and in the multitude of Thy mercy hast brought all things from nothing into being...” etc., and concludes with the exclamation, saying: “Through the grace and compassions and love for mankind of Thine Only-begotten Son, with Whom Thou art blessed, together with Thine all-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.”

After this the priest, having bowed low before the Holy Mysteries, steps aside a little unto the right side, and washes the fingers of both hands, wherewith he is to touch the Most Holy Body of the Lord—and having wiped them, comes again unto the midst of the holy Altar-Table and bows low before the Holy Mysteries.

Having washed his hands and lifted up his eyes on high, he prays attentively and secretly: “Hear us, O Lord Jesus Christ our God, from Thy holy dwelling-place and from the throne of the glory of Thy kingdom...” etc., upon the conclusion of which prayer he saith thrice (striking his breast): “O God, be merciful unto me a sinner!”

The deacon (if there is one), while the priest saith the prayer: “Hear us, O Lord...” etc., comes from the ambo into the holy Sanctuary [Altar] and girds his orarion crosswise in order to manifest his reverence for the Holy Gifts in imitation of the Seraphim, who with wings cover their faces during the sacred action—and goes forth again before the Royal Doors onto the ambo. When he sees that the priest hath uncovered the holy Chalice, he bows low and exclaims: “Let us attend!” (*Во́ньмемъ*) and having entered into the holy Sanctuary [Altar], closes the Royal Doors. And the priest, having taken into his hands the Holy Lamb, elevating it over the Diskos, exclaims with a loud voice: “Holy things are for the holy!”[^207] unto which the choir sings: “One is holy, one is Lord, Jesus Christ, to the glory of God the Father. Amen”; and the priest, having chanted “Holy things are for the holy,” bows low and breaks the Holy Lamb into four parts over the Diskos, saying: “Broken and divided is the Lamb of God, Which is broken and not divided, Which is ever eaten and never consumed, but sanctifies them that partake,” and places them upon the Diskos crosswise in token of the crucifixion of Christ upon the Cross—and that in such manner that he places the first part of the Holy Lamb with the letters “IC” above, “XC” below, “NI” on the left side, and “KA” on the right side. After this, having bowed low, he takes the first particle (that is, “IC”) with the first two fingers of both hands, moves it over the Chalice, and makes therewith the sign of the Holy

=== LEAF p219 ===
[Book Page 195]

Cross, and with the prayer: “The fullness of the Holy Spirit,”*) places it into the Chalice.[^208] And having covered the holy Chalice, he bows low. And immediately the fingers wherewith he broke the Holy Lamb he joins together, and until the washing of the holy Chalice holds them thus joined.

After this, having slightly inclined his head, he outstretches his hands and, looking reverently upon the Holy Mysteries, quietly prays: “I believe, O Lord, and I confess that Thou art truly the Christ, the Son of the living God, Who camest into the world to save sinners, of whom I am the first...” etc. Upon the conclusion of this prayer he bows low and, having taken the particle of the Lamb with the fingers of his right hand, saith: “The precious, most holy, and most pure Body of our Lord and God and Savior Jesus Christ is imparted unto me, [N.], priest, for the remission of my sins and unto life everlasting. Amen,” and having bowed down, receives communion over the Diskos with the particle “XC” of the Body of Christ with fear and attentiveness. (The remaining particles remain for those who receive communion.) After this, having joined his hands, he meditates a little upon the Holy Mysteries. And having uncovered the holy Chalice, he bows low and, taking the Diskos into his left hand, sweeps the remaining particles of the Holy Body carefully with the thumb of his right hand into the Chalice—and covers the Chalice with the lention. Then he takes the Chalice into his left hand, and the Diskos into his right hand, and places the Diskos under the Chalice and saith: “The precious, most holy, and most pure Blood of our Lord and God and Savior Jesus Christ is imparted unto me, [N.], priest, for the remission of my sins and unto life everlasting. Amen,” and receives communion of the Most Holy Blood once only, that is, he drinks of the Most Holy Blood only once, and with the lention wipes his lips. Having thus received communion, he sets the Chalice again upon the Iliton, and having joined his hands, kisses the edge of the holy Chalice, saying: “Behold, this hath touched my lips, and shall take away mine iniquities and cleanse my sins.” Afterwards he covers the Chalice with the lention and the veil, and bows low.[^209] While the communion of the priest takes place, the choir sings the Communion Hymn,***) of the day, or of the Sunday, or of the feast.[^210]

After this the priest takes the holy Chalice into his hands and turns toward the peo-

=== LEAF p220 ===
[Book Page 196]

-ple and exclaims:*) “With the fear of God and with faith draw near,”[^211] unto which the choir sings: “Blessed is He That comes in the name of the Lord; the Lord is God, and hath appeared unto us.”

(Here follows the communion of the laity, of which mention will be made in the III. Part.)

After “Blessed is He That comes,” the priest, holding in both hands the holy Chalice covered with the lention and veil, blesses the people with the Chalice, saying: “O God, save Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance.” Unto which the choir, confessing the benefits of the Savior, renders thanksgiving unto the Holy Trinity with the following hymn: “For many years, O Master. We have seen the true light, we have received the heavenly Spirit, we have found the true faith, worshiping the undivided Trinity: for the same hath saved us.” The priest at that time turns by that same side (that is, to the right) unto the holy Altar-Table, sets the holy Chalice upon the Iliton, and having bowed low thrice, saith: “Be Thou exalted above the heavens, O God, and Thy glory over all the earth,” and immediately takes the Chalice with both hands—and having placed all the veils and the Diskos and the Asterisk [Star] beneath the Chalice, makes the sign of the cross over the Altar-Table, saying: “Blessed is our God,” and turns toward the people and blesses the people with the holy Chalice, continuing with a loud voice the initial prayer: “always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages.”[^212]

And immediately the priest carries the Holy Gifts unto the Table of Preparation [Prothesis],***)[^213] and the choir, having chanted “Amen,” glorifies and thanks the Lord with a hymn of praise: “May our mouths be filled with Thy praise, O Lord, that we may sing of Thy glory: for Thou hast made us worthy to partake of Thy holy, divine, immortal, and life-creating Mysteries...” etc. At the Table of Preparation [Prothesis] the priest places the Chalice in the midst, but the Aer with one veil, and the Diskos with the Asterisk [Star] on his left side, and uncovers the holy Chalice and bows low, and after this begins to consume the Holy Mysteries, but so that he places the Diskos—as before in the drinking—beneath the Chalice. After this, with wine which the deacon or server pours, he rinses the Chalice and drinks the purification; at the last, with wine and water he washes the Chalice and the fingers of both his hands, and having wiped them with the lention, drinks from the Chalice the wine and water remaining in the holy Chalice, that is, the ablution. And having wiped his mouth and the Chalice with the same lention, he folds the vessels, covers them with the veils and the Aer, saying within himself: “May our mouths be filled...” etc., and after this the litany: “Having received the divine, holy, immaculate, immortal, heavenly, and life-creating, awesome Mysteries of Christ, let us worthily give thanks unto the Lord...” etc.

---

=== LEAF p221 ===
[Book Page 197]

Upon the conclusion of the litany, the priest, having taken the holy Gospel Book from the right side, makes therewith the sign of the holy cross over the Iliton, saying the exclamation: “For Thou art our sanctification, and unto Thee we ascribe glory: to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages.” The choir sings: “Amen.” The priest: “Let us go forth in peace.” The choir: “In the name of the Lord.” The deacon: “Let us pray to the Lord.” The choir: “Lord, have mercy.” After this the priest goes forth through the Royal Doors, and standing in the midst of the church (below the ambo)[^214] saith the prayer (the Prayer Behind the Ambo): “O Lord, Who blessest them that bless Thee, and sanctifiest them that put their trust in Thee...”[^215] etc. Unto such prayer the choir sings: “Amen.” After this: “Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth, and unto the ages” (thrice), and Psalm 33: “I will bless the Lord at all times.” During the singing of this psalm the priest distributes unto the faithful the antidoron[^216] (blessed bread), in memory of that which the Christians of the first centuries

=== LEAF p222 ===
[Book Page 198]

left the holy bread unto the poor. After the distribution of the antidoron the priest enters through the Royal Doors into the holy Sanctuary [Altar], and standing before the holy Altar-Table quietly saith the prayer: “Thou Who art Thyself the fulfillment of the Law and the Prophets, O Christ our God...” etc. After this: “Let us pray to the Lord.” The choir: “Lord, have mercy.” The priest: “The blessing of the Lord be upon you, through His grace and love for mankind, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages.” The choir: “Amen.” The priest turns unto the holy Altar-Table and saith: “Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee!” (*Сла́ва тебѣ̀, Хрїстѐ Бо́же, оу҆пова́ніе на́ше, сла́ва тебѣ̀*) The choir: “Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen. Lord, have mercy (thrice). Bless.” After this the priest turns toward the people and pronounces the dismissal: “May Christ our true God, through the prayers of His most pure Mother...” etc. After the dismissal the choir sings: “Many years.” And the priest, having bowed thrice before the holy Altar-Table, saith the prayers of thanksgiving: “Glory to Thee, O God; glory to Thee, O God; glory to Thee, O God,” and the prayer of St. Simeon: “Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master...” etc., the Trisagion through the Our Father, the Troparion of St. John Chrysostom: “The grace of thy mouth shining forth...” etc., the Kontakion: “From heaven hast thou received divine grace...” etc., “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times), “More honorable than the Cherubim,” “Glory be to the Father... now and forever,” and makes the small dismissal. After this he divests himself of the sacred vestments, saying: “Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart,” and kisses the holy Altar-Table.

Note 1. The hymn: “Only-begotten Son” (*Е҆диноро́дный Сы́не...*)[^217] is customarily sung at the Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom and St. Basil the Great after the second litany. But if this Liturgy is served on a Saturday, and a Feast of the Mother of God should occur, then instead of “Only-begotten Son” (*Е҆диноро́дный Сы́не...*) the Dogmatikon of the Feast of the Mother of God is sung.

Note 2. If several priests concelebrate, then at the Great Entrance the serving priest saith: “The Most Holy Universal... our God-loving Bishop, and all the priestly order...”[^218] And all the concelebrants say: “May the Lord God remember thy priesthood in His kingdom, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages.”[^219]

#### ARTICLE II. Concerning the Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great.

§. 142. The Order of the Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great without Vespers.

The Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great is celebrated 10 times in the year: 1. On the day of Saint Basil the Great (January 1); 2. On the first five Sundays of Great Lent; 3. On Great Thursday; 4. On Great Saturday; 5. On the Eve of the Nativity of Christ; 6. On the Eve of the Theophany of the Lord.

The order of this Liturgy is entirely the same as that of the Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom, with this distinction only: 1. that the priestly prayers in the Liturgy of Saint Basil are longer, on which account the singers sing the chants more slowly and at greater length, that the priest during that time may quietly read his prayers; 2. the priest at the words of consecration

=== LEAF p223 ===
[Book Page 199]

doth not say: “Take, eat,” but first saith: “He gave it unto His holy disciples and Apostles, saying: Take, eat: this is My Body...” Likewise also over the holy chalice he first saith: “He gave it unto His holy disciples and Apostles, saying: Drink of it, all of you: this is My Blood...”[^220] 3. Instead of “It is truly right” (*Достойно есть*) there is sung: “All of creation rejoices in thee, O full of grace, the assembly of Angels and the human race: sanctified temple and spiritual paradise, virgin boast, from whom God was incarnate and became a child, Who is our God before the ages; for He made thy womb a throne, and shaped thy body more spacious than the heavens. All of creation rejoices in thee, O full of grace, glory to thee!”[^221] 4. The Prayer Behind the Ambo is different: “O Master rich in mercy...”

§. 143. The Order of the Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great with Vespers.

With Vespers the Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great is celebrated: 1. On the Eve of the Nativity of Christ; 2. On the Eve of the Theophany; 3. On Great Thursday; 4. On Great Saturday. If the Eve of the Nativity of Christ or of the Theophany should fall on a Saturday or Sunday, then on the Eve the Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom is celebrated, and on the feast day itself that of Saint Basil the Great.

The order of this Liturgy with Vespers is as follows:

After the initial exclamation: “Blessed is the kingdom...” there is read: “Come, let us worship,” and the Introductory Psalm 103: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” The deacon reads the prayers of the Lucernarium. After the psalm there is the Litany of Peace. After the litany: “Lord, I have cried” (*Господи воззвахъ*) on 8, at the end the Theotokion or Dogmatikon, during which the entrance is made with the censer (and if the Gospel Book is to be read, then with the Gospel Book). After the entrance: “O Joyful Light.” After this the deacon: “Let us attend!” (*Во́ньмемъ*) The priest: “Peace be unto all.” The deacon: “Wisdom! Let us attend!” (*Премꙋ́дрость, про́сти!*) and the Prokimenon of the day. After the Prokimenon the Paremias are read (on the Eve of the Nativity 8, of the Theophany 13, on Great Thursday 3, on Great Saturday 15). After the Paremias

=== LEAF p224 ===
[Book Page 200]

there is the small litany, and the Trisagion (but on Great Thursday and Great Saturday, instead of the Trisagion: “All ye who have been baptized into Christ”). After the Trisagion the Prokimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel. After the Gospel, the customary order of the Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great.

Note. The Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom is likewise celebrated with Vespers on the day of the Annunciation,[^222] if it falls upon any weekday of Great Lent, except Saturday and Sunday.

#### ARTICLE III. The Order of the Divine Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts.

The Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts is a divine service wherein the unbloody sacrifice is not accomplished, that is, there is no consecration of the gifts (bread and wine), but communion takes place with the Gifts presanctified at a full Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom or Saint Basil the Great. A full Liturgy is not served[^223] on all days of Great Lent, except Saturdays and Sundays, and the Feast of the Annunciation; but that the faithful might not be deprived of Holy Communion during this holy time of repentance, it was enacted by the Synod of Laodicea[^224] and the Synod in Trullo to celebrate on Wednesdays and Fridays of Great Lent the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts.

The order of this Liturgy consists of 4 parts: (a) The Preparation of the Holy Lamb, (b) Vespers, (c) The Liturgy of the Catechumens, (d) The Liturgy of the Faithful.

§. 144. (a) The Preparation of the Holy Lamb.

The Holy Lamb[^225] for the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts is prepared at a full Liturgy on the preceding Sunday (or Saturday), when there is prepared not one Lamb alone, but two or three

=== LEAF p225 ===
[Book Page 201]

(according to the number of Presanctified Liturgies to be celebrated). At the Proskomedia over each Lamb the priest doth the same as over the first Lamb. At the consecration of the gifts, however, the priest pronounces the words of consecration once only in the plural number: “Make these breads the precious Body of Thy Christ...” And at the fraction of the Lamb the priest takes the first Lamb and incises it and places a particle into the chalice; the other Lambs he doth not divide, but having dipped the spoon into the Holy Blood, he anoints them crosswise on the side of the incision with the Holy Blood of Christ and places them into the Tabernacle [Kyvot] upon the holy Altar-Table[^226] until the day of celebration of the Presanctified Liturgy.

§. 145. (b) Vespers, as a Part of the Holy Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts.

The Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts is celebrated on Wednesday and Friday of all weeks of Great Lent, on Thursday of the Great Canon (in the 5th week), on Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday of Passion Week, and if the parish feast or the day of the 40 Martyrs should fall on a weekday of the fast.[^227]

On the appointed day the priest vests in all the sacred vestments, of bright or dark color, praying only: “Let us pray to the Lord. Lord, have mercy” over each vestment, and kisses the vestments.

Having opened the curtain (the Royal Doors, however, remaining closed), the priest makes a bow before the holy Altar-Table, kisses the Gospel Book and the Altar-Table, takes the Gospel Book with both hands, and having signed the Altar-Table crosswise, exclaims: “Blessed is the kingdom of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages.” The choir: “Amen.”

> **Reader:** “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (thrice), and the Introductory Psalm 103: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” The priest, having gone forth through the northern door, standing before the Royal Doors reads the prayers of the Lucernarium (beginning from the 4th, because the first three prayers are read later at the litanies of the faithful). After the psalm the priest enters into the holy Sanctuary [Altar], and exclaims the Great Litany: “In peace let us pray to the Lord.” After the litany the reader reads the 18th Kathisma: “Unto the Lord in mine affliction have I cried...” divided into three Stases [Slavas]

=== LEAF p226 ===
[Book Page 202]

At the first Stasis [Slava] the priest takes the Holy Lamb from the Tabernacle [Kyvot], places it upon the Diskos, and censes the holy Altar-Table round about and the Holy Lamb.

At the second Stasis [Slava] the priest, having censed the Altar-Table, takes the Diskos with the Holy Lamb and, having placed it upon his head, transfers it through the northern door unto the Table of Preparation [Prothesis], a candle-bearer preceding him with a candle and censer. Having arrived at the Table of Preparation [Prothesis], he sets down the holy Diskos, pours wine and water into the chalice, censes the Asterisk [Star] and the veils, covers the Diskos and the chalice each with its veil separately, and thereafter both together with the Aer, praying: “Let us pray to the Lord” (*Господу помолимся*) over each, and censes the oblation, saying nothing.

At the third Stasis [Slava] the priest returns unto the holy Altar-Table, folds the Iliton, and censes the Altar-Table round about.

After the Kathisma there is the small litany. After the litany: “Lord, I have cried” (*Господи воззвахъ*) is sung with stichera on 10. At the last sticheron the Royal Doors are opened and the entrance is made with the censer (and if the Gospel Book is to be read, then with the holy Gospel Book). The priest kisses the holy Altar-Table, goes forth through the northern door before the Royal Doors, and exclaims: “Wisdom! Stand aright!” (*Премудрость прости*) The choir sings: “O Joyful Light.”

After “O Joyful Light” (*Свѣ́те ти́хїй*) the priest enters into the holy Sanctuary [Altar], and standing before the holy Altar-Table saith: “Let us attend!” (*Во́ньмемъ*) “Peace be unto all.” “Wisdom! Let us attend!” (*Премꙋ́дрость, про́сти!*) The reader exclaims the first Prokimenon of the day. The priest: “Wisdom!” (*Премꙋ́дрость!*) The reader: the title of the first Paremia (from the Book of Genesis). The priest: “Let us attend!” (*Во́ньмемъ*) The reader reads the Paremia. The Royal Doors are closed.

After the first Paremia the Royal Doors are opened. The priest exclaims: “Let us attend! Wisdom!” The reader exclaims the second Prokimenon of the day. After the Prokimenon the reader saith: “Command!” And the priest, having taken with both hands the candlestick with the lighted candle and the censer, standing before the holy Altar-Table and turning toward the east, signs crosswise and exclaims: “Wisdom! Stand aright!” (*Премудрость прости*) After this, turning toward the west unto the people, he exclaims: “The light of Christ enlightens all!” And the people prostrate themselves unto the ground. The priest, having returned unto the holy Altar-Table, sets down the candlestick and the censer. The Royal Doors are closed. The reader reads the second Paremia (from the Book of Proverbs). After the Paremia the priest exclaims: “Peace be unto thee.” The reader: “And unto thy spirit.”

And immediately the Royal Doors are opened. The priest, having taken the censer and standing before the holy Altar-Table, sings with a contrite voice:[^228]

=== LEAF p227 ===
[Book Page 203]

“Let my prayer arise like incense before Thee, the lifting up of my hands as an evening sacrifice.” The choir sings the very same, and all the people pray kneeling. And the priest censes the holy Altar-Table on the right side.

Then the priest sings the 1st verse: “Lord, I have cried unto Thee, hear me; attend to the voice of my supplication, when I cry unto Thee.” The choir: “Let my prayer arise...” And the priest censes the High Place.

Then the priest sings the 2nd verse: “Set a guard, O Lord, upon my mouth, and a door of enclosure about my lips.” The choir: “Let my prayer arise...” The priest censes the holy Altar-Table on the left side.

Then the priest sings the 3rd verse: “Incline not my heart unto words of malice, to devise excuses for sins.” The choir: “Let my prayer arise...” The priest censes the holy Altar-Table on the front side.

Then the priest sings the 4th verse: “With men that work iniquity, and I will not join with their chosen ones.” The choir: “Let my prayer arise...” The priest departs unto the Table of Preparation [Prothesis] and censes the offered Holy Gifts.

Then the priest sings: “The righteous man shall correct me in mercy and reprove me, but let not the oil of the sinner anoint my head.” The choir: “Let my prayer arise...”

Then the priest, having returned before the holy Altar-Table, sings the first half of the verse: “Let my prayer arise like incense before Thee.” And having handed over the censer, he prostrates himself unto the ground. And the choir finishes singing: “The lifting up of my hands as an evening sacrifice.” The priest makes 3 prostrations with the prayer of St. Ephrem: “O Lord and Master of my life...”[^229] The Royal Doors are closed.

=== LEAF p228 ===
[Book Page 204]

(If it is a feast day, then after “Let my prayer arise” (*Да и҆спра́витсѧ*) the Epistle and Gospel of the feast are read.)

And hereby Vespers as a part of the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts is concluded.

§. 146. (c) The Liturgy of the Catechumens.

Thereafter the Royal Doors are opened, and the priest reads the Litany of Fervent Supplication: “Let us all say with our whole soul and with our whole mind, let us say...” The choir sings: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (thrice each time). After the litany the priest exclaims: “For a merciful and man-befriending God art Thou, and unto Thee we ascribe glory: to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages.” The choir: “Amen.”

After this the deacon or priest exclaims: “Pray unto the Lord, ye catechumens.” The choir: “Lord, have mercy.” “Ye faithful, let us pray for the catechumens...” and the priest prays quietly the prayer for the catechumens. After the litany the exclamation: “That they also with us may glorify Thine all-honorable and majestic name...”

From Wednesday of the 4th week of Great Lent (Mid-Pentecost of the fast), after the Litany of Fervent Supplication there is added also the Litany for Those Preparing for Illumination (for those catechumens who prepare for Holy Baptism on Great Saturday, on which account they were called the illuminated—*illuminandi*): “Pray, ye that are preparing for illumination, unto the Lord...” And the priest quietly prays the prayer for those preparing for illumination: “Manifest, O Lord, Thy countenance...” After the litany the exclamation: “For Thou art our illumination, and unto Thee we ascribe glory: to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages.”

After this he exclaims: “All ye that are preparing for illumination, depart... All ye who are catechumens, depart; let none of the catechumens remain; as many as are of the faithful, again and again in peace let us pray to the Lord.”

And hereby the Liturgy of the Catechumens is concluded.

=== LEAF p229 ===
[Book Page 205]

§. 147. (d) The Liturgy of the Faithful.

The Liturgy of the Faithful begins with two small litanies: 1. “Again and again in peace let us pray to the Lord”; the priest reads the 1st Prayer of the Faithful: “O God, great and praiseworthy...” and exclaims: “For unto Thee is due all glory, honor, and worship...” The choir: “Amen.” 2. “Again and again...”; the priest reads the 2nd Prayer of the Faithful: “O holy Master, exceedingly good...” and the exclamation: “According to the gift of Thy Christ...” The choir: “Amen.”

After these litanies the Royal Doors are opened, and the priest censes the holy Altar-Table round about, reciting Psalm 50. The choir then begins to sing the magnificent hymn:

“Now the heavenly hosts minister invisibly with us: for behold, the King of Glory enters; behold, the mystical sacrifice, fully accomplished, is ushered in with spears. In faith and love let us draw near, that we may become partakers of life everlasting. Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia.”

During this hymn the priest prays before the holy Altar-Table, saying thrice with bows: “Now the heavenly hosts...” After this, having kissed the Altar-Table, he departs unto the Table of Preparation [Prothesis], a candle-bearer preceding him with a candle and censer. Having censed the gifts, the priest takes the Aer and places it upon his left shoulder, takes the Diskos into his right hand and the chalice into his left hand before his breast, and saying nothing goes forth through the northern door amidst profound silence. The people and the singers fall prostrate upon the earth with fear and reverence, because the Holy Gifts are being transferred, already consecrated and transubstantiated into the true Body and true Blood of Christ the Savior.

The priest, having passed through the church, enters through the Royal Doors into the holy Sanctuary [Altar], sets the holy Diskos and the holy chalice upon the antimension (iliton), removes the veils, and covers them with the Aer, and censes them thrice, saying nothing.

The choir then sings the second half of the hymn: “In faith and love let us draw near...” Upon the conclusion of this singing, all arise from the earth; the priest makes 3 prostrations with the prayer of St. Ephrem: “O Lord and Master of my life...” The Royal Doors are closed, and the curtain is drawn halfway.

=== LEAF p230 ===
[Book Page 206]

After this the priest exclaims the Litany of Supplication: “Let us complete our evening prayer unto the Lord.” The choir: “Lord, have mercy.” “For the precious Gifts set forth and presanctified...” And the priest prays quietly: “O God of ineffable and invisible mysteries...” After the litany the exclamation: “And account us worthy, O Master, with boldness...” The choir sings: “Our Father.” The priest: “For Thine is the kingdom...” The choir: “Amen.” The priest: “Peace be unto all.” The choir: “And unto thy spirit.” The priest: “Bow your heads unto the Lord.” The choir: “To Thee, O Lord.” The priest quietly saith the prayer of the bowing of heads: “O God, Who alone art good and compassionate...” and the exclamation: “Through the grace and compassions...” The choir: “Amen.”

The priest prays quietly: “Attend, O Lord Jesus Christ our God...” After the prayer he bows thrice, saying: “O God, be merciful unto me a sinner!” and having inserted his hand beneath the Aer, touches the Holy Bread with reverence. And immediately the curtain is drawn completely, and the priest elevates the Holy Lamb, saying: “The Presanctified holy things for the holy!” The choir sings: “One is holy, one is Lord, Jesus Christ, to the glory of God the Father. Amen.”

And it sings the Communion Hymn of the day: “O taste and see that the Lord is good. Alleluia” (thrice).

The priest breaks the Holy Lamb into 4 parts in the customary manner, and places the particle “IC” into the holy chalice, saying nothing. The server pours in the zeon [warm water]. The priest receives communion of the particles of the Body and Blood of Christ, just as at the full Liturgy, but from the chalice he drinks saying nothing.[^230]

After communion the curtain and the Royal Doors are opened, and the priest shows the holy chalice unto the people, exclaiming: “With the fear of God and with faith draw near!” The choir: “I will bless the Lord at all times; His praise shall continually be in my mouth.” The priest blesses the people, saying: “O God, save Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance.” The choir: “Taste of the heavenly bread and the cup of life, and see that the Lord is good. Alleluia” (thrice).

The priest carries the Holy Gifts unto the Table of Preparation [Prothesis]. Having returned unto the Altar-Table, he exclaims: “Always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages.” The choir: “May our mouths be filled with Thy praise...” After this the thanksgiving litany: “Stand aright! Having partaken...” The priest folds the antimension, takes the holy Gospel Book, and makes therewith a cross over the antimension, exclaiming: “For Thou art our sanctification...” The choir: “Amen.” The priest: “Let us go forth in peace.” The choir: “In the name of the Lord.” The deacon: “Let us pray to the Lord.” The choir: “Lord, have mercy.”

The priest goes forth from the Sanctuary [Altar], and standing before the ambo reads the Prayer Behind the Ambo of the Presanctified Liturgy: “O Master Almighty, Who hast fashioned all creation in wisdom...” After the prayer the choir sings: “Amen,” and “Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth, and unto the ages” (thrice); and the priest, having returned into the holy Sanctuary [Altar], reads the prayer at the consumption of the Holy Gifts: “O Lord our God, Who hast led us into these all-honorable days...” He consumes the Holy Gifts, washes his hands, divests himself of the vestments, reads the prayers of thanksgiving, and makes the dismissal.

---

=== LEAF p231 ===
[Book Page 207]

[...Blessed be the] name [of the Lord,”] the choir reads Psalm 33 (“I will bless the Lord” (*Благословлю Господа*)), and upon the conclusion of this reading exclaims: “It is truly right... of our [God],” unto which the priest exclaims: “Wisdom!” (*Премꙋ́дрость!*) and the choir continues: “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc., and there is the dismissal and blessing. After the dismissal the priest saith: “Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart,” etc., and the prayers found in the Sluzhebnik. Finally, having kissed the holy Altar-Table and the Cross, he departs from the holy Altar-Table and, having divested himself of the priestly vestments, washes his hands, reads the prayers after Holy Communion, and having given thanks unto God, departs.

### CHAPTER II.

§. 148.

Concerning Liturgies on Various Occasions.

It was already stated that the Greek Church in a single Horologion combines the Oktoechos with the Menaion, that is, the celebration of an immovable feast with the celebration of a movable feast, because she celebrates every day a twofold feast—and from this combination there have arisen various occurrences. So it is likewise concerning the holy Liturgy. But here the variety of these occurrences is further increased also by the fact that here with the daily Liturgy there can be combined also a Liturgy of various intentions, as for example: for every petition, or for the remission of sins, or for the sick, etc. But besides this there are also certain extraordinary occurrences in holy Liturgies. And in accordance herewith we shall speak here: (α) concerning regular occurrences, and (β) concerning the most notable extraordinary occurrences.

#### ARTICLE I.

Concerning Regular Occurrences.

§. 149.

I. How the Liturgy is Celebrated if it is Sunday and a Minor Saint?

At the Liturgy the typical antiphons are sung, that is, “Bless the Lord, O my soul” (*Благослови душе моя Господа*) (Psalm 102) and “Praise the Lord, O my soul” (*Хвалѝ, дꙋшѐ моя̀, Го́спода...*) (Psalm 145). The first is concluded with “Glory... now and forever,” and the second after “Glory

=== LEAF p232 ===
[Book Page 208]

and now and forever” with the hymn “Only-begotten Son,” etc. At the Beatitudes, the Sunday stichera of the Oktoechos on 8. After the entrance, the Sunday troparion; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the Sunday kontakion; “Now and forever”: the kontakion according to the tone from the feasts of the Mother of God,[^231] or of the temple, if the temple is of Christ or of the Mother of God,[^232] or “O Protection of Christians,” if the temple is of a Saint.[^233] The Prokimenon of the tone. The Epistle and Gospel of the Sunday. The Communion Hymn likewise of the Sunday: “Praise the Lord.” From this it is evident that a Minor Saint hath no proper celebration at the Liturgy.

§. 150.

II. How the Liturgy is Celebrated if it is Sunday and a Saint on 6.

Among these Saints there are such as have a proper celebration, and then the Liturgy is celebrated thus: The typical antiphons; stichera at the Beatitudes of the tone of the Oktoechos on 6, and of the Saint Ode 3 on 4. After the entrance, the troparia are taken in such wise that first the Sunday troparion is sung, thereafter the troparion of the Saint, the Sunday kontakion; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of the Saint; “Now and forever”: the Theotokion as stated above. The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the Sunday in course, and—if the Saint likewise hath them—then they are taken also for him.

§. 151.

III. How the Liturgy is Celebrated if it is Sunday and a Polyeleos Saint.

“Bless the Lord, O my soul,” etc. The Beatitudes of the tone in course on 6, and unto the Saint Ode 3 on 4. After the entrance, the Sunday troparion and the troparion of the Saint; the Sunday kontakion; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of the Saint; “Now and forever”: the kontakion according to the tone from the feasts of the Mother of God, or “O Protection of Christians.” The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the Sunday and of the Saint.

=== LEAF p233 ===
[Book Page 209]

§. 152.

IV. How the Liturgy is Celebrated if it is Sunday and a Forefeast of a Feast of the Lord or of the Mother of God?

The typical antiphons are taken; the Beatitudes of the Oktoechos on 6, and of the Forefeast Ode 3 on 4. After the entrance, the Sunday Troparion and the Troparion of the Forefeast; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the Sunday Kontakion; “Now and forever”: the Kontakion of the Forefeast. The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn are taken of the Sunday alone.

§. 153.

V. How the Liturgy is Celebrated if it is Sunday and a Feast of the Mother of God?

“Bless the Lord, O my soul,” and the Sunday Beatitudes on 6, and of the feast Ode 3 on 4. After the entrance, the Sunday troparion and the troparion of the feast; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the Sunday kontakion; “Now and forever”: the kontakion of the feast. The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the Sunday and of the Feast. At the Megalynarion [“Especially”], instead of “It is truly right,” the refrain and Heirmos of the 9th Ode of the Feast.

§. 154.

VI. How the Liturgy is Celebrated if it is Sunday and a Feast of the Lord?

Festal antiphons are taken.[^234] After each verse of the 1st festal Antiphon there is appended the refrain: “Through the prayers of the Mother of God, O Savior, save us”; after each verse of the 2nd Antiphon there is appended the refrain: “Save us, O Son of God, Who art risen from the dead” (or “Who wast born of the Virgin,” or “Who wast transfigured on the mount,” corresponding to the feast), “who chant unto Thee: Alleluia.” After the 2nd Antiphon: “Glory... now and forever,” “Only-begotten Son.” After each verse of the 3rd Antiphon the troparion of the feast is sung. Likewise instead of “Come, let us worship,” the great feasts have an Introit [Entrance Hymn], that is, entrance verses of the feast taken from the prophetic Psalms pertaining unto the

=== LEAF p234 ===
[Book Page 210]

feast. After the entrance, the troparion of the Feast; “Glory... now and forever”: the kontakion of the Feast. At the Megalynarion [“Especially”], instead of “It is truly right,” the refrain of the feast is sung with the Heirmos of the 9th Ode. The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the Feast; of the Sunday nothing is taken.[^235]

Notes: 1) If it is Sunday, a Minor Saint, and a service of intention:
After the entrance: the troparion of the Sunday and the troparion of intention, the kontakion of intention; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the Sunday kontakion; “Now and forever”: as above (that is, if the temple is of Christ or of the Mother of God, the kontakion of the temple; if of a Saint: “O Protection of Christians” (*Заступнице*) or from the Theotokion kontakia). The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn first of the Sunday, and of intention. (Unto the Minor Saint nothing is taken.)
2) If it is Sunday, a Great Saint, and a service of intention:
After the entrance: the Sunday troparion, the troparion of the Saint, and the troparion of intention; the kontakion of intention; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion unto the Saint; “Now and forever”: as above.
3) If it is Sunday, a Feast of the Lord, and a service of intention:
After the entrance: the troparion of the feast and of intention; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of intention; “Now and forever”: the kontakion of the feast. (Of the Sunday nothing is taken.) The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the feast and of intention.

§. 155.

VII. How the Liturgy is Celebrated if it is Sunday and within a Feast there is a Minor Saint?

Unto the Minor Saint nothing is taken, but only unto the Sunday and the Afterfeast. There are sung: the typical antiphons; stichera at the Beatitudes of the tone on 6, and of the feast the ode in course from the canon on 4. After the entrance, if it is an afterfeast of a Feast of the Lord, after “Wisdom! Stand aright!” (*Премудрость прости*): “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) and “Save us, O Son of God, Who wast born of the Virgin,” or “Who wast transfigured on the mount,” etc. After the entrance, the Sunday troparion, the troparion of the feast; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the Sunday kontakion; “Now and forever”: the kontakion of the feast. The Prokimenon of the tone and of the feast. The Epistle and Gospel of the Sunday only. Instead of “It is truly right,” the refrain of the feast with the Heirmos of the 9th Ode. The Communion Hymn of the Sunday and of the Feast.

=== LEAF p235 ===
[Book Page 211]

§. 156.

VIII. How the Liturgy is Celebrated if it is Sunday and within a Feast there is a Middle Saint having a Celebration?

The typical antiphons. The Beatitudes of the tone on 4, of the feast the ode in course from the canon on 4, and of the Saint Ode 3 on 4. After the entrance, if it is an afterfeast of a Feast of the Lord, there is sung “Come, let us worship,” and the final refrain of the feast. After the entrance: the Sunday troparion, the troparion of the Saint, and the troparion of the feast; likewise the Sunday kontakion; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of the Saint; “Now and forever”: the kontakion of the feast. The Prokimenon of the tone and of the Saint. The Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the Sunday and of the Saint. Instead of “It is truly right,” the refrain of the feast and the Heirmos of the 9th Ode of the canon are sung.

§. 157.

IX. How the Liturgy is Celebrated if it is Sunday and within a Feast there is a Great Saint?

The typical antiphons; stichera at the Beatitudes of the Oktoechos on 4, of the feast the ode in course on 4, and of the Saint Ode 3 on 4. At the entrance, if it is an afterfeast of a Feast of the Lord, there is sung: “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) and the final refrain of the feast. After the entrance: the Sunday troparion, the troparion of the feast, and the troparion of the Saint; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of the Saint; “Now and forever”: the kontakion of the feast. The Prokimenon of the tone and of the Saint. The Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the Sunday and of the Saint.[^236] Instead of “It is truly right,” the refrain of the feast.

§. 158.

X. How the Liturgy is Celebrated if the Apodosis of a Feast Falls on a Sunday?

The typical antiphons. The Beatitudes of the tone on 6, and of the feast Ode 9 on 4, that is, unto 4 verses of the Beatitudes the 4 troparia of the 9th Ode of the Matins canon prescribed for the feast are appended. At the entrance, if it is the Apodosis of a Feast of the Lord, there is sung: “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) and the final refrain of the feast. After the entrance: the Sunday troparion and the troparion of the feast;

=== LEAF p236 ===
[Book Page 212]

“Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the Sunday kontakion; “Now and forever”: the kontakion of the feast. The Epistle and Gospel of the Sunday in course only. Instead of “It is truly right,” the Heirmos of the 9th Ode of the feast is sung. The Prokimenon and Communion Hymn of the Sunday and of the Feast. The dismissal of the Sunday.

§. 159.

XI. How the Liturgy is Celebrated if the Patronal Feast of a Saint Falls with a Forefeast or with the Apodosis of a Feast on a Sunday?

The typical antiphons; stichera at the Beatitudes: the Sunday ones on 4, of the feast Ode 3 on 4, of the temple Ode 6 on 4. After the entrance, the troparion of the Sunday, of the feast, and of the temple of the Saint; the Sunday kontakion; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of the temple; “Now and forever”: of the feast. The Prokimenon of the tone and of the temple. The Epistle in course and of the temple. The Gospel in course and of the temple. The Communion Hymn of the Sunday and of the temple.

Notes: 1) If it is Sunday, within a feast a Minor Saint, and a service of intention:
After the entrance: the Sunday troparion, the troparion of the feast, and the troparion of intention; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of intention; “Now and forever”: of the feast. The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the Sunday and of intention.
2) If it is Sunday, within a feast a Great Saint, and a service of intention:
After the entrance: the Sunday troparion, the troparion of the feast, the troparion of the Saint, and the troparion of intention; the kontakion of intention; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of the Saint; “Now and forever”: of the feast. The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the Sunday, unto the Saint, and of intention.
3) If it is Sunday, the Apodosis of a feast, and a service of intention:
After the entrance: the Sunday troparion, the troparion of the feast, and the troparion of intention; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of intention; “Now and forever”: the kontakion of the feast.

§. 160.
