XII. How the Holy Liturgy is Celebrated if it is a Weekday and a Minor Saint?

The weekday service according to the Oktoechos is taken on Monday unto the Angels, on Tuesday unto the Forerunner, on Wednesday and Friday unto the Cross, on Thursday unto the Apostles and Saint Nicholas, and on Saturday unto All Saints. But the service unto those Saints at the Liturgy is taken only

=== LEAF p237 ===
[Book Page 213]

then, when upon a weekday there falls a Minor or Middle Saint.[^237]
On such days the daily antiphons are taken (1st Antiphon: “It is good to give praise” (*Бла́го є̂́сть*); 2nd Antiphon: “The Lord reigns” (*Госпо́дь воцари́ся*); 3rd Antiphon: “Come, let us rejoice in the Lord”).[^238]
After the entrance (if the service is taken for the day alone): The troparion of the day; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of the day; “Now and forever”: the kontakion from the feasts of the Mother of God according to the tone, or the kontakion of the temple, if the temple is of Christ or of the Mother of God; but if the temple is of a Saint, at “Now and forever”: “O Protection of Christians.”[^239] The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the day. (Unto the Minor Saint nothing is taken.)
Note. With a service of intention it is taken thus: The troparion of the day and the troparion of intention; the kontakion of intention; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of the day; “Now and forever”: “O Protection of Christians” (*Заступнице*) (or the kontakion of the temple, where the temple is of Christ or of the Mother of God).[^240] The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the day [and] of intention.

§. 161.

XIII. How the Liturgy is Celebrated on a Weekday if there is a Saint on 6.

The daily antiphons, unless the Saint hath [stichera] at the Beatitudes, otherwise the typical antiphons. After the entrance: the troparion of the day and the troparion of the Saint (if he hath an Epistle and Gospel), the kontakion of the day; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of the Saint; “Now and forever”: the kontakion as above. The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the day and of the Saint, if he hath them.[^241]

=== LEAF p238 ===
[Book Page 214]

Note. If there is a service of a particular intention, there is sung: the troparion of the day, the troparion of the Saint, and the troparion of intention; the kontakion of the day, the kontakion of the Saint; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of intention; “Now and forever”: the Theotokion, as above. On Wednesday or Friday it is covered with the daily kontakion of the Cross.

§. 162.

XIV. How the Liturgy is Celebrated if a Great Saint Falls on a Weekday.

When on a weekday (other than Sunday) a Great Saint falls, then the entire daily service—the troparion with the kontakion, the Prokimenon with the Communion Hymn—is omitted at the Liturgy. The Epistle and Gospel in course (that is, the daily series [*stolp*]) are read one day beforehand, and the entire service is taken unto the Saint.[^242] The typical antiphons. After the entrance: the troparion unto the Saint; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion unto the Saint; “Now and forever”: “O Protection of Christians,” or the kontakion of the temple, where the temple is of Christ or of the Mother of God. The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn unto the Saint.[^243]
Note. If the priest hath a particular intention, then: the troparion of the Saint and the troparion of intention; the kontakion of intention; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of the Saint; “Now and forever”: as above. The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn unto the Saint and of intention.

§. 163.

XV. What We Need to Note Concerning Weekday Liturgies Celebrated during the Holy Pentecostarion (That Is, from Thomas Sunday until the Sunday of All Saints).[^244]

Since those Sundays are festal, whose entire service is repeated also on weekdays, therefore the troparia, kontakia, prokimena, and communion hymns of those Sunday services are likewise repeated on weekdays at the liturgical services. The Epistles and Gospels are read daily in course, corresponding to the festal Sundays. Concerning these Liturgies we note the following:

=== LEAF p239 ===
[Book Page 215]

1) At a weekday Liturgy during the Holy Pentecostarion with the service of a Minor Saint:
(α) Without an intention: The typical antiphons and the Beatitudes of the feast, the ode in course. After the entrance: the Sunday troparion (festal, of the resurrection),[^245] “Glory... now and forever”: the festal kontakion (of the Sunday).[^246] The Prokimenon and Communion Hymn of the festal Sunday: “Praise the Lord” (*Хвали́те*):[^247] In place of “It is truly right” (*Достойно есть*): “The Angel cried aloud,” and at Mid-Pentecost: the Heirmos of the 9th Ode until the Apodosis.
(β) With an intention: The Sunday troparion and the troparion of intention; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of intention; “Now and forever”: the festal kontakion. The Prokimenon, Epistle, and Gospel in course and of intention. The Communion Hymn: “Praise the Lord” (*Хвали́те*) and of intention. In place of “It is truly right” (*Достойно есть*): “The Angel cried aloud,” and at Mid-Pentecost: the Heirmos of Mid-Pentecost.

2) At a weekday Liturgy during the Holy Pentecostarion with the service of a Polyeleos Saint:
(α) Without an intention: The troparion of the Sunday and the troparion of the Saint; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion unto the Saint; “Now and forever”: the festal kontakion (of the Sunday). The Prokimenon, Epistle, and Gospel in course and unto the Saint. The Communion Hymn: “Praise the Lord” (*Хвали́те*) and unto the Saint.
(β) With an intention: The festal troparion, the troparion unto the Saint and of intention; the kontakion of intention; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion unto the Saint; “Now and forever”: the festal kontakion (of the Sunday). The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn unto the Saint and of intention.

§. 164.

XVI. How the Liturgy is Celebrated on Weekdays if a Forefeast Occurs with a Minor Saint?

Unto the Minor Saint at the Liturgy nothing is taken. There are taken: the typical antiphons. After the entrance: the troparion of the forefeast; “Glory... now and forever”: the kontakion of the forefeast. The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the day (in course).
If there is also a service of some intention, then it is taken thus: The troparion of the forefeast and the troparion of intention; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of intention; “Now and forever”: of the forefeast. The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the day and of intention.

=== LEAF p240 ===
[Book Page 216]

§. 165.

XVII. How the Liturgy is Celebrated if on Weekdays a Feast of the Mother of God or of the Lord Should Occur?

Feasts of the Mother of God do not have proper antiphons, and therefore on those feasts the typical antiphons are taken. The Beatitudes from the canon: Odes 3 and 6 on 8.
Feasts of the Lord, however, have their proper antiphons with the final refrains of the feast. After the entrance: the troparion of the feast; “Glory... now and forever”: the kontakion of the feast. The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, Magnification, and Communion Hymn of the feast.

§. 166.

XVIII. How the Liturgy is Celebrated on Weekdays if within a Feast a Minor Saint Should Occur?

Unto the Minor Saint nothing is taken. The typical antiphons; the Beatitudes of the feast on 4, of the Saint on 4; at the entrance, if it is an afterfeast of a Feast of the Lord, we sing “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) and the final refrain of the feast. After the entrance: the troparion of the feast; “Glory... now and forever”: the kontakion of the feast. The Epistle and Gospel of the day (in course), but the Prokimenon and Communion Hymn of the feast. Instead of “It is truly right,” the Heirmos of the 9th Ode with the refrain of the feast is sung.
If the service is also one of various intentions, then after the entrance it is taken thus: The troparion of the feast, the troparion of the service of intention; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of the service of intention; “Now and forever”: the kontakion of the feast. The Epistle and Gospel of the day and of the service of intention.

§. 167.

XIX. How the Liturgy is Celebrated on Weekdays if within a Feast there is a Middle Saint?

The antiphons are taken as was stated above. After the entrance: the troparion of the feast and the troparion of the Saint; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of the Saint; “Now and forever”: the kontakion of the feast. The Prokimenon of the feast and of the Saint. The Epistle and Gospel of the day (in course) and of the Saint. Instead of “It is truly right,” the Heirmos of the feast. The Communion Hymn of the feast and of the Saint.

---

=== LEAF p241 ===
[Book Page 217]

(p. 217)

§. 168.

XX. How is the Liturgy Celebrated on Weekdays if a Great Saint Occurs Within a Feast?

If the Saint hath a Polyeleos, then it is taken thus: The typical antiphons. At the Beatitudes, Ode 3 of the feast on 4 and Ode 6 of the Saint on 4. After the entrance: the troparion of the feast and the troparion of the Saint; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of the Saint; “Now and forever”: the kontakion of the feast. The Prokimenon of the feast and of the Saint. The Epistle and Gospel of the Saint (while that in course of the day is read on the day before). In place of “It is truly right,” the Heirmos of the feast. The Communion Hymn of the feast and of the Saint.[^248]
If there is also a service of various intention, then after the entrance: the troparion of the feast, the troparion of the Saint, and the troparion of the intention; the kontakion of the Saint; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): the kontakion of the intention; “Now and forever”: the kontakion of the feast. The Prokimenon of the feast, of the Saint, and of the intention. The Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the Saint and of the intention.[^249]

§. 169.

XXI. How is the Liturgy Celebrated on Weekdays if the Apodosis of a Feast Occurs?

The typical antiphons; at the Beatitudes, Ode 9 from both canons on 8. After the entrance: the troparion of the feast; “Glory, now and forever”: the kontakion of the feast. The Prokimenon and Communion Hymn of the feast. The Epistle and Gospel of the day (in course). In place of “It is truly right,” the Heirmos of the ode from the festal canon. The festal dismissal.

§. 170.

XXII. When is it Permitted to Celebrate the Liturgy of the Departed in Black Vestments?

There is a distinction between the service for the departed (*pro defunctis*) and the “service of the departed” (*missa defunctorum*).[^250]

=== LEAF p242 ===
[Book Page 218]

(p. 218)

I. As regards the Liturgy for the departed: the priest is permitted to celebrate it every day, that is, he takes and completes the service of the feast, or of the Sunday, or of the day, or of the Saint in white vestments without any litany for the departed, yet nevertheless applies the special fruits of the Liturgy (*fructus speciales*) unto the departed. This Liturgy also in its external aspect is dedicated unto the Sunday, Feast, or Saint, and only in its internal ministerial application (*in applicatione*) is for the departed. It is celebrated without any regard unto the feast day.
II. The “service of the departed” is celebrated specifically for the departed in black vestments. It hath proper troparia (“With profound wisdom,” etc.), a proper: Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel,[^251] Communion Hymn, and Dismissal. In place of “It is truly right,” there is sung: “Save those who hope,” etc. Into the Litany of Peace are inserted 2 petitions for the departed; and after the litany “Let us all say,” the litany “Have mercy on us, O God” (*Помилуй насъ Боже*) is sung; and at the conclusion of the holy Liturgy a Pannychis[^252] for the departed is sung.[^253]
To celebrate the Liturgy for the departed in black vestments, according to the decree of His Excellency, our Bishop Julius, given under No. 4227:1897, is not permitted in the Eparchy of Mukachevo on the following days: 1) On Sundays and on decreetal feasts. 2) On Polyeleos feasts (yet on Doxology feasts it is permitted). 3) On the Paramon [Eve] of the Nativity of Christ and of Theophany, when the Royal Hours are celebrated. 4) From Lazarus Saturday unto Thomas Sunday. 5) Throughout the entire Week of the Exaltation of the Precious Cross and in the Week of the Sunday of the Veneration of the Cross, because the precious Cross is exposed for the sake of the Passion of Christ. 6) In Great Lent, in the Cathedral Church at the main Altar-Table, the celebration of the holy Liturgy for the departed in black vestments is permitted only on Saturdays, yet at the side Altar-Tables and in other

=== LEAF p243 ===
[Book Page 219]

(p. 219)

churches it is not permitted to celebrate the holy Liturgy for the departed in black vestments only on such a day when a feast is celebrated. If anyone on prohibited days should wish to serve a Liturgy for the departed, the Liturgy is served not of the departed (in black vestments), but for the departed (in white vestments). The Liturgy of the departed in black vestments is celebrated as is written in the Sluzhebnik (p. 497). In the Sluzhebnik on pp. 488–496 are set forth the services of the departed for every day of the week. The service of the departed *praesente cadavere* [in the presence of the body of the deceased] may be served on any day.

#### ARTICLE II.

Concerning Liturgical Extraordinary Occurrences.

§. 171.

Which are the Most Notable Extraordinary Occurrences in Holy Liturgies?

1. The holy Liturgy hath its proper beginning (*initium proprium*),[^254] namely: “Blessed is the kingdom,” etc., and thus the Litany of Peace; but if the holy Liturgy is combined with Vespers, then “Blessed is the kingdom” is taken, “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) 3 times, etc.
2. “Heavenly King” (*Царю̀ небе́сный*) before the beginning of the holy Liturgy with the Royal Doors opened is sung 3 times: (α) On the Sunday of the Descent of the Holy Spirit; (β) At the first holy Liturgy of a newly ordained presbyter; and (γ) If a holy Liturgy is celebrated for invoking the help of the Holy Spirit. We do not say “Heavenly King” (*Царю̀ небе́сный*) from the Sunday of Pascha until the Descent of the Holy Spirit, but instead of it we take from the Sunday of Pascha until the Ascension “Christ is risen,” and from the Ascension until the Descent of the Holy Spirit “Thou Who didst ascend into the heavens, O God.”

=== LEAF p244 ===
[Book Page 220]

(p. 220)

3. From the Sunday of Pascha until the Ascension, after “Blessed is the kingdom,” we sing “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) 3 times, and only then follows the Litany of Peace.
4. Festal antiphons are appointed for: Pascha, the Ascension, the Descent of the Holy Spirit, the Transfiguration, the Exaltation, the Nativity of Christ, Theophany, and Palm Sunday. Festal antiphons are sung on all those Master Feasts which have their own antiphons, and that—as was said—only on the very first day of the feast.[^255] Daily antiphons[^256] are taken at weekday Liturgies when a Minor or Middle Saint occurs on a weekday. The typical antiphons (“Bless the Lord, O my soul” (*Благослови душе моя Господа*) and “Praise the Lord, O my soul” (*Хвалѝ, дꙋшѐ моя̀, Го́спода...*)) and the Beatitudes (“In Thy kingdom when Thou comest”) are taken: a) On all Sundays of the entire year (except Palm Sunday, Bright Week, the Wednesday before Ascension, and the Sunday of the Descent of the Holy Spirit, as well as the Master Feasts having proper antiphons). b) On all forefeasts and afterfeasts. c) On all Marian feasts. d) And on all Polyeleos and Doxology feasts.[^257] As regards the Beatitudes, in the Horologion we have only the refrains, and between these refrains are placed the troparia corresponding unto the feast or tone. For the Sundays of the entire year, the troparia at the Beatitudes are set forth in the Oktoechos. On feasts, forefeasts, and afterfeasts, as well as on feasts of Saints, the troparia at the Beatitudes are taken from the canon of those feasts and are sung in the tone of the troparia. It is necessary to note here that on a Polyeleos feast there are taken at the Beatitudes usually Ode 3 and Ode 6 from the canon of the Saint on 8, and on afterfeasts the ode in course on 8. But what is an ode in course? There are 9 odes in a canon (Ode 2 doth not exist here), and if, for example, the Nativity of the Theotokos should be on Monday, then at the weekday Liturgy on Tuesday we take at the Beatitudes Ode 1, on Wednesday Ode 3, on Thursday Ode 4, on Friday Ode 5, etc.
5. In place of the Trisagion, “As many as have been baptized into Christ” is sung on the Nativity of Christ, Theophany, on Lazarus Saturday, on Great Saturday, on the Sunday of Pascha, throughout the entire Bright Week, and on the

=== LEAF p245 ===
[Book Page 221]

(p. 221)

Descent of the Holy Spirit (on the Saturday before Pentecost and on the very feast of Pentecost itself).
6. “Before Thy Cross we bow down in worship, O Master” is sung: (α) On the Exaltation of the Precious Cross[^258] and throughout the entire afterfeast of this feast; (β) On the Sunday of the Veneration of the Cross and throughout its entire week;[^259] (γ) On the feast of the “Procession of the Precious Wood” (August 1); and (δ) On the very apodosis of the feast of the Exaltation of the Precious Cross.[^260] On these feasts the holy Cross is brought out into the middle of the temple upon the Tetrapod for the veneration and kissing of the faithful; and the bringing forth of this Cross takes place after the Great Doxology at Matins.[^261]
7. On Great Saturday after the Epistle, in place of “Alleluia,” there is sung: “Arise, O God, judge the earth, for Thou shalt inherit among all the nations” with its verses. And when they sing “Arise, O God,” then the priest and deacons strip off the black vestments and vest in white, and thus the Gospel is read.
8. On the Sunday of Pascha the holy Gospel according to John (“In the beginning was the Word,” etc.) is read in 12 sections in various languages

=== LEAF p246 ===
[Book Page 222]

(p. 222)

by multiple priests; and at each section of the Gospel they strike the bells.[^262]
9. Because of Sundays in course—from the Sunday of All Saints unto the Sunday of Zacchaeus—there are 32, which sometimes are lacking, and sometimes are in excess, therefore concerning the Epistles and Gospels—they are taken in the following manner: That is, if they are lacking: then the 28th, 29th, and 30th are repeated; yet if they are in excess: then the 28th, 29th, and 30th are omitted, but in such wise that the 32nd (of Zacchaeus) is always taken. Sometimes these Sundays in course are also supplemented by festal Sundays (that is, by the Sunday of the Holy Forefathers, the Sunday before the Nativity, the Sunday after the Nativity, the Sunday before the Enlightenment [Theophany], and the Sunday after the Enlightenment).
10. Litanies of various intentions are inserted into the Litany of Peace after the petition “For those who travel by sea, land, and air” and before the petition: “That we may be delivered.” And in the Triple Litany [Litany of Fervent Supplication] after the petition: “Again let us pray for our God-loving Bishop” and before the petition: “For the people here present.” But if a Liturgy for the departed is celebrated—the litany of the departed: “Have mercy on us, O God” (*Помилуй насъ Боже*) is inserted after the exclamation of the Triple Litany and before the litany of the catechumens (“Pray, ye catechumens”).
11. In place of the Cherubic Hymn (“Let us who mystically represent the Cherubim”), on Great Thursday there is sung: “Of Thy Mystical Supper,” etc.; on Great Saturday: “Let all mortal flesh keep silence” (*Да молчитъ*); and at the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts: “Now the Heavenly Powers.”
12. In place of “It is truly right,” there is taken on Great Feasts the Heirmos of the 9th Ode with the festal refrain, and that is called the *zadostoinyk* (that is, in place of the *dostoinyk* [hymn to the Theotokos]). In Great Lent at the Liturgy of St. Basil the Great, the Theotokion of Tone 8: “All creation rejoices in thee.” On Great Saturday: “Weep not for Me, O Mother.” At the Liturgy of the departed: “Save those who hope in thee, O Mother,” etc.
13. From the Sunday of Pascha until the Ascension, the priest at the holy Liturgy exclaims “With the fear of God” and “Save, O God, Thy people” (*Спаси Боже*) thus: “With the fear of God and with faith draw near: Christ is risen,” and again: “Save, O God, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance: Christ is risen,” unto which the cantor, both in place of “Blessed is He that comes” and in place of “Many years, O Master,” sings “Christ is risen.” From the Ascension until its Apodosis, in place of “We have seen the true light,” there is sung: “Be Thou exalted above the heavens, O God.”

=== LEAF p247 ===
[Book Page 223]

(p. 223)

14. At the Liturgy of St. Basil the Great, in place of “All creation rejoices in thee,” there is sung on Great Thursday “Come, O ye faithful, let us enjoy the Master’s hospitality,” and on Great Saturday “Weep not for Me, O Mother.”
15. In place of “Blessed be the name of the Lord,” from the Sunday of Pascha until its Apodosis, “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) is sung.
16. On Great Saturday straightway after the Prayer Behind the Ambo, the priest comes unto the Tetrapod and blesses the 5 loaves, wheat, wine, and oil, exclaiming: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and reads the litany prayer: “O Lord Jesus Christ our God, Who didst bless,” etc., and only then censes and sings: “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (*Буди имя Господне*) 3 times. But if there is an offering [kolyvo], then after “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (*Буди имя Господне*) the blessing thereof takes place thus: The cantor saith “Our Father,” unto which the priest saith the exclamation: “For Thine is the kingdom,” etc.; after Amen,[^263] the choir sings the troparia of the Sunday or feast, while the priest censes. At the conclusion of the singing, the priest exclaims “Let us pray to the Lord,” and saith the prayer over the kolyvo (“O Lord, Who hast completed all things by Thy word”); after this he sprinkles the offering with holy water, saying: “These gifts are blessed and sanctified by the sprinkling of this holy water, in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit. Amen,” and straightway wishes many years, saying: “Unto those who have adorned these gifts, grant, O Lord, peace, health, and blessing for many years.”
17. After “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (*Буди имя Господне*) and before the dismissal, the antidoron is distributed, and on litiya feasts at the same time there takes place also the Myrovanije,[^264] that is, anointing with the oil[^265] blessed at

=== LEAF p248 ===
[Book Page 224]

(p. 224)

the litiya. During the distribution of the “antidoron” and the Myrovanije, the priest greets the faithful thus: “Christ is among us!”—and from the Sunday of Pascha until the Apodosis: “Christ is risen!” From the Sunday of Pascha until the Ascension, when the antidoron is distributed, at the same time there takes place also the kissing of the Cross.
18. At his first Liturgy, the newly ordained priest distributes the blessing after “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (*Буди имя Господне*) and before the dismissal, laying his hands upon the people and saying thus: “The Lord bless thee and keep thee; the Lord make His face shine upon thee and be gracious unto thee, in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit. Amen.” Or: “I lay my hands upon the servant (or handmaid) of God, that he (or she) may be whole, in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit. Amen.”
19. On feasts at the dismissals, in place of “More honorable than the Cherubim,” there is said the refrain of the feast and the Heirmos of the 9th Ode. Besides this we note that the festal dismissal is taken not only at the Liturgy, but also at public services, and besides this also at such sanctifications and blessings as do not have a mystical festal event. But at the Holy Mysteries administered on Master Feasts and afterfeasts, the festal dismissal is not taken. A burial hath its own proper dismissal. In these latter cases neither is the Heirmos taken at the dismissal, for it is taken only at public sacred actions. In Bright Week (through Saturday inclusive) there is no other dismissal except the Dismissal of Pascha, and that in all cases without exception, even at the Holy Mysteries.[^266]
20. In Bright Week from Sunday Matins even unto the end of the Saturday Liturgy, the Royal Doors, the Deacon’s Doors, and the servers’ doors remain open as a sign that Christ by His resurrection hath opened unto us the doors of the heavenly kingdom. Finally we also note that the priest on Great Friday after Vespers, when the laying of the Plashchanytsia into the tomb hath already been completed, exposes the Holy Gifts from the tabernacle upon the holy Altar-Table, where they shall remain exposed even unto Sunday Matins.[^267]

=== LEAF p249 ===
[Book Page 225]

(p. 225)

### APPENDIX.

§. 172.

What is Necessary to Note Concerning the Eves [Paramons] of the Nativity of Christ and of Theophany?

I. If the Paramon [Eve][^268] of the Nativity of Christ occurs on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, or Friday, then on that very day there is a fast, and the service: that is, the Royal Hours and Vespers with the Liturgy of St. Basil the Great are celebrated on the Paramon itself in the morning at the 10th hour. But if the Paramon of the Nativity of Christ occurs on Saturday or Sunday, then the service is divided: that is, the fast is transferred unto Friday, the Royal Hours we sing on Friday, and on the Paramon itself there is the Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom without Vespers. Vespers we sing on the Paramon itself in the afternoon at the 3rd hour, while the Liturgy of St. Basil the Great without Vespers is celebrated on the Nativity of Christ itself.[^269]
II. If the Paramon[^270] of Theophany occurs on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, or Friday, then on the Paramon itself there is a fast, in the morning are celebrated the Royal Hours, and Vespers with the Liturgy of St. Basil the Great; and the Great Blessing of Water takes place after the Liturgy of Basil. But if the Paramon of Theophany occurs on Saturday or Sunday, then the service is divided, and that in this wise: that on the Paramon itself there is not a strict fast, but only abstinence from meat and dairy products, and the fast of the Paramon is not transferred unto Friday, but on this Friday on account of the compact week the eating of meat is permitted. The Royal Hours nevertheless are celebrated on Friday. On the Paramon itself in the morning is celebrated the Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom without Vespers, while Vespers takes place on the Paramon in the afternoon at the 3rd hour, at which time also the blessing of water takes place. And the Liturgy of St. Basil the Great without Vespers is celebrated on the very feast of Theophany itself.

=== LEAF p250 ===
[Book Page 226]

(p. 226)

§. 173.

What is Necessary to Know Concerning the Extraordinary Occurrences of the Remaining Feasts?

I. If the feast of the “Three Holy Hierarchs” occurs on Meatfare Saturday, that is, of All Souls: we sing their service on Friday in all things without fail. But if the temple is dedicated unto them: then the service of the departed is sung on the preceding Saturday; and on Saturday we sing the service of the Saints only.
II. If the feast of the Meeting of the Lord occurs on Meatfare Saturday, that is, of All Souls, the service of the departed is sung on the preceding Saturday or on Meatfare Thursday. And on Saturday the entire service of the feast alone is sung without fail. If the Meeting of the Lord occurs on the Monday of the first week of Lent, the service of the feast is sung on Cheesefare Sunday.
III. If the Finding of the Precious Head of St. John the Baptist occurs on Meatfare Saturday, that is, of All Souls, the entire service of the Forerunner is sung on Meatfare Friday. But if it occurs on the Monday of the first week of holy Lent, it is sung on Cheesefare Sunday. If it occurs on any day of the 1st week of Lent, we sing the service of the feast either on Cheesefare Sunday or on the Saturday of the 1st week of Lent.
IV. If the 40 Martyrs occurs on the Thursday of the Great Canon, we sing their service on the Tuesday before that same week. If this feast occurs on the Saturday of the Akathist, we sing their service on the 5th Sunday of holy Lent.
V. If the feast of the holy Great-Martyr George occurs on Great Friday, or on Great Saturday, or on the Sunday of Pascha itself, we sing his service on the Monday of Bright Week together with the service of the day.

§. 174.

What is Necessary to Note Concerning the Annunciation?

The feast of the Annunciation of the Most-Holy Theotokos begins from the Thursday of the 3rd week of holy Lent, and extends unto the Wednesday of Bright Week. An extensive instruction concerning this feast is found in the Triodia and in the Triodion or Menaion; here however in brief we set down only the following notes:
I. It is fitting to know that if the Annunciation of the Most-Holy Theotokos occurs on the 3rd, 4th, or 5th Sunday of holy Lent, Small Vespers is sung on Saturday according to the instruction set down in the Triodion.

---

=== LEAF p251 ===
[Book Page 227]

(p. 227)

Then according to custom Great Vespers with litiya (All-Night Vigil). At Matins on Sunday, the Gradual of the tone. The Prokimenon and Gospel of the feast. The Great Doxology, and the rest as in the Anthologion [Triphologion]. The Divine Liturgy of St. Basil the Great without fail. In place of “It is truly right,” we sing the Heirmos: “As of a living [ark].” On the 3rd Sunday of the Fast, in place of the Trisagion, we say: “Before Thy Cross.”

II. If the Annunciation occurs on Monday of the 4th, or 5th, or 6th week of the Holy Fast: On Sunday there is Small Vespers, at which the stichera of the feast are on 4, etc. Great Vespers is celebrated with litiya (usually before sunrise on Monday). Readings of the feast: 3. At the blessing of loaves, the troparion of the feast 3 times. At Matins, the Polyeleos and Gospel of the feast. The Small Doxology, and the rest of the service as is prescribed for a fasting day in course in the Anthologion [Triphologion]. The Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom with Vespers, and the rest.

III. If the Annunciation occurs on the fasting days of the 4th, 5th, and 6th weeks of the Fast, that is to say: on Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, and Friday, then on the forefeast in the evening (if Wednesday or Friday) we begin Vespers without a kathisma. At “Lord, I have cried,” we set 10 verses, and we sing 5 from the Triodion of the day, and 3 of the feast, repeating two. Entrance. Readings of the day and of the feast: 3; (1st of Genesis: “Jacob went forth” (*И҆зы́ди І҆а́кѡвъ...*) [Gen. 28:10]; 2nd of the Prophet Ezekiel: “It shall be from the eighth day” (*Бꙋ́детъ ѿ днѐ ѻ҆сма́гѡ...*) [Ezek. 43:27]; 3rd of Proverbs: “Wisdom hath built” [Prov. 9:1]). Then: “Let my prayer arise.” And 3 great prostrations. And the rest of the Presanctified Gifts.

If, however, there is no Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts (if the forefeast be on Tuesday or Thursday), at “Lord, I have cried” (*Господи воззвахъ*): prosomia stichera from the Triodion, and of the feast 5. Entrance. Prokimenon and readings of the day and of the feast: 3 (of the Theotokos). And immediately the Royal Doors are closed. Then: “Vouchsafe, O Lord.” “Let us complete our evening prayer.” At the aposticha, the stichera of the Triodion, etc. After “Now Thou lettest depart”: the troparion of the feast. Litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and 3 great prostrations, and the dismissal. Before sunrise Great Compline is sung with litiya and the customary vigil prayers, as was prescribed on the feast of the Nativity of Christ. At the blessing of loaves, the troparion of the feast 3 times. At “God is the Lord” (*Богъ Господь*): the troparion of the feast 3 times. After the 1st kathisma, both Sessional Hymns of the Triodion without a litany. After the 2nd kathisma, a litany and the Sessional Hymn of the feast. The Polyeleos and the refrain of the feast in the Heirmologion. The Gospel of the feast, etc. Small Doxology without chanting. Litany: “Let us complete.” At the aposticha, the stichera of the Triodion. “Glory,” and “Now and,” of the feast. After “It is good to give praise,” the troparion of the feast once. Litany: “Have mercy on us, O God.” And at the end 3 great prostrations, saying only: “God, be merciful unto me a sinner.”

=== LEAF p252 ===
[Book Page 228]

(p. 228)

And the dismissal. And the First Hour with a kathisma, the troparion and kontakion of the feast. And at the end 3 great prostrations and the prayer: “O Christ, the true Light.” In this manner also we say the remaining Hours. After this the Beatitudes briskly without chanting and without prostrations, and the rest. The Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom together with Vespers.

IV. If the Annunciation occurs on Saturday of the 3rd, 4th, and 5th weeks of the Fast: On Friday, Vespers together with the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts. The stichera of the day and of the feast are sung. And the readings: first of the day, and of the feast 5. And “Let my prayer arise.” And the rest of the Presanctified Gifts. On Saturday before sunrise Great Compline is sung with litiya and with the customary prayers of the All-Night Vigil. At Matins: the entire service is of the feast. We sing also the Great Doxology. At the Liturgy of Chrysostom without Vespers, all is of the feast.

V. If the Annunciation of the Most-Holy Theotokos occurs on Thursday of the 5th week of the Fast, when the Great Canon is sung: For the sake of the feast the Great Canon of our holy Father Andrew is transferred. And we sing it on Tuesday of that same week with the triodion of Tuesday. And the apostolical triodion we sing with the feast of the Annunciation. And at the conclusion, the Katavasia of the Triodion.

VI. If the Annunciation of the Most-Holy Theotokos occurs on Friday of the 5th week, we sing the forefeast on Wednesday in course, while that of the Saint we sing at Compline.

VII. If the Annunciation occurs on Great Friday:[^271] Vespers of the Annunciation falling on Holy and Great Friday is begun on Great Thursday with the Divine Liturgy of St. Basil the Great, that is to say: at “Lord, I have cried,” the stichera of the day on 5 (“Now the assembly of the Jews convene”), and of the Feast of the Annunciation on 5 (“The eternal counsel”); “Glory,” of the day (“The brood of vipers”); “Now and forever,” of the feast (“The Angel was sent”). Entrance with the Gospel. The Prokimenon and readings of the day, and of the feast 3 (“Jacob went forth,” “Thus saith the Lord,” “Wisdom hath built herself a house”). Small litany, the Trisagion, Prokimenon, Epistle, Gospel of the day, Matthew, peric. 107: “The Lord said unto His disciples: Ye know that after two days is the Feast of Pascha” [Matt. 26:1–2], and the remaining order of the Liturgy of Basil, which according to custom is to be celebrated in colored vestments.

Great Compline, which formerly was celebrated for this feast with the sweet chanting of “God is with us,” is now not sung, nor is litiya celebrated, but only Matins itself of the Annunciation, which otherwise ought to be celebrated early on the very feast day, is now in the eve-

=== LEAF p253 ===
[Book Page 229]

(p. 229)

ning of Great Thursday combined with the Passion [Gospels] of Christ in the following order, that is to say: After the bell is rung at the seventh hour of the evening, the priest, having vested himself in an epitrachelion, neither light nor black, but of some other color, proclaims before the Royal Doors: “Blessed is our God.” Then after the Psalms and the Litany of Peace, at “God is the Lord,” the troparion of the feast: “Today is the fountainhead of our salvation” twice; “Glory, now and forever”: “When the glorious disciples.” Small litany, and after this immediately: “That He would vouchsafe unto us...” and the 1st Passion Gospel is read in its proper order; likewise also according to the rubric of the Triodion, the remaining Gospels are read even unto the 7th Gospel inclusive. After the 7th Gospel, the Gradual, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4: “From my youth” (*Ѡ̂тъ ю́ности моея́*); the Prokimenon of the feast and the Gospel of the feast, Luke, peric. 4: “Mary arose” [Luke 1:39]. This is read according to the custom of Matins upon the holy Altar-Table; and upon its conclusion is sung: “Glory to Thee, O Lord.” The priest immediately proclaims: “That He would vouchsafe unto us...” and having turned unto the people at the Royal Doors reads the 8th Passion Gospel; after this Psalm 50 [LXX Ps. 50]. The sticheron of the feast: “Gabriel announces good tidings”; “O God, save Thy people”; after these, the canon of the feast with the Katavasia of the feast, and at those odes where a triodion is sung, the Katavasia of the Triodion, and so forth, as the Typikon prescribes; at the 9th Ode “More honorable” (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю*) is not sung, but the refrain of the feast: “O earth, announce good tidings,” the Exaposteilarion of the feast, “Glory,” of the day, “Now and forever,” of the feast. — After this the 9th Passion Gospel. At the Praises, the idiomela stichera of the day; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): “Already the reed is dipped”; “Now and forever,” of the feast: “Let the heavens rejoice”; then the 10th Passion Gospel, and after it the Small Doxology; then the litany “Let us complete,” and after it the 11th Passion Gospel; and immediately at the aposticha, the stichera of the feast; “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): of the day; “Now and forever”: of the feast; then the 12th Passion Gospel. — “It is good to give praise” (*Бла́го є̂́сть*); “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*); the troparion of the day: “Thou hast redeemed us...”; “Glory, now and forever,” of the feast: “Today is the fountainhead of our salvation.” Litany: “Have mercy on us, O God.” “Wisdom,” and the Dismissal. (The Typikon prescribes that the First Hour be celebrated immediately after Matins, but on account of the length of this Matins it will be more convenient to celebrate the First Hour together with the other Hours at its proper time. — Furthermore it is fitting to know that after each Passion Gospel the customary ringing of bells is to take place, but at the festal Gospel of this Matins it is not proper to ring, but only when the 8th Passion Gospel is read after it.)

In the morning on Great Friday at the sixth hour, the tolling is given not with the klepalo, but for the sake of the feast with the bells

=== LEAF p254 ===
[Book Page 230]

(p. 230)

for the celebration of the Hours; and the priest, having come unto the church in the very same vestments wherein he celebrated the Matins of the Annunciation with the Passion [Gospels] of Christ, celebrates the 1st, 3rd, 6th, and 9th Hours with their readings, Epistles, and Gospels, as is written in the Triodion; and at these Hours there are sung the troparia and kontakia not only of the day, but also of the feast. After the Hours, the Beatitudes without chanting. — The Creed is omitted, because there will be a Liturgy. After “Pardon, remit,” and “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*): the kontakion of the day; “Glory, now and forever”: the kontakion of the feast. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) 40 times. “All-Holy Trinity,” and the Dismissal.

Although on Great Friday the Divine Liturgy generally doth not take place, nevertheless for the sake of the solemn feast of the Annunciation there is a departure from this rule, and on the Annunciation falling on Great Friday the Liturgy of Chrysostom with Vespers is to be celebrated in the following order: When at the 10th hour before noon the peal of bells is given not with the klepalo, but for the sake of the feast with the chiming of bells, then the priest, having vested in festal vestments, begins the Liturgy of Chrysostom with Vespers, at which at “Lord, I have cried,” there are taken the stichera of the day on 6 (“All creation is changed”), and of the Feast of the Annunciation on 4 (“In the sixth month”)... “Glory,” of the day (“O how the lawless assembly”); “Now and forever,” of the feast (“The Angel was sent”); then the entrance with the Gospel, Prokimenon, readings of the day (“The Lord said”) and of the feast 2 readings (“Moses entered,” “The Lord created me” [Prov. 8:22]). — After these, the small litany, “For holy art Thou, O our God.” The Trisagion, Prokimenon, Epistle, and Gospel first of the feast, Luke, peric. 3: “In the sixth month the Angel was sent” [Luke 1:26], then the Gospel of the day, Matthew, peric. 110: “When morning was come, they took counsel” [Matt. 27:1]... and so forth in course the Liturgy of Chrysostom; at the Commemoration of the Theotokos [“Especially”] the Heirmos of the feast is sung: “As of a living Ark of God”; then the great bell is tolled, and from that time the bells fall silent even unto the most bright Resurrection of Christ; — after the Prayer behind the Ambo and after “Blessed be the name of the Lord,” when the choir begins to sing the aposticha stichera: “When Joseph took Thee down dead from the Tree,” then the priest divests himself of the festal vestments, for the feast of the Annunciation is already completed, and having vested in black vestments, censes the Plashchanytsia upon the Altar-Table, and after “Now Thou lettest depart,” taking it upon his shoulders, goes forth through the Royal Doors and carries it around the church according to custom in procession, with the chanting of: “The noble Joseph” (*Благообразный Іѡсифъ*); and from that time it will be necessary always to sound the klepalo and not to ring the bells. And the priest, having returned into the church, lays the Plashchanytsia into the tomb, or upon another prepared place adorned with burning candles, and having censed it, having ascended the pul-

=== LEAF p255 ===
[Book Page 231]

(p. 231)

pit, or upon the ambo, preaches the holy sermon: “Concerning the Passion of Christ”; after which, having kissed the holy Plashchanytsia with the people, he makes the final dismissal: “May He Who for the sake of us men...”

Furthermore it is fitting to know that although on Great Friday no food whatsoever is permitted unto adults and healthy people, nevertheless when the Annunciation falls on Great Friday, then upon the conclusion of the Divine Liturgy with Vespers the eating of a little dry food is permitted for the sake of the feast.

Finally on that same Great Friday, at the seventh hour of the evening the klepalo is sounded, and in the church before the holy Plashchanytsia Small Compline is sung, with the canon on the lamentation and compassion of the Most-Holy Virgin Theotokos; and thus the divine services of this Great Friday are concluded; the order of Great Saturday, however, is celebrated as indicated in the Triodion.

VIII. If the Annunciation occurs on the Great Sunday of Pascha: On Great Saturday in the evening, after “Gladsome Light,” the Prokimenon is not said. But immediately the priest: “Wisdom”; and the 1st reading of the day: one Paremia. Then of the feast: 5. Then also the remaining readings of the day according to their order, etc. The Divine Liturgy of St. Basil the Great. On Sunday at Matins, after “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) and after the 4 verses, the priest having chanted loudly the last time: “Christ is risen,” the doors being opened, we enter into the temple chanting: “And unto those in the tombs.” And after the priest enters into the sanctuary, we say “Glory,” and we sing the troparion of the feast; “Now and forever”: the same. Then the Great Litany. After the 6th Ode of the canon, the Prokimenon of the feast. “Let every breath” (*Всякое дыханіе*) and the Gospel of the feast. Then “Having beheld the resurrection of Christ” 3 times, and the rest see in the Triodion. At the Divine Liturgy, when the time comes to read the Holy Gospel, the priest reads the Gospel of Pascha by 12 stases. Upon the conclusion of this, a single deacon reads the Gospel of the Annunciation according to custom, as always, and for that Gospel the bell is not struck; and in course the Liturgy of Chrysostom. In place of “It is truly right” (*Достойно есть*): the Heirmos of Pascha. The remaining rubrics concerning that feast see in the Triodion.

§. 175.

Concerning Processions.

Processions among us are performed thus: after the Divine Liturgy, after “Blessed be the name of the Lord,” the priest proclaims on the ambo: “Let us go forth in peace”; whereunto the choir sings: “In the name of the Lord,” and they go forth outside the church. In front goes the cross-bearer; behind him go the people with processional banners, and thus goes the cantor, and after him the priest, holding the holy Cross in his hands, with candle-bearers preceding.

=== LEAF p256 ===
[Book Page 232]

(p. 232)

Processions among us take place either around the church, or are made unto designated places. Around the church, processions take place on Bright Monday, on Monday of the Descent of the Holy Spirit, on Holy and Great Friday at Vespers, at the Matins of the Resurrection of Christ on Easter Sunday, during a Jubilee, and on the Patronal Feast of the Temple.[^272] Unto designated places, processions take place on the feast of Theophany, when the water is sanctified, or for the blessing of the sown fields on a Sunday near the feast of the holy Evangelist Mark, or for the blessing of a cemetery, cross, house, school, church, etc., if these blessings or sanctifications are performed publicly.

At processions, when the people go forth from the church, the cantor sings “Our Father,” “O Theotokos Virgin, rejoice,” the troparion of the feast, or other suitable hymns.

At processions the Gospels also are read in such wise that the priest proclaims: “That He would vouchsafe unto us...” etc., after this “Peace be to all,” and so: “Wisdom! Stand upright! Let us hear the Holy Gospel,” etc. At those processions which are performed around the church,[^273] they are read only at the third circling around the church, and that:

1. On Bright Monday the following Gospels are read: from Matthew, peric. 115; Mark 70; Luke 112; John 63.
2. On Monday of the Descent of the Holy Spirit: from Matthew, peric. 47; Mark 59; Luke 56; John 53.
3. On Holy and Great Friday: from Matthew, peric. 113; Mark 69; Luke 111; John 62.[^274]
4. During a Jubilee, or on the Patronal Feast of the Temple, or generally at Prahonic Pilgrimages [Vidpusts]: from Matthew, peric. 20; Mark 51; Luke 88; John 1; or Matthew 17; Mark 7; Luke 33; John 65. Besides this:
5. At Theophany, when the blessing of water takes place at the river, the Gospels read are: from Matthew, peric. 6; Mark 2; Luke 10; John 3.
6. For the blessing of the sowing of seeds: from Matthew, peric. 52; Mark 17; Luke 66; John 20.

At those processions which are made unto designated places (as for example on the feast of Theophany, or for the bles-

=== LEAF p257 ===
[Book Page 233]

(p. 233)

sing of seeds), upon returning at the end of the procession “Glory to God in the highest” (*Слава во вышнихъ Богу*) is sung; but at ordinary processions around the church this Angelic Hymn is not sung, with the exception of a Jubilee or other occasion of thanksgiving.

When the priest with the people hath returned into the church, he chants the litany of the Midnight Office: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc., “Again we pray,” “That this city may be preserved,” etc. “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) 12 times. “That the voice of our supplication may be heard,” etc.; the exclamation: “Hear us, O God,” etc., and thus takes place the dismissal and the blessing.

## SECTION III.

§. 176.

Concerning Celebrations Following the Divine Liturgy in General.

Unto these celebrations belong: (α) The Rites of the Holy Mysteries, and (β) The Rites of Ecclesiastical Sanctifications and Blessings.

The Holy Mysteries of Christ, by means whereof, as through conduits, the power issuing from the sufferings of the Savior, that is to say, the grace merited upon the altar of the Cross, is to pass over unto us, are seven in number.

Ecclesiastical sanctifications and blessings, however, through which a sinner is either prepared for justification, or if he is already justified through the grace of God, this grace is increased in that person, in regard of number are not limited.

Nevertheless, between the Holy Mysteries and ecclesiastical sanctifications and blessings there is such a distinction, that: (a) The Holy Mysteries are instituted by Jesus Christ (*institutionis divinae* [*of divine institution*]), and are visible signs of invisible grace, which they have annexed unto them (*habent adnexam gratiam* [*they have grace annexed*]); whereas ecclesiastical blessings and sanctifications are instituted by the Church (*institutionis ecclesiasticae* [*of ecclesiastical institution*]), and although they too are visible signs, nevertheless they do not have grace annexed unto themselves (*non habent adnexam gratiam* [*they do not have grace annexed*]), but only prepare for it or increase it in men. — (b) Through the Holy Mysteries we receive the grace of God unfailingly “by the power of the completed action” (*ex opere operato* [*by the work worked*]), whereas through ecclesiastical sanctifications and blessings “by way of intercession” (*ex opere operantis* [*by the work of the worker*]), in consideration of the prayers and merits of the Church and of the interior preparation of our soul.

=== LEAF p258 ===
[Book Page 234]

(p. 234)

### CHAPTER I.

Concerning the Rites of the Holy Mysteries.[^275]

§. 177.

The Order of Holy Baptism.

The rite of Holy Baptism is divided into 3 parts: (α) Actions performed before approaching the water of Holy Baptism; (β) Actions performed at the water of Holy Baptism; (γ) Actions performed after Holy Baptism.[^276]

The first part of the rite of Holy Baptism corresponds unto the ancient catechumenate, and in brief is an aggregate of those actions of Holy Bap-

=== LEAF p259 ===
[Book Page 235]

(p. 235)

tism which before the bringing of the one to be baptized into the church are performed in the narthex. That is to say: the priest comes into the narthex vested in epitrachelion and phelonion, holding in his hands the holy Cross, where those who brought the child already await him. Here the priest first with great diligence inquires concerning the parents and concerning their faith; after this: “Who are the sponsors that receive the child from the holy font?”[^277] After this, bestowing a name upon the child, he signs his head with the sign of the holy Cross 3 times, saying: “In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit,” etc.; and having laid his right hand upon the head of the child, he recites the prayer: “In Thy name, O Lord...” etc. After this prayer follow the 3 exorcisms, — and immediately the 2nd prayer: “O Master, Lord, Who hast created man...” in which, when the priest speaks the words: “Drive out from him (or: from her) every evil and unclean spirit,” etc., he blows upon the forehead, the mouth, and the breast of the child (these words the priest speaks 3 times, and likewise blows 3 times); upon the conclusion of this, the prayer is concluded with the words: “...now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.” — After this prayer the priest turns the one to be baptized with his face toward the west, and commands the sponsors to do likewise, of whom he inquires 3 times in the present tense: “Dost thou renounce Satan, and all his works, and all his angels, and all his service, and all his pride?” etc., whereunto the catechumen or the sponsors answer: “I renounce him.” — After this the priest again inquires of them in the past tense: “Hast thou renounced Satan?” etc., and they answer: “I have renounced him.” And immediately the priest bids them to blow and spit upon the ground (that is, upon Satan); and when they have done this, again at the command of the priest they turn toward the east. — After this the priest inquires of them: “Dost thou unite thyself unto Christ?” whereunto they answer 3 times: “I do unite myself.” — This he inquires of them also in the past tense, saying: “Hast thou united thyself unto Christ?” whereunto they answer: “I have united myself.” Finally the priest inquires of them: “Dost thou believe in Christ?” and they answer: “I believe”; and entering into the nave, they recite the Creed. During this the priest, having laid the epitrachelion upon the one to be baptized, saith: “He shall enter into Thy house, he shall worship toward Thy holy temple,” etc., — and immediately — when he hath already entered into the nave — he saith: “Blessed is our God, Who desires that all men should be saved,” etc. And herewith the 1st Part is concluded.

=== LEAF p260 ===
[Book Page 236]

(p. 236)

In Part II the priest first at the prepared table loudly proclaims: “Let us pray unto the Lord,” and after this recites the prayer: “O Master, Lord our God,” etc. (After this prayer follows the consecration of the water and of the “oil of catechumens,” if such be not already present in church.) If, however, it is present, then after the prayer the priest begins to anoint the one to be baptized with the oil of catechumens (*oleum catechumenorum* [*oil of catechumens*]); and having taken of the holy oil with the thumb, he first makes the sign of the cross upon the forehead of the one to be baptized, reciting the prayer: “The servant of God is anointed...” etc.; — after this, anointing upon the breast, he saith: “For the healing of soul and body...” etc.; further, anointing upon the shoulders, he saith: “Unto zealously taking up the good yoke of Christ...”; anointing upon the ears, he saith: “Unto the hearing of faith...” etc.; anointing upon the hands (upon the palms), he saith: “Unto lifting up hands blamelessly...” etc.; and finally he anoints the feet, saying: “Unto his walking in the footsteps...” etc. — After the anointing, the priest wipes his thumb and the anointed parts of the child with paper and destroys the paper.

After this, having taken the consecrated water in a small vessel, he pours it upon the head of the one to be baptized cruciformly 3 times, whom the sponsors hold, and pronounces the formula of Baptism: “The servant of God (or: the handmaid of God) [Name] is baptized in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit. Amen.”[^278]

In Part III, which takes place after Baptism, the cantor reads Psalm 31 [LXX Ps. 31]: (“Blessed are they whose iniquities are forgiven,” etc.) After this the priest gives unto the baptized one the white “robe of righteousness,” whereunto the cantor recites the troparion: “Grant unto me a robe of light,” etc. After this the priest hands unto the newly baptized one or unto the sponsors into the right hand a burning candle, saying: “Receive this burning candle,” etc. After this he makes the Dismissal, beginning from: “Wisdom”; “More honorable.”

Among us the priest combines Chrismation with Baptism; and therefore after the prayer: “Receive this candle,” etc., he immediately loudly proclaims: “Let us pray unto the Lord,” and reads the prayer of Chrismation: “Blessed art Thou, O Lord God,” etc., and then the Dismissal is omitted. — But if by reason of grave necessity it should happen that a priest of the Eastern Rite baptizes the child of Latin parents, then the priest is not permitted to administer also Chrismation; for this Mystery among the faithful 

=== LEAF p261 ===
[Book Page 237]

(p. 237)

---


of the Latin Rite is celebrated by the Bishop alone. And therefore after the prayer: “Receive this burning candle,” he makes the Dismissal.[^279]

§. 178.

The Order of the Holy Mystery of Chrismation.

If a child of the Greek Rite was baptized by a priest of the Latin Rite, then the priest, vested in epitrachelion and phelonion, comes unto the tetrapod, upon which are laid the cross, the Gospel-Book, and burning candles, and administers Holy Chrismation unto him, saying thus: “Blessed is the Kingdom,” etc., whereunto the cantor speaks the sticheron in Tone 6: “O Heavenly King,” and so follows the Litany of Chrismation: “In peace let us pray unto the Lord.” After the Litany the priest loudly proclaims: “Let us pray unto the Lord,” and reads with the greatest attentiveness loudly the prayer: “Blessed art Thou, O Lord God Almighty,” etc. After the prayer, having dipped his finger into the holy Myron,[^280] he cruciformly anoints the newly-baptized one upon the forehead, upon the eyes, upon the nostrils, upon the lips, upon the ears, upon the breast, upon the hands, and upon the feet, together with the anointing saying:[^281] “The seal of the gift of the Holy Spirit. Amen.” After this he wipes his finger and all parts of the chrismated one and destroys the paper, while the cantor speaks: “As many of you as have been baptized into Christ,” etc. After this follows the Prokimenon. The Epistle. The Gospel. After the Gospel the priest loudly proclaims: “Let us pray unto the Lord,” and reads loudly the prayer: “O Lord God

=== LEAF p262 ===
[Book Page 238]

(p. 238)

our God, from the fullness...” etc. After the prayer, the Litany: “Have mercy on us, O God.” The Dismissal. The Blessing. After the blessing the priest gives the cross unto the sponsors to kiss.

Note. “The Myron is not permitted to be kept in an earthen or wooden vessel, or in a glass vial; but in a silver or pewter vessel, and that always in the church, and not in private houses.” (*Syn. Zamosc.* tit. III, §. 2, p. 83.)

§. 179.

The Order of the Holy Mystery of the Eucharist.

The priest administers this Mystery either unto the healthy, or unto the sick.

I. If the administration of the Holy Eucharist is unto healthy people, it is celebrated in the Holy Liturgy, and that in the following manner. After “With the fear of God,” the priest places the spoon into the holy Chalice, and covers it with the communion-cloth [lention], and after this takes the Chalice into his left hand, and between two fingers of the left hand takes the diskos, and having turned toward the people signs himself with the holy cross and loudly speaks the prayer: “I believe, O Lord, and I confess, that Thou art truly the Christ, the Son of the living God, Who didst come into the world to save sinners, of whom I am the first.

Of Thy Mystical Supper today, O Son of God, receive me as a communicant: for I will not speak of the Mystery unto Thine enemies, neither will I give Thee a kiss as did Judas, but as the thief confessing Thee I cry unto Thee:

Remember me, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom.

Remember me, O Master, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom.

Remember me, O Holy One, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom.

Let not the communion of Thy holy Mysteries be unto me for judgment or for condemnation, O Lord, but for the healing of soul and body.

For I believe and confess that that which I receive is the true, most pure Body of Thine and the very most pure and life-creating Blood of Thine, which I pray make me worthy to receive worthily unto the remission of all my sins and unto life everlasting. Amen.”

After the prayer, for the communicating of the laity, the priest descends unto the ambo, and administers Holy Communion in such a manner that, holding the holy Chalice in the left hand, and between two fingers, between the second and middle fingers, the diskos, which he places under the mouth of the communicant, and with his right hand from the Chalice with the spoon he takes the Body and Blood of the Savior, and placing it into the mouth of the communicant, say-

=== LEAF p263 ===
[Book Page 239]

(p. 239)

ing the prayer: “The servant of God (the handmaid of God) partakes of the precious and all-holy and most pure Body and Blood of our Lord and God and Savior Jesus Christ, unto the remission of all his (her) sins and unto life everlasting. Amen.” After this, wiping the mouth of the communicant with the communion-cloth [lention], he saith: “Lo, this hath touched thy lips, and shall take away thine iniquities, and shall purge all thy sins.”[^282] And when in this manner all the faithful have become partakers of Holy Communion, the priest continues the Holy Liturgy, saying: “O God, save Thy people,” and the rest.

II. If, however, the Holy Eucharist is administered unto the sick, this takes place thus. — The priest, especially the pastor, — since he must particularly care also concerning this, that not one of his faithful at the departure from his life should be deprived of Divine Communion of the Holy Mysteries, — first must keep and carefully preserve the Holy Mysteries in the church upon the holy table in the tabernacle [ciborium]; furthermore, secondly, he must admonish his faithful also unto this, that if sickness should fall upon any of them, they should make this known unto him as quickly as possible. — If, then, it is necessary for the priest to go unto the sick person, then he comes into the church and there takes upon himself the epitrachelion, then having devoutly taken forth from the tabernacle a particle of the Holy Eucharist, places it into a pyx specially prepared for this, which he carefully shuts, and after this places the pyx into a burse [pira] made of silk cloth, in which in a separate compartment he puts an eileton, a spoon, and a communion-cloth [lention]. Having then carefully tied this burse, he hangs it upon his neck, so that the Divine Mysteries lie upon his breast. Thus prepared he proceeds unto the house of the sick person, on the way devoutly reciting quietly to himself Psalm 50 [LXX Ps. 50], and the other Penitential Psalms. — Likewise the priest must have with him one server, who

=== LEAF p264 ===
[Book Page 240]

(p. 240)

preceding with a candle, is to carry with himself a vessel with wine, and according to custom a bell, which he rings frequently, so that those who see the priest going unto the sick person, or hear the ringing of the small bell, may render honor and adoration unto the All-Holy Mysteries. — When, however, the priest enters into the house of the sick person, he speaks loudly unto all those being in the house and having bowed their knees unto the ground: “Peace be unto this house, and unto all that dwell in it.” After this, upon the covered table where candles are burning, he spreads the eileton, places upon it the Holy Mysteries, and having bowed low, takes into his hand the aspergillum and sprinkles the sick person and the entire house with holy water, saying: “Thou shalt sprinkle me with hyssop, and I shall be cleansed; Thou shalt wash me, and I shall be made whiter than snow.” And immediately the troparion is sung: “O Lord, save Thy people,” etc., as in the Trebnik [Book of Needs]. After this — if the sick person hath not yet confessed — the priest bids all those present to withdraw, and confesses and absolves the suffering one. After the confession the server enters again into the room, and then the priest with the server first complete the prayers from “Blessed is our God,” wherewith they prepare the sick person for Holy Communion. After the prayers the priest, having bowed low unto the Holy Mysteries and taken from the pyx with the spoon a particle of the Holy Body and poured a little wine into the spoon, turns unto the sick person, saying: “With the fear of God and with faith draw near.” Whereunto the cantor speaks: “Blessed is He That comes in the name of the Lord,” etc., and the priest loudly and distinctly confesses the prayer: “I believe, O Lord, and I confess,” etc. After this, having placed the diskos under the mouth of the sick person, he communicates him, saying the prayer: “The servant of God partakes of the precious and all-holy and most pure...” etc., etc., and having wiped his mouth with the communion-cloth [lention], saith: “Lo, this hath touched thy lips,” etc. After Holy Communion the priest reads the prayer found in the Trebnik [Book of Needs]: “Thy Body,” etc., and the prayer unto the Most-Holy Theotokos: “O Most-Holy Lady Theotokos,” etc., and having made the Dismissal, departs.[^283]

Note. There is among us the pious custom of the “First Communion of Children.” It is for the most part celebrated on Thomas Sunday. The children on the preceding day toward evening come for

=== LEAF p265 ===
[Book Page 241]

(p. 241)

their first holy confession; on the very day of Holy Communion, dressed in new and white clothing, adorned with wreaths and ribbons, they come into the church for the Liturgy, and stand upon the ambo in a row, the boys separately, and the girls likewise separately. — When the time of Holy Communion is come, each child receives a burning candle, and one by one through a parent or sponsor [nanashko] (and this is better) they are brought unto Holy Communion. On this occasion the priest delivers a holy sermon both unto the children and unto their parents. This rite is worthy of imitation, for the remembrance of this rite grows with the children and their piety is tenderly nourished. (Dukhnovych, *Liturgics*, Lviv, 1855, p. 171.)

§. 180.

The Order of the Holy Mystery of Repentance.

If a priest is called unto the hearing of holy confession, let him show himself ready for this work without any murmuring. For the hearing of confessions, however, if he hath time for this, let him prepare himself by fervent prayer and the stirring up of good intention. In the church, and not in private houses, the priest is to receive confessions, except for a necessary cause. When he receives holy confession, let him be vested in the epitrachelion, and sitting and seeing that the sinner draws near unto him, let him speak the prayer: “O Master, Lord our God, receive this Thy servant who repents of his sins, and give unto him contrition of heart, and direct his conscience, that with good hope he may confess all his transgressions before the glory of Thy dread throne and my unworthiness, and that there may be glorified in him thereafter Thy most honorable and magnificent name, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit. Amen.” After this, when the approaching sinner bows his knees and makes upon himself the sign of the cross, the priest likewise signs him with the sign of the cross, having laid upon his head the epitrachelion, saying unto him: “May our Lord Jesus Christ be in thy heart and upon thy lips, that thou mayest worthily confess all thy transgressions: in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit. Amen.”

And immediately the sinner himself or with the priest speaks the prayer: “I confess unto the Lord God...” etc. — After this the priest inquires of the penitent: “When didst thou confess the last time? Hast thou concealed in thy last confession any sin? Hast thou performed the works of penance [pokuta] appointed in thy former confession? What sins dost thou remember since thy last confession?”

=== LEAF p266 ===
[Book Page 242]

(p. 242)

Unto these and similar questions the sinner must answer sincerely. Especially the priest must inquire of the sinner concerning his dominant sin (*peccatum dominans* [*dominant sin*]), or that which most of all gnaws his conscience. After a sincere, full, and accurate confession and acknowledgment of sins, the priest once more inquires of the sinner: “Dost thou not yet remember any other sin?” and after this saith unto him: “Dost thou repent for the sins committed by thee?” whereunto in answer the sinner speaks the prayer: “For these and all my sins, conscious and unconscious...” etc.

After such an expression of sorrow the priest inquires of the sinner: “Hast thou hope in the mercy of God that He will forgive thee thy sins?” And again: “Hast thou a good will to amend and not to sin?” and after this follows the spiritual instruction of the priest as teacher, judge, and physician, as Pastoral Theology teaches.[^284] Finally the priest imposes upon the sinner a penance [pokuta] corresponding unto his sins, looking upon the station, life, strength, etc., of the sinner.

Absolution is administered with these words: “Let us pray unto the Lord.” (“Lord, have mercy.”) “May our Lord and God Jesus Christ, through the grace and bounties of His love for mankind, forgive thee, child [Name], all thy transgressions; and I, an unworthy priest [Name], by His power given unto me, forgive and absolve thee from all thy sins. In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit. Amen.” And dismissing the sinner he saith: “Go in peace, and sin no more.”[^285]

§. 181.

The Order of the Holy Mystery of Priesthood.

The Holy Mystery of Priesthood, — as is known from Dogmatic Theology, — hath several orders. And although it is celebrated only by the Bishop alone: nevertheless for the completeness of this little book we shall speak also concerning the celebration of this Mystery.

I. Ordination [Cheirothesia] of a Reader. This rite among us the Bishop celebrates in the Liturgy after the exclamation: “For holy art Thou, O our God.” — And that thus: The Bishop, vested in all pontifical

=== LEAF p267 ===
[Book Page 243]

(p. 243)

vestments, sits near the holy Altar-Table upon a faldstool [chair], while the one being ordained a reader is brought before the Royal Doors upon the ambo. After this the Archdeacon, having taken his stand at the Royal Doors, begins the rite with the words: “Most-Reverend Bishop, the Church of God desires that this God-fearing man be numbered unto the Levitical order.” Unto this the Hierarch answers: “Knowest thou him to be worthy?” etc., whereunto the Archdeacon again speaks: “So far as human frailty can know, I know,” while the Bishop saith: “Thanks be to God.”

After this declaration of the Bishop, the one being ordained a reader makes three metanias and enters into the Sanctuary, where before the Bishop he bows his knees, while the Bishop saith: “If thou hast been bound by any bonds of an oath... I... absolve thee,” (and signs his head with the cross) “in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit. Amen.” Then, having laid his hand upon the head of the one being ordained, he speaks the prayer: “Who hast created all things,” etc., with the Exclamation.

After this the Hierarch hands a candle into the hand of the one being ordained, and he, having received it, kisses the hand of the Hierarch and the candle, saying: “Bless, Master,” whereunto the Bishop answers: “May the Lord bless thee as a Reader of the holy Church,” etc., and loudly proclaims: “Blessed is our God,” etc., — whereunto the one being ordained answers: “Amen,” and standing with the candle in his hands, continues, saying: The Trisagion, “Our Father,” and the troparia: “O holy Apostles,” etc., “Unto all the earth,” etc., “From thy mouth,” etc. “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): “The shepherd’s pipe,” “Now and forever”: “By the prayers, O Lord, of all,” etc.

After this the one being ordained, having given back the candle, comes unto the Hierarch already sitting upon the faldstool and bows his knees before him, who with scissors tonsures him, saying: “The servant of God [Name] is tonsured in the hair of his head: in the name of the Father †, and of the Son †, and of the Holy Spirit †. Amen.” Whereunto the choir responds: “Lord, have mercy,” 3 times. After this the Hierarch saith: “Peace be unto all,” and immediately the Archdeacon: “Let us pray unto the Lord,” and the Hierarch reads the prayer: “Blessed art Thou, O Lord,” etc., after which the Bishop blesses the one being ordained with his hand with the words: “In the name of the Father †, and of the Son †, and of the Holy Spirit †. Amen.” And again follows: “Peace be unto all,” and the Archdeacon again speaks: “Let us pray unto the Lord,” whereunto the Bishop lays his hand upon the head of the Reader and speaks the prayer: “O Lord God Almighty,” etc.

After this prayer the Hierarch hands unto the Reader the Book of the Epistles [Apostol], who kisses the right hand of the Bishop and the book. At this the Archdeacon proclaims: “Let us attend,” the Bishop: “Peace be unto all,”

=== LEAF p268 ===
[Book Page 244]

(p. 244)

the Archdeacon: “Wisdom! Let us attend,” — and the cantor reads the Prokimenon. After the Prokimenon: The Archdeacon proclaims: “Wisdom!” (*Премꙋ́дрость!*) The Reader: “The Reading from the Acts of the Holy Apostles.” The Archdeacon: “Let us attend,” and the reader reads the Epistle. (Acts of the Apostles, pericope 16.)

After the reading of the Epistle, the Hierarch, having laid upon the one being ordained the sticharion (the reader kissing the Hierarch upon the hand and the sticharion), saith: “Thy priests shall be clothed with righteousness,” etc.; further, handing unto him the cincture [zone], he saith: “Gird thy loins with the power of truth,” etc. — And having arisen, the Hierarch saith unto him: “Confess the Orthodox Faith,” etc., whereunto he loudly professes the Symbol of Faith (“I believe in one God” (*Вѣ́рую во є҆ди́наго Бо́га...*)...).

After this, the reader having bowed his knees, the Hierarch saith unto him: “Son! the order of reader is the first degree of the Priesthood,” etc., and at the end saith unto him: “Blessed is God: behold, the servant of God is ordained a Reader of the holy Church, in the name of the Father †, and of the Son †, and of the Holy Spirit †. Amen,” etc., whereunto the choir sings: “Axios” [“He is worthy”]. And herewith the ordination of a reader is concluded.

II. The Order of Ordination [Cheirothesia] of a Subdeacon. The ordination of a Subdeacon takes place in the Holy Liturgy and that before the Great Entrance after the prayer: “None is worthy,” etc., and that in the following manner:

The Hierarch, sitting upon the faldstool near the holy Altar-Table, blesses the Reader with his hand, saying: “In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit,” etc., and having laid his hand upon his head, speaks the prayer: “O Lord our God,” etc.

Upon the conclusion of this prayer, the Subdeacon having fallen prostrate before the holy Altar-Table, the Archdeacon loudly proclaims the Litany: “In peace let us pray unto the Lord,” and at the conclusion the Hierarch sprinkles the Subdeacon with holy water and speaks the exclamation: “For Thou art our sanctification,” etc.

After this they bring the washbasin with water and a towel [lention], and the Hierarch, having received from the servers first the towel, spreads it upon his knees; the washbasin, however, he gives unto the ordained Subdeacon; he, having received this, first kisses the hand of the Hierarch, after this the washbasin, and thus pours water upon the hands of the Hierarch, saying three times: “All who are faithful.” The Hierarch, while washing his hands, speaks the prayer: “I will wash my hands in innocency,” etc. After this the Bishop blesses the subdeacon and commands him, covered with the towel, to be led behind the Altar-Table, while he himself continues the Holy Liturgy.

III. The Ordination [Cheirotonia] of a Deacon. In the Holy Liturgy after

=== LEAF p269 ===
[Book Page 245]

(p. 245)

the proclamation: “And may the mercies of our great God and Savior Jesus Christ be with you all,” the Hierarch sits upon the faldstool in the mitre and with the pastoral staff, while the deacon to be ordained is led out through the north door unto the ambo before the Royal Doors. The Archdeacon, standing behind the Royal Doors, begins, saying unto the Bishop: “By the good pleasure of God... the reverend Subdeacon is promoted unto the Diaconate; command, holy Master”; unto this the one being ordained makes a bow before the Royal Doors, while the Hierarch saith: “By the command of the Lord the heavens were established,” etc. After this the Archdeacon, having stepped a little closer, again saith: “Bless him, holy Master”; whereunto the Hierarch, unto the one being ordained who makes a bow at the very Royal Doors, saith: “May the Lord bless him,” etc. And immediately the Archdeacon, standing before the Altar-Table, saith: “Let us pray for him, holy Master”; while the one being ordained already makes a bow before the holy Altar-Table, and the Hierarch saith: “The Holy Spirit shall come upon him,” etc. And in this manner the one being ordained, having approached unto the Hierarch, kisses his epigonation [nabedrennik], his hand, and his right shoulder, and unto his words: “Christ is in our midst,” answers: “He is and shall be.”

After this the Archdeacon leads the one being ordained around the holy Altar-Table, that he may kiss the 4 corners of the holy Table. This takes place 3 times. The choir during this sings: “O Holy Martyrs,” etc. After this the one being ordained falls upon his knees at the holy Altar-Table, while the Hierarch, standing without the mitre, lays the omophorion upon the one being ordained and makes the sign of the cross three times with his right hand upon his head, saying: “In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit,” etc.; when the Archdeacon proclaims: “Let us attend,” the Hierarch, having laid his right hand upon the head of the one being ordained, reads the prayer, saying: “Divine grace... promotes the reverend subdeacon unto the diaconate; let us therefore pray for him, that the grace of the All-Holy Spirit may come upon him, and let us all say.” Whereunto the choir begins to sing: “Lord, have mercy,” 3 times. The Hierarch with the sign of the holy cross again 3 times signs the head of the ordained deacon, saying: “In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit,” etc., and again having laid his hand upon his head secretly speaks the prayer: “O Lord our God,” etc., with the Exclamation.

After this the deacon lies prostrate upon the earth cruciformly, during which the Hierarch sits and the Archdeacon speaks the litany: “In peace let us pray unto the Lord,” while the exclamation is spoken by the Bishop.

After the litany the deacon, having arisen, bows his knees, while the Hierarch, standing without the mitre, at the exclamation of the Archdeacon: “Let us pray unto the

=== LEAF p270 ===
[Book Page 246]

(p. 246)

Lord,” lays his hand upon the deacon and speaks the prayer: “O Lord God our Savior,” etc.

After this prayer follows the investiture with the ecclesiastical vestments (*porrectio instrumentorum* [*handing-over of the instruments*]), and that in such a manner that the Hierarch, sitting upon the faldstool with the mitre and with the pastoral staff, gives unto the deacon the dalmatic [sticharion] with the words: “May the Lord clothe thee with the garment of salvation... Axios” [“He is worthy”]; unto this the deacon kisses the hand of the Hierarch and the dalmatic, while the choir at the words of the Hierarch “Axios,” likewise sings it 3 times. After this the Hierarch gives unto the deacon the orarion and saith: “Receive the pure orarion... for God is able to multiply in thee His grace. Axios”; whereunto the deacon kisses the hand of the Hierarch and the orarion, while the choir sings 3 times: “Axios,” etc. The Hierarch further hands unto him the Gospel-Book with the words: “Receive authority to read the Gospel... in the name of the Lord... Axios”; while the deacon again kisses the hand of the Hierarch and the Gospel-Book, and the choir sings 3 times: “Axios.” — Finally the Hierarch hands unto him the censer, saying: “Receive this vessel, in which thou hast to offer incense... Axios”; — and the deacon again kisses the hand of the Hierarch and the censer, while the choir sings 3 times: “Axios.”
