And herewith the ordination of a deacon is ended. — Upon this they clothe the deacon in his vestments, while the Hierarch, having arisen, continues the Holy Liturgy.

IV. The Ordination [Cheirotonia] of a Priest. After the singing of the Cherubic Hymn and after the Entrance, the Archdeacon leads the deacon to be ordained a priest unto the Hierarch sitting near the holy Altar-Table in the mitre and holding the pastoral staff in his hands, exactly in the manner that was described above; that is to say, the Archdeacon begins, saying unto the Hierarch: “By the good pleasure of God and the operation of the Holy Spirit... the reverend deacon is promoted unto the Priesthood; command, holy Master.” At these words of the Archdeacon the one being ordained makes one bow before the Royal Doors, while the Hierarch saith: “By the command of the Lord the heavens were established,” etc. After this the Archdeacon, having stepped closer a little, saith: “Bless him, holy Master”; the one being ordained at this makes one bow at the very Royal Doors, while the Hierarch saith: “May the Lord bless him from Zion”... etc. Finally the Archdeacon, having taken his stand before the holy Altar-Table, saith: “Let us pray for him, holy Master”; the one being ordained again makes one bow before the Altar-Table, while the Hierarch saith: “The Holy Spirit shall come upon him,” etc.

After this the one being ordained, having approached unto the Hierarch, kisses 

=== LEAF p271 ===
[Book Page 247]

(p. 247)

---


his epigonation [nabedrennik], his hand, and his right shoulder, and unto his words: “Christ is in our midst,” answers: “He is and shall be.”

After this the Archdeacon leads the one being ordained 3 times around the Altar-Table exactly as was described above at the ordination unto a Deacon.

After this the one being ordained falls upon his knees before the holy Altar-Table, lays his hands cruciformly upon the holy Table, and upon them rests his head; — the Hierarch, however, stands without the mitre, covers his head with the omophorion, and blesses it three times with his right hand, saying: “In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit.” And at the exclamation of the Archdeacon: “Let us attend,” the Hierarch, having laid his right hand upon his head, reads the secret prayer: “Divine grace... promotes the reverend Deacon unto a Priest...” etc. The choir sings: “Lord, have mercy,” three times. The Hierarch again blesses the head of the newly-ordained Priest, and reads the prayer: “O God without beginning...” etc., with the Exclamation.

After this the one being ordained arises, and having bowed his knees before the Hierarch, holds before him an open book in which is set forth the confession of faith, saying loudly: “I believe and confess...” etc.

After this follows the investiture with the ecclesiastical vestments (*porrectio instrumentorum* [*handing-over of the instruments*]). The Hierarch, having sat upon the faldstool in the mitre with the pastoral staff in his hands, gives unto the newly-ordained Priest the epitrachelion, saying: “Blessed is God Who pours out His grace... Axios”; the choir sings three times: “Axios.” After this he gives unto him the cincture, saying: “Blessed is God Who girds thee with strength... Axios”; and the choir sings: “Axios.” Further he gives unto him the cuffs, saying: “Thy right hand, O Lord, is glorified in strength... Axios”; and the choir sings: “Axios.” Further he gives unto him the phelonion, saying: “Thy priests, O Lord, shall be clothed with righteousness... Axios”; and the choir sings: “Axios.” — After this the Archdeacon carries the holy Chalice with the diskos standing upon it (and upon the diskos lie the asterisk and the veils, and upon the holy Chalice lies the veil and the holy aer) and gives it unto the Hierarch, while the Hierarch gives it unto the newly-ordained Priest, saying: “Receive these vessels, and keep them as a guardian of the Mysteries of Christ... Axios.” The newly-ordained Priest kisses the holy Chalice and the hand of the Hierarch, while the choir sings three times: “Axios.” — Finally the Hierarch gives unto him the holy Sluzhebnik, saying: “Receive this Liturgikon... Axios”; and the Priest again kisses

=== LEAF p272 ===
[Book Page 248]

(p. 248)

the Sluzhebnik and the hand of the Hierarch, while the choir sings: “Axios,” three times.

And herewith the order of ordination unto a Priest is ended. — The Hierarch, having arisen, celebrates the Liturgy further.

The newly-ordained Priest, however, stands near the Altar-Table and communicates according to the order of Priests, but with this difference, that the Hierarch gives unto him the holy Particle, saying: “Receive, O newly-ordained Priest, the Body of Christ, concerning which thou hast to give an answer on the day of judgment”; while the newly-ordained Priest takes the Particle into his hands and kisses the hand of the Hierarch, and having stepped behind the holy Altar-Table, with fear and attentiveness reads the following prayer: “Honorable and holy...”[^286] and upon completing the prayer communicates of the holy Body of Christ; in like manner the Hierarch gives unto him also the holy Blood of Christ.

After the Ambo Prayer the Hierarch removes from the newly-ordained Priest the vestments, and gives unto him pastoral blessing and instruction.

V. The Consecration of a Bishop.[^287]

It is celebrated in the episcopal cathedral. In the church there is prepared a faldstool before the Royal Doors with a seat, and in the church eagle-rugs [orletzy][^288] are spread out. On this day they ring all the bells, and the choir sings spiritual hymns.

When the Hierarch (Archbishop) comes into the church, and is vested in all the hierarchal vestments, then two Bishops-consecrators take the one newly elected unto a Bishop, vested in priestly vestments without the phelonion, and lead him before the Royal Doors upon the first eagle-rug.

=== LEAF p273 ===
[Book Page 249]

(p. 249)

Here one of the consecrators presents the Apostolic letter (bull), which the secretary reads loudly; and after the reading the choir sings: “Thanks be to God.”

After this the newly-elected one stands upon the tail of the eagle-rug, and reads the Creed: “I believe in one God...” After the reading the consecrators lead him unto the middle of the eagle-rug, where he reads the second exposition of faith, in which is set forth in detail the dogma concerning the Most Holy Trinity and the Incarnation of the Son of God. — After this the consecrators lead him unto the head of the eagle-rug, where he reads the third exposition of faith, touching the dogmas of the Church, the recognition of the ecumenical councils, and obedience unto the Holy Apostolic See.

After this the one being ordained draws near unto the Hierarch Consecrator, bows down unto him to the earth, and kisses his hand; and the Hierarch signs him with a blessing.

After this the Holy Liturgy begins.

After the Little Entrance and after the singing of the troparia, when the choir sings: “Many Years,” the one being ordained is led by the consecrators before the Royal Doors; here the Hierarch Consecrator lays upon him the epigonation [palitza / nabedrennik], saying: “Gird Thy sword upon Thy thigh, O Mighty One... Axios”; and the choir sings: “Axios,” three times.

After this the Bishop Consecrator, delivering unto the one being ordained

=== LEAF p274 ===
[Book Page 250]

(p. 250)

the pastoral staff, speaks: “Receive this staff... Axios”; and the choir sings: “Axios.”

After this the one being ordained falls upon his knees before the holy Altar-Table, and lays upon it his hands and his head. The consecrators hold the open holy Gospel-Book over his head with the writing turned downward; while the Hierarch Consecrator lays his right hand upon his head and reads the prayer of ordination: “Divine grace... promotes the reverend... unto a Bishop...” The choir sings three times: “Lord, have mercy.” — The Hierarch reads the secret prayers, while the consecrators speak the litany.

After this follows the investiture with all the hierarchal vestments: the sakkos, the omophorion, the panagia, the pectoral cross, and the mitre, with the singing of: “Axios.”

After the investiture the Hierarch Consecrator and all the consecrators give unto the newly-ordained Bishop the fraternal kiss (the kiss of peace).

After this the newly-ordained Bishop is led up unto the high place, where he sits as a Bishop during the reading of the Epistle.

After the Ambo Prayer the newly-ordained Bishop makes the circuit of the church, blessing the people on both sides, while the choir sings: “Many Years.”

=== LEAF p275 ===
[Book Page 251]

(p. 251)

After this the newly-ordained Bishop goes into the sacristy, where the liturgical vestments are removed from him, and he is clothed in the hierarchal ryasa and mantle.

§. 182.

The Order of the Holy Mystery of Matrimony.[^289]

I. The Order of the Blessing of the Matrimony of the First-Married.

Before the priest proceeds unto the blessing of the marriage, he must assure himself concerning the free status of the bridegroom and the bride, concerning their mutual consent, concerning the completion of the lawful banns, and whether there be any canonical impediment (*impedimentum*) unto the marriage. Likewise the bride and bridegroom must approach unto the Holy Mystery of Repentance and Holy Communion.

On the appointed day and hour the bridegroom and bride, accompanied by witnesses (groomsmen and bridesmaids) and parents, come into the church, and take their stand before the tetrapod: the bridegroom on the right hand, and the bride on the left hand. Upon the tetrapod are laid the holy cross, the holy Gospel-Book, two crowns, and two candles.

The priest, vested in epitrachelion and phelonion, comes out before the Royal Doors or unto the tetrapod, and questions the bridegroom: “Hast thou a good and unconstrained desire, and a firm purpose, to take unto thyself as wife this woman (*Name*)?” The bridegroom answers: “I have, reverend Father.” Further he questions: “Hast thou not promised thyself unto another bride?” He answers: “I have not promised myself, reverend Father.” In like manner he questions the bride also.

=== LEAF p276 ===
[Book Page 252]

(p. 252)

After this the priest gives into their hands lighted candles, and begins the betrothal, saying: “Blessed is our God...” Cantor: “Amen.” There follows the Great Litany with special petitions for those being betrothed: “That this betrothal may be blessed...” After the litany the priest reads two prayers, blesses the rings, and lays them upon the Altar-Table or upon the tetrapod. After this he takes the ring of the bridegroom, makes with it a cross over the head of the bridegroom, saying three times: “The servant of God (*Name*) is betrothed unto the handmaid of God (*Name*), in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit. Amen.” And he places the ring upon a finger of his right hand. In like manner he makes also for the bride. After this the groomsmen exchange the rings three times. The priest reads the prayer: “O Lord our God, Who didst accompany the servant of the Patriarch Abraham...” And there follows the Augmented Litany and the dismissal of the betrothal.

After this follows the crowning itself.

The priest goes before them with the censer unto the analoj [tetrapod], while the cantors sing Psalm 127 LXX [Psalm 128 MT]: “Blessed are all they that fear the Lord...” with the refrain: “Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.”

Having arrived at the tetrapod, the bridegroom and bride lay their right hands upon the holy Gospel-Book, while the priest binds their hands with the epitrachelion, blesses them with the cross, and they pronounce the marital oath of mutual fidelity, love, and the indissolubility of the union even unto death.

After this the priest begins: “Blessed is the Kingdom...” The Great Litany. Three long prayers. After the prayers the priest takes the crown of the bridegroom, signs the bridegroom cruciformly with it, and crowns him, saying three times: “The servant of God (*Name*) is crowned unto the handmaid of God (*Name*), in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit. Amen.” In like manner he crowns the bride also. After this he blesses both three times, saying: “O Lord our God, crown them with glory and honor!”

There follows the Prokimenon, the Epistle (Eph. § 230), the Gospel (John § 6), the Augmented Litany and the Litany of Supplication. “Our Father.”

=== LEAF p277 ===
[Book Page 253]

(p. 253)

After “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) there is offered the common cup with blessed wine, from which the bridegroom and bride drink three times. After this the priest binds their hands with the epitrachelion, and leads them three times around the tetrapod, while the cantors sing the troparia: “Rejoice, O Isaiah...”, “O Holy Martyrs...”, “Glory to Thee, O Christ God...”

After this the priest removes the crowns from the bridegroom and bride, saying unto each the corresponding greeting: “Be thou magnified, O bridegroom, as Abraham...” and: “And thou, O bride, be thou magnified as Sarah...” He reads two prayers for the removal of the crowns. After this there follows the Augmented Litany and the full festal dismissal: “He Who in Cana of Galilee...” The newlyweds kiss the cross and receive congratulations from those present.

II. The Order of the Blessing of the Matrimony of the Second-Married.

If both (the bridegroom and bride) are second-married (widowed), it is celebrated according to a special order having a penitential character. The betrothal is not celebrated separately, but together with the crowning.

The priest vests only in epitrachelion and phelonion. Beginning: “Blessed is our God...” Trisagion. “O Most Holy Trinity...” “Our Father...” Troparion of the day. There follows the litany: “In peace let us pray unto the Lord,” with the addition of petitions for the remission of sins and cleansing of the iniquities of the bridegroom and bride.

After the litany the priest reads two prayers with penitential meaning: “O Eternal God, Who hast gathered together the dispersed into union...” and the second prayer: “O Lord our God, Who desirest that all men be saved...”

After this he places the rings without special prayers, but directly with the words: “The servant of God is betrothed...”

=== LEAF p278 ===
[Book Page 254]

(p. 254)

After this he lays on the crowns with the words: “The servant of God is crowned...” and blesses: “O Lord our God, crown them with glory and honor!” He reads two prayers: “O Master, Lord our God, Who hast mercy upon all men...” and: “O Lord Jesus Christ, Word of God...”

After this the common cup is offered, the procession around the tetrapod with the singing of: “Rejoice, O Isaiah...”, the removal of crowns with the corresponding prayers.

The litany: “Have mercy on us, O God...” and the dismissal.[^290]

Note. If, however, only one of those being married is second-married (a widower or a widow), and the other first-married (a bachelor or a maiden), then the order of the first-married is celebrated, with all solemnities.

§. 183.

The Order of the Holy Mystery of Holy Anointing [Eleosvyashcheniye].

This holy Mystery is celebrated over the severely ill by a synod of seven priests (whence also the name “Soborovanie”), but in case of need it can be celebrated also by a smaller number of priests, even by a single priest.

In the house of the sick person there is prepared a table covered with a white cloth. Upon the table is set a vessel with wheat (or rye), and upon the wheat is placed a lamp (or cup) with oil mixed with wine. Around the vessel are set 7 small wands wrapped with cotton [struptsy] for the anointing, and 7 lighted candles.

The priests vest in epitrachelia and phelonia (if, however, there be one priest, then in epitrachelion and phelonion).

=== LEAF p279 ===
[Book Page 255]

(p. 255)

The usual beginning: “Blessed is our God...” Trisagion. “Our Father.” Psalm 142 LXX [Psalm 143 MT]: “O Lord, hear my prayer...” The Little Litany. Penitential troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us...” Psalm 50 LXX [Psalm 51 MT]: “Have mercy upon me, O God...” The Canon of Holy Anointing, the composition of Patriarch Arsenius, in Tone 4: “O bountiful and merciful Lord...” After the 9th Ode, the stichera and the troparion: “O Christ, Who alone art swift to help...”

There follows the Great Litany of Holy Anointing: “In peace let us pray unto the Lord... That this oil may be blessed...” After the litany the presiding priest reads the prayer over the oil: “O Lord, Who by Thy mercy and bounties healest the contrite of our souls and bodies, do Thou Thyself bless this oil also...”

After this follows the 7-fold reading of the Epistle, the Gospel, the prayer, and the 7-fold anointing of the sick person.[^291]

The 1st Epistle from James (§ 57: “Is any sick among you...”). The Gospel from Luke (§ 51: concerning the Good Samaritan). The 1st Prayer: “O Father without beginning, eternal, Holy of Holies...” After the prayer the priest takes a wand, dips it into the holy oil, and anoints the sick person cruciformly upon the forehead, the nostrils, the cheeks, the lips, the breast, and upon both hands,[^292] saying the form of the mystery: “O Holy Father, Physician of souls and bodies... [Forma Sacramenti] heal Thy servant (*Name*) from the bodily and spiritual infirmity which holds him...”[^293]

In like manner are celebrated also the second, third, fourth, fifth, sixth, and seventh anointings with the corresponding Epistles, Gospels, and prayers.

=== LEAF p280 ===
[Book Page 256]

(p. 256)

After the seventh anointing the sick person (if he can) himself kneels or sits, while the Priests open the holy Gospel-Book with the writing turned downward, and hold it over the head of the sick person; and the presiding Priest reads the prayer of absolution: “O Holy King, compassionate and plenteous in mercy...” The sick person kisses the holy Gospel-Book.

There follows the Augmented Litany: “Have mercy on us, O God...” with the petition for the healing of the sick person, and the dismissal. The sick person asks forgiveness from the Priests and from all his kinsfolk and relatives, saying three times: “Bless me, holy fathers, and forgive me, a sinner.” And the Priests answer: “May God forgive and heal thee.”

CHAPTER II.

Concerning the Rites of Diverse Ecclesiastical Consecrations and Blessings.[^294]

§. 184.

The Order of the Blessing of Children.

On the appointed day (usually on the Feast of the Nativity of Christ or on Theophany, or on the New Year) parents bring their children into the church.

The priest, vested in epitrachelion and phelonion, comes out before the Royal Doors or unto the tetrapod, and blesses: “Blessed is our God...” He reads the usual beginning, while the cantors sing the troparion of the feast.

After the exclamation he reads also the two prayers which be- 

=== LEAF p281 ===
[Book Page 257]

(p. 257)

---


-found in the Trebnik. After the prayers he blesses them and sprinkles them with holy water, saying: “May the blessing of Almighty God, the Father, † and the Son, † and the Holy Spirit, † descend upon you, preserve and direct you, and abide always with you.” Amen.

An admirable and praiseworthy rite, imitating the Most Holy Virgin!

§. 185.

The Order of the Churching of a Bride after Marriage.

When the newly-married bride comes unto the church, she stands outside the church doors; the Priest, however, vested in the epitrachelion, comes unto her and speaks: “Enter, child, into the courts of the Lord,” etc.

And immediately the Priest enters into the church with the bride and with those present. And having entered, the Priest, standing before the Icon of the Mother of God — the bride standing near him — begins usually: “Blessed is our God...” etc., the Trisagion, “Our Father,” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times), “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (3 times), etc., and Psalm 66: “May God be merciful unto us...” etc.

After this the Priest, before the bride who hath bowed her head, proclaims: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and speaks the prayer: “O God, our God,” and blesses her head with his right hand, saying: “May the Lord cover thee under the shelter of His wings,” etc. And immediately he sprinkles her with holy water, saying: “By the sprinkling of this sanctified water, may blessing descend and abide upon thee,” etc.

And after this he makes the Dismissal; and he concludes the rite with this blessing: “Go in peace, and diligently observe all the commandments of the Lord,” etc.

§. 186.

The Order of the Churching of a Woman after Childbirth.

When the woman comes with the child into the church, the Priest, having taken the epitrachelion and having come unto the church doors, where the woman, having fallen upon her knees, awaits him, begins thus: “In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, Amen.” And having laid his right hand upon the head of the woman, he speaks first the prayer: “O Lord God Almighty...”[^295] etc., and secondly the second prayer: “O Lord our God...” etc.

=== LEAF p282 ===
[Book Page 258]

(p. 258)

After these prayers he leads the woman into the church, saying unto her: “Enter into the house of God”; concerning the child, however, he speaks the prayer: “The servant of God [or: the handmaid of God] is churched...” etc., which he speaks 3 times, namely: at the doors of the church, in the midst of the church, and at the Royal Doors.

When the Priest comes unto the Royal Doors, he sprinkles the woman with holy water, saying: “May the peace and blessing of Almighty God, the Father, † and the Son, † and the Holy Spirit, † descend upon thee and abide always”; and the child he takes into his arms, saying: “Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart...” etc. And thus he makes the Dismissal.[^296]

§. 187.

The Order of the Burial of Infants.

Infants in the Church are called those children who know not yet how to speak; when, however, they begin to speak, even unto the seventh year, they are called children [*otroky*]. — The burial of infants as well as of children is celebrated in bright, that is, white vestments.

When the Priest comes into the house where the deceased infant lies, being vested in epitrachelion and phelonion, he puts incense into the censer, and goes into the room in which the deceased lies; during this the choir sings: “When Thou didst descend...”

After this, in the room, the Panikhida begins, namely: “Blessed is our God...” etc., the Trisagion, “Our Father,” “With the spirits of the righteous...”[^297] etc., and the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” with the exclamation. After the exclamation there is a special dismissal ending with: “In blessed repose grant eternal rest, O Lord...” etc., unto which the choir sings: “Memory eternal.” — And the Priest, having sprinkled the body of the deceased with holy water, goes out with the cantor from the room into the courtyard, where the burial itself begins.

First the Priest speaks: “Blessed is our God...” etc.; the choir: “Amen”; and among us[^298] there follow: Psalms 112, 102, and 23 (“Praise the Lord, O ye servants...” etc., “O Lord, my heart is not exalted...” etc., “The earth is the Lord’s...” etc.).

After the Psalms the Priest proclaims: “For holy art Thou, O God...” etc., and the Trisagion is sung. — After this follow: the Prokeimenon, the Epis-

=== LEAF p283 ===
[Book Page 259]

(p. 259)

-tle, and Gospel; and there takes place the last kiss, during which the choir sings the stichera: “O who would not weep...” etc. The litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” with the exclamation, and after which the Priest speaks: “In blessed repose...” etc.; the choir: “Memory eternal,” etc.

And immediately the body of the deceased is carried unto the cemetery. On the way the “Trisagion” is sung. At the grave the Priest proclaims: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and reads the prayer: “O Lord, Who preservest infants,” after which the body of the deceased is lowered into the grave, while the choir sings: “Open wide, O earth, receive the body formed from thee by the hand of God...” etc. (in Tone 8). During this singing the Priest, having sealed the grave with the words: “This grave is sealed until the second coming of Christ...” etc., makes the Dismissal.

§. 188.

The Order of the Burial of Lay Persons.

The Priest, having come into the house where the remains of the deceased lie, puts upon himself the epitrachelion and phelonion, and having put incense into the censer, goes into the room, while the cantor sings: “When Thou didst descend” (*Егда снизшелъ еси*); in the room he begins the Panikhida: “Blessed is our God...” etc. After this the Trisagion, and the rest as usual. After the Dismissal the Priest speaks: “In blessed repose...” etc., the choir sings: “Memory eternal,” during which singing the Priest sprinkles the body of the deceased with holy water, and with the cantor goes out of the room.

When the body is laid upon the prepared place in the courtyard, the Priest begins: “Blessed is our God...” etc., and immediately among us[^299] the choir sings “Alleluia” 3 times, and the troparia: “With depth of wisdom,” Glory, and Now: “We have thee as a wall and a haven...” etc.

After the troparia there are sung first the verses from the first stasis of Kathisma 17: “Blessed are the undefiled,” with the verse: “Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach us Thy statutes,” etc.[^300] After this are sung verses from the second stasis of Kathisma 17: “I am Thine...” etc., with the verse: “O Savior, save the soul of Thy servant,” concluding with the sticheron: “My soul shall live...” etc. Finally the stichera “The choir of the Saints,” with the verse: “Blessed art Thou, O Lord,” and we conclude with “Alleluia” (3 times).

After this the Little Litany, and the Priest censes the body of the deceased from the four sides.

=== LEAF p284 ===
[Book Page 260]

(p. 260)

And immediately are sung the idiomela stichera of St. John of Damascus in the 8 Tones: “What earthly pleasure...” etc.

After these, among us,[^301] there are at once taken: the Prokeimenon, the Epistle, and the Gospel, after which takes place the last kiss, when the Priest gives the holy Cross to kiss, while the choir sings: “Come, let us give the last kiss...” etc. The litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and the Priest proclaims: “In blessed repose...” etc. And the body is carried unto the cemetery. On the way Psalm 50 is sung. (At solemn funerals the Gospels: from John, sections [pericopes] 15, 16, 22, and 23.) At the cemetery, at the grave, the Priest proclaims: “Bowing our knees, let us pray to the Lord,” whereupon those present fall upon their knees, and the Priest speaks the prayer of absolution, namely: if the deceased made confession: “O compassionate and plenteous in mercy...” etc., but if he made no confession: “O Lord Jesus Christ our God...” etc.

After the prayer of absolution they lower the body of the deceased into the grave, while the choir sings: “Open wide, O earth,” Glory, and Now: “O Mother of the Savior of them that hope in thee.” And having lowered the deceased into the grave, the Priest sprinkles the grave with holy water, and after this seals it with a shovel in such manner that, taking earth with the shovel, he casts it cruciformly upon the remains, saying: “This grave is sealed until the second coming of Christ, in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, Amen,” and thus he makes the Dismissal. At the conclusion he speaks: “In blessed repose, eternal rest...” etc. Memory eternal.

§. 189.

The Order of the Burial of Secular Priests.

If a certain secular Priest departs unto the Lord, 3 Priests come and with a sponge dipped in pure oil wipe his face, and clothe him in all the priestly vestments. And having covered his face with the aer, they place the Gospel-Book at his head.

After this the Priests, having clothed themselves in epitrachelion and phelonion, come and stand round about the remains; the presiding Priest censes the deceased and begins: “Blessed is our God...” etc., the Trisagion, etc., and the Panikhida concludes with the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and with the Dismissal.

When the presiding Priest proclaims: “In blessed repose grant eternal rest, O Lord, unto Thy departed servant

=== LEAF p285 ===
[Book Page 261]

(p. 261)

his...” etc., he then sprinkles his body with holy water. — Unto which the choir: “Memory eternal.” After this, singing: “Holy God...” etc., the body is carried into the church.

In the church the body of the deceased is placed with the head toward the Sanctuary, and the face toward the people. The presiding Priest begins: “Blessed is our God...” etc. The verses from Kathisma 17 (“Blessed are the undefiled,” with the verse: “Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach us Thy statutes.”) “I am Thine...” etc., with the verse: “O Savior, save the soul of Thy servant.” At the conclusion, three times: “My soul shall live...” etc. After this “The choir of the Saints,” etc., with “Alleluia,” and thus the Little Litany. Sessional hymn: “Give rest, O Savior.”

After this the Prokeimenon, the Epistle, the Gospel. Litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” with the exclamation. Sticheron: “Today he is separated.”

And again: Prokeimenon, Epistle, and Gospel; litany: “Have mercy on us, O God...” etc.; sticheron: “The departing soul.”

In all there are 7 Epistles and Gospels with the appropriate litanies, prayers, and stichera.

After the 7th litany there is read: “In Thy Kingdom,” and thus follows the canon: “Unto Him Who of old drowned the pursuing tyrant in the waves of the sea,” consisting of 9 odes. After the 9th ode the Exapostilarion and the stichera “O deification in the departure...” etc., which are taken unto the verses “Praise the Lord from the heavens.”

The Doxology is read, and “Vouchsafe, O Lord.” And there follow the stichera of St. John of Damascus: “What earthly pleasure...” of the 8 Tones. After this “It is good to give praise,” “Our Father,” and the Troparia: “Give rest, O Savior,” Glory: “In Thy resting place, O Lord,” Now: “Holy Mother.” And thus the litany: “Have mercy on us,” with the exclamation.

After this the last kiss with the hymn: “Come, let us give the last kiss...” etc.; at this, bowing the knees, there takes place the prayer of absolution: “O compassionate and plenteous in mercy.” After this the Priest, standing before the remains of the deceased priest and holding the oil, saying: “May the Lord and our God Jesus Christ, Who strengthened thee in the faith...” etc. When, however, he pours the oil upon the deceased, he speaks: “Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia,” etc. And thus the Dismissal. Memory eternal.

Among us, besides this priestly burial, there is usually celebrated also another simple burial with the Liturgy, as was stated above.

§. 190.

The Order of Burial celebrated in Bright Week.

First of all it is proper to know that — if anyone departs in Bright Week — the order of singing at the burial on account of

=== LEAF p286 ===
[Book Page 262]

(p. 262)

the majesty of the Feast of the Resurrection is in a certain manner different.

The Priest, having come into the house in which the body of the deceased lies, and having put upon himself a bright (and not a black) phelonion and epitrachelion, goes into the house, singing: “Christ is risen...” etc. And having censed the remains in the house, he begins: “Blessed is our God...” etc. The choir: “Amen,” and “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) is sung 3 times (the Priest once, and the cantor 2 times). After this is sung unto the usual verses: “Let God arise...” etc., “Christ is risen,” “Glory, and Now,” “Christ is risen.” Litany: “Have mercy on us,” with the exclamation and the Dismissal. After this the Priest sprinkles the body with water, while the cantor sings: “Christ is risen.” And the remains are carried into the courtyard.[^302]

Here in the courtyard the Priest begins anew: “Blessed is our God...” etc., and having sung “Christ is risen,” the Paschal Canon begins: “The Day of Resurrection.” Between the odes are litanies. After the 6th ode the Kontakion: “With the saints give rest,” and the Ikos: “Thou alone,” and thus: “As many of you as have been baptized into Christ...” etc. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and “Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ,” with the sticheron: “Jesus having risen from the grave.” — After this are sung the 7th, 8th, and 9th odes of the canon with the Exapostilarion: “Having fallen asleep in the flesh.” And the Sunday Troparia: “The assembly of the Angels,” with the refrain: “Blessed art Thou, O Lord,” which conclude with: “Alleluia” (3 times), “Glory to Thee, O God.”

After this (instead of “Come, let us give the last kiss”) are sung the Paschal Stichera with verses (“A sacred Pascha”), while the people kiss the Cross. After the stichera the litany for the departed: “Have mercy on us, O God,” with the exclamation. Instead of “Memory eternal,” there is sung: “Christ is risen.”

And when the body is carried unto the cemetery, on the way also is sung: “Christ is risen.” At the cemetery everything is as usual. The Sunday Dismissal.[^303] Instead of “Memory eternal”: “Christ is risen.”[^304]

=== LEAF p287 ===
[Book Page 263]

(p. 263)

§. 191.

The Order of Parastas.[^305]

The presiding Priest and all the Priests present there, having entered into the Sanctuary, clothe themselves in epitrachelion and phelonion, and having gone out from the Sanctuary, stand by the tetrapod, upon which is placed a dish with kolyvo.

The presiding Priest, having taken the censer with incense, censing, begins: “Blessed is our God...” etc. (The censing from the 4 sides of the tetrapod takes place cruciformly.) At this they sing the “Trisagion.” After this: “Our Father,” “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (3 times), and Psalm 90 (“He that dwells in the aid...”); and thus the Litany of Peace.

After the Litany of Peace the cantors sing first “Alleluia” 3 times, and then the troparia: “With depth of wisdom.”

After the troparia are sung the verses from Kathisma 17, and that: first from the 1st stasis: “Blessed are the undefiled,” with the verse: “Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach us Thy statutes.” After this from the second stasis: “I am Thine,” with the verse: “O Savior, save the soul of Thy servant,” concluding with the sticheron: “My soul shall live.” After this is sung: “The choir of the Saints,” and it concludes with “Alleluia” (3 times). After this the Little Litany, when the Priest censes, and the Sessional hymn; and thus follows Psalm 50.

After this is sung the canon: “Having traversed the water.” After the 3rd ode of the canon takes place the Sessional hymn, after the 6th the Kontakion and Ikos, and after the 9th “It is truly meet,” when the Priests stand.

After this the Priests sing: “Holy God,” and the Little Panikhida begins with the litany for the departed and with the Dismissal. At the conclusion the Priest proclaims: “In blessed repose...” etc., and the cantor sings: “Memory eternal.”

=== LEAF p288 ===
[Book Page 264]

(p. 264)

§. 192.

Instruction concerning the Singing of the Parastas from Thomas Sunday unto the Apodosis of the Feast of the Resurrection.

After “Blessed is our God,” instead of the “Trisagion”: “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) 3 times. After this Glory, and Now; Most Holy Trinity; Lord, have mercy (3 times); Our Father, with the exclamation; and everything in order right up unto the canon.

In the canon, for the katavasia are taken the irmosi of the Paschal Canon.

Instead of “It is truly meet” (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*): “The Angel cried.”

Instead of the Trisagion: “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) three times. The rest in order; only at the conclusion after the Dismissal: “In blessed repose...”[^306] instead of “Memory eternal”[^307] there is taken: “Christ is risen.”

§. 193.

The Order of the Sanctification of Water on the Feast of Theophany.[^308]

After the Ambo Prayer[^309] the Priest, with candle-bearers preceding him, goes unto the place where in a certain vessel (in the font) water hath been prepared, while the choir sings the Troparia: “The voice of the Lord upon the waters,” during which singing the Priest censes the water. After this follow 3 Paremias [Old Testament readings], the Prokeimenon, the Epistle, the Gospel, and the Litany: “In peace let us pray to the Lord,” with the exclamation.

After this the Priest proclaims loudly: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and begins the prayer: “O Trinity beyond all essence,” in which prayer, when he comes unto the words: “Great art Thou, O Lord,” he takes a three-branched lighted candle and signs with it cruciformly the water (and that: he speaks these words 3 times, and 3 times signs the water); after this he continues the prayer, and when the Priest speaks the words: “Do Thou

=== LEAF p289 ===
[Book Page 265]

(p. 265)

therefore, O King Who lovest mankind,” he breathes upon the water cruciformly (and that: inasmuch as he speaks these words 3 times, therefore also 3 times he breathes); after this, continuing again, he comes unto the words: “Do Thou Thyself now also, O Master,” when he blesses the water cruciformly with his hand, dipping the fingers of his right hand cruciformly (and inasmuch as he speaks those words 3 times, so also 3 times he dips them); and continuing the prayer, he speaks: “And save, O Lord, Thy servant, our most faithful Emperor...” etc.; these words he proclaims loudly 3 times, and the choir answers 3 times: “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*); finally the Priest concludes this prayer: “Of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever...” etc. After the prayer: “Peace be unto all” (*Ми́ръ всѣ̑мъ*); “Bow your heads...” etc., and thus the exclamation, after which the Priest, having finished the prayer, takes with both hands the holy Cross and immerses it 3 times into the water, namely: lowering it cruciformly into the water and raising it up, during which is sung the troparion: “When Thou, O Lord, wast baptized in the Jordan...” etc., and that: 3 times.

After this the Priest, having kissed the Cross, sprinkles the Sanctuary and the whole church, and gives the Cross unto the people to kiss, and sprinkles them with holy water, while the choir sings the troparion: “When Thou, O Lord, wast baptized in the Jordan.” “Glory, and Now”: “Let us the faithful sing praise...” etc. After this takes place: “Blessed be the name of the Lord,” and thus the Dismissal: “May He Who deigned to be baptized in the Jordan by John for our salvation, Christ our true God...” etc.

§. 194.

The Order of the Sanctification of Water on the Feast of the Procession of the Precious Wood of the Venerable and Life-Giving Cross.[^310]

The Priest, having finished the Holy Liturgy and being vested in phelonion and epitrachelion, after the Ambo Prayer goes unto the Altar-Table, while the cantor sings: “God is the Lord and hath appeared unto us” (3 times) etc., with the troparion unto the Mother of God, “Glory, and Now”: “We shall never be silent.” Psalm 50, after which the Priest sings troparia in honor of the Mother of God: “Having received joy from the Angel,” while the cantor answers: “Most Holy Mother of God, save us.” After “Glory, and Now,” the Priest sings again troparia unto the Most Holy Mother of God, the Archangels, the Forerunner, the Apostles, the Unmercen-

=== LEAF p290 ===
[Book Page 266]

(p. 266)

-aries, with “Glory, and Now.” And the cantor answers: “Most Holy Lady, pray to God for us”; “Holy Apostles, pray to God for us”; “Holy Forerunner, pray to God for us,” etc. After these troparia the cantors begin to sing the troparia: “The doors of compassion,” and immediately follow the idiomela troparia: “Now the time hath come,” after which the Priest proclaims: “For holy art Thou,” the Trisagion, the Prokeimenon, the Epistle, and the Gospel. After this the Litany of Peace and the prayer: “O Lord our God...” etc., in which when the Priest speaks these words: “And send down the grace of Thy Holy Spirit,” he blesses the water cruciformly, dipping his fingers into it 3 times; in the continuation of this prayer he speaks 3 times the words: “Preserve Thy servant, our most faithful Emperor,” unto which the choir sings: “Lord, have mercy.” After the prayer, which ends with “now and forever, and unto the ages of ages,” follows: “Peace be unto all” (*Ми́ръ всѣ̑мъ*); “Bow your heads...” etc., with the exclamation. — After the exclamation the Priest blesses the water cruciformly, immersing the Cross into the water 3 times, lowering and raising it up, and sings the troparion: “Save, O Lord...” etc., which the choir also repeats 2 times. After this the Priest — having kissed the Cross and giving it unto the people to kiss — sprinkles all present with holy water, likewise the Sanctuary and the whole church. The cantor sings the stichera: “Fountain of healings.” After which follows: “Blessed be the name of the Lord,” and the Dismissal of the Liturgy.

§. 195.

The Order of the Sanctification of Water in Brief.

This sanctification is not celebrated publicly in church, but only privately (apart) in case of necessity for the sake of speed. — In church publicly only those two sanctifications of water are celebrated concerning which mention hath been made. — This brief sanctification, however, is celebrated thus:

The Priest, having taken upon himself the epitrachelion, begins: “Blessed is our God.” The cantor reads: Heavenly King; Trisagion; Our Father; Lord, have mercy (12 times); Come, let us worship (3 times). And immediately Psalm 28: “Bring unto the Lord, O ye sons of God...” etc.

After the Psalm: Glory, and Now; Alleluia (3 times), and there is taken the troparion: “Send down grace from heaven...” etc.

After this the Priest proclaims: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and reads over the water prepared in a vessel the prayer: “O God of great name...” etc.

After the prayer he immerses the Cross or the fingers of his right hand 

=== LEAF p291 ===
[Book Page 267]

(p. 267)

---


three times cruciformly into the water, lowering and raising it, and singing three times the troparion: “Save, O Lord, Thy people.”

After this he sprinkles with the holy water all those present, and makes the dismissal of the day.

§. 196.

Order of the Blessing of Palms.

The Blessing of Palms takes place on Palm Sunday at Matins. The cantor after the Gospel begins to read Psalm 50, and the Priest, without saying “Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ,” immediately goes forth unto the branches and censes them cruciformly. When the choir hath finished Psalm 50, the Priest proclaims: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and in a loud voice saith the prayer: “O Lord our God, Who sittest upon the Cherubim,” etc. After this: “Peace be unto all.” “Bow your heads,” etc., and the exclamation: “O Lord God Almighty,” etc. At the conclusion of this the Priest sprinkles the branches with holy water, saying: “These branches and palms are blessed and sanctified by the sprinkling of this holy water, in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, Amen,” etc. At the conclusion of this the Priest gives the Gospel to kiss and distributes the branches; and Matins continues as prescribed.

§. 197.

Order of the Blessing of Foods.

The Blessing of Foods takes place on Pascha Sunday.

At the Liturgy after the Ambo Prayer and after “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) hath been sung three times, etc., the Priest proclaims: “Glory to the Holy, Consubstantial,” etc., unto which the choir sings: “Amen,” etc. After this “Christ is risen” (*Христосъ воскресе*) three times, during which singing the Priest, having come unto the place where the artos and the foods have been prepared, censes them. After this he proclaims: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and recites three prayers, namely: a) for the blessing of the artos, b) for the blessing of the lamb and other meat foods, and c) for the blessing of dairy and other foods. And having finished these prayers, he sprinkles the foods with holy water, saying: “These foods are blessed and sanctified by the sprinkling of this holy water, in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit,” etc., and the Dismissal.

=== LEAF p292 ===
[Book Page 268]

(p. 268)

§. 198.

Order of the Blessing of a Sown Field.

The blessing of a sown field takes place around the feast of Saint Mark, and that in the following manner:

When the Priest at the Liturgy after “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (*Буди имя Господне*) hath pronounced the words: “Let us depart in peace,” etc., the procession, having gone forth from the church, with the cross-bearer preceding, goes unto the field. On the way various songs are sung, especially: “Our Father” (*Отче нашъ*) and “O Theotokos Virgin.”[^311]

Having arrived at the field at the designated place, the Priest first of all turns with his face toward the east (First Stasis), and the cantor begins to read Psalm 84: “Lord, Thou hast been favorable unto Thy land.” After this: “Glory, and Now. Alleluia” (three times), and the Priest proclaims: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and in a loud voice recites the prayer: “O Lord God from before all ages,” etc., and so follow the Gospel and the second prayer: “O Almighty God.” After this the Priest first conjures with the words: “I conjure you, aerial waves,” etc., afterwards sprinkles the field with holy water and blesses the seeds, saying: “May God bless,” etc., and with this he concludes the stasis.

After this the Priest turns toward the south (Second Stasis), and the choir reads Psalm 125: “When the Lord turned again the captivity of Zion”; after this the Priest in a loud voice recites the prayer: “O Holy Master,” then the Gospel, and again saith the prayer: “Multiply, we pray Thee, O Master”; after the prayer he first conjures, saying: “I conjure you, clouds,” etc., and so blesses the field with holy water, saying: “Bless, O Lord, the earth,” etc.

At the Third Stasis the Priest turns toward the west; the cantor reads Psalm 146: “Praise the Lord” (*Хвали́те*); after which the Priest in a loud voice recites the prayer: “O Master Jesus Christ,” reads the Gospel, and again the second prayer: “O Almighty,” etc., after which he first conjures with the words: “I conjure you, waves and winds,” etc., and only so blesses, saying: “May He bless you, the seeds,” etc.

At the Fourth Stasis he turns toward the north, and the choir reads Psalm 147: “Praise Jerusalem,” after which the Priest in a loud voice recites the prayer: “O Creator of all,” reads the Gospel, and the second prayer in a loud voice: “O Holy Master,” etc. And he conjures with the words: “I conjure you, devils,” etc.; afterwards he blesses the field, sprinkling with holy water, saying as before: “May He bless,” and with this is concluded also the Fourth Stasis, after which they return unto the church.

=== LEAF p293 ===
[Book Page 269]

(p. 269)

Here is pronounced the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc. (as at the Midnight Office), and thus the usual dismissal.

§. 199.

Order of the Blessing of Herbs.

Among us for the most part herbs are blessed on the day of the Nativity of Saint John the Baptist, in some places however on the Dormition of the Most Holy Virgin.

After the Liturgy after “Blessed be the name,” the Priest proclaims: “Blessed is our God,” and begins to cense the herbs, which the people hold standing on both sides from the Royal Doors even unto the church gates. The cantor, having sung “Amen,” saith: “Heavenly King,” “Trisagion,” “Our Father,” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times), “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (three times), and immediately reads Psalm 64 (“Praise is meet for Thee”). After the Psalm he sings the troparion and kontakion of the feast, unto which the Priest: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and in a loud voice recites the prayer: “O Almighty and Pre-Eternal God.” After this prayer he in a loud voice recites the second prayer: “O God, Who through Moses,” and so: “Peace be unto all,” “Bow your heads,” etc., with the exclamation. After the exclamation he sprinkles the herbs with holy water, walking in the midst of the people and saying: “May the blessing of God Almighty, the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, descend upon this creation and abide with it always. Amen,” etc. Dismissal.

§. 200.

What Other Blessings We Have.

a) The Blessing of Candles: it is twofold: on the feast of the Meeting of the Lord before the Holy Liturgy solemnly;[^312] on other feast days, however, it is performed in brief, and that in such a manner, that after “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (*Буди имя Господне*) the Priest proclaims: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and reads the prayer: “O God Almighty,” etc. After the prayer he sprinkles the candles with holy water, saying: “These lights are sanctified by the sprinkling of this holy water, in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, Amen.”

b) Grapes or vine berries and grain seeds are blessed on the feast of the Transfiguration of Christ, and that in token of thanksgiving for the receiving of these fruits and in test-

=== LEAF p294 ===
[Book Page 270]

(p. 270)

-imony that the wine and bread from these fruits must be brought for the sacred action.

c) On the feast of the Nativity of the Most Holy Virgin the field seeds that have already been sown are blessed.

d) On the day of the Falling Asleep of Saint John the Theologian takes place the blessing of wine.

Besides these, yet other blessings of this kind are traced in the Trebnik, but concerning these we do not speak.

§. 201.

Order of the Blessing and Laying of the First Stone for the Foundation of a Church.

The Priest, having received from the Bishop the authority for the foundation of a church and for the blessing of the first stone, performs this rite in this manner: On the preceding day the Priest erects a wooden cross upon that place where the Altar-Table is to be; and by the workmen on that place where the foundation is to be, trenches are excavated, and stones are prepared, of which one four-cornered stone is to be with the representation of the Holy Cross.

On the following day, on the day of the blessing, the Priest vests in bright vestments and goes with the whole clergy, the deacons preceding him with censers, and the church servers with holy water and with other necessary things, unto this place. As they go, the cantors sing the litiya stichera of the Saint unto whom the temple will be dedicated.

Having arrived at the place, they place upon the prepared Tetrapod candles, the Gospel, and the Holy Cross.[^313] And the Priest, having censed the Tetrapod cruciformly, proclaims: “Blessed is our God,” etc. After this follows: “Heavenly King,” “Trisagion,” “Our Father,” “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (three times), etc. Psalm 83: (“How lovely are Thy tabernacles, O Lord.”) The Litany of Peace and the troparia: “Thou Who hast founded it, O Christ,” etc.

After the troparia the Priest sprinkles with holy water the place where the Holy Cross is to be — or hath already been erected — and saith the prayer: “Deign, O Christ our God,” etc.; and having taken the Holy Cross, if it is not yet erected, he erects it, while the choir sings the troparion: “The Cross having been erected on the earth.” Afterwards the Priest

=== LEAF p295 ===
[Book Page 271]

(p. 271)

before the erected cross reads this prayer: “O Lord, Incomprehensible God,” etc.

After the prayer follow the Prokeimenon, the Epistle, the Gospel.

After this the Priest goes unto the trench where the foundation is to be laid, and with all the clergy sings in Tone 6: “Arise, as tomorrow,” etc., and there is first read Psalm 126: “Except the Lord build the house.” After this the Priest in a loud voice recites the prayers: “O Lord Jesus Christ,” and “Bless, O Lord, this creation.”

After the prayers the Priest sprinkles the stone with holy water and with his own hands lays it upon the foundation of the sanctuary, saying: “In the faith of Jesus Christ, this first stone, being laid into this foundation, I bless and establish: In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, Amen,” etc.

And the stone laid and strengthened he sprinkles with holy water, saying: “How awesome is this place,” etc.[^314]

After this the choir reads Psalm 86: “His foundation is in the holy mountains,” at the conclusion of which the Priest encompasses the whole place of the sanctuary, sprinkling it with holy water, going first toward the north, and after that toward the west.

Finally, having returned unto the place where he had begun to sprinkle, he stands before the erected cross, saying: “Again and again, having bowed our knees, let us pray to the Lord,” and there follow two prayers: “O God Almighty,” and “O Lord our God.” At the end, when they have stood up, the Litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” the Dismissal, and Many Years.

§. 202.

Order of the Blessing of a New Church.[^315]

The Priest, having permission from the Bishop, wishing to bless a new church, after the singing of Matins, vested in all the priestly vestments, goes with the other invited Priests unto the doors of the church, one holy cross preceding them, where first of all he saith secretly the prayer of intention: “O God of our action.” After this he begins in a loud voice: “Blessed is our God,” etc. “Heavenly King,” “Trisagion,” “Our Father,” “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (three times). Psalm 50.

=== LEAF p296 ===
[Book Page 272]

(p. 272)

During the reading of the Psalm the Priest, compassing round about from without the church, empty and stripped of all sanctuary adornments, sprinkles its walls with holy water, saying: “Thou shalt sprinkle me with hyssop,” etc. The cantor, having finished Psalm 50, sings the troparia: “The circuit of the sanctification today,” etc.

After the sprinkling from without, the Priest, standing before the church doors, proclaims in a loud voice: “With compunction, having bowed our knees, let us pray to the Lord,” and the Priest, having bowed his knees, saith the prayer: “O Lord God of mercy,” etc.

After this prayer the Priest, having arisen, signs the church doors cruciformly with his right hand, saying: “In the name of the Father, † and of the Son, † and of the Holy Spirit, † with peace let us enter.” The choir sings unto this: “In the name of the Lord,” etc., and the Priest, having opened the doors with the holy cross, first enters himself into the church, and after him the others, singing the sticheron: “Even as the house of Zacchaeus,” etc.

After the entry into the church the Priest stands before the Royal Doors, and the choir reads Psalm 121: (“I was glad of them that said unto me”), after which the Priest, having chanted the litany: “In peace let us pray to the Lord,” proclaims: “With compunction, all having bowed the knees, let us pray to the Lord,” and having bowed his knees, he reads in a loud voice the prayer: “O God, Almighty, All-Bountiful,” etc. After the prayer the cantor reads Psalm 120, during the reading of which the Priest, having arisen, sprinkles with holy water the Altar-Table, the Table of Oblation, and the right and left walls of the church, saying: “Thou shalt sprinkle me with hyssop, and I shall be cleansed.” The choir, having read the Psalm, sings beneath this the sticheron in Tone 6: “Bless, O Lord, this temple,” and again reads Psalm 25: “Judge me, O Lord,” etc.

After the reading of the Psalm (if there be not the Liturgy) follow: the Prokeimenon, the Epistle, the Gospel (of the sanctification of a church), and the sticheron: “O Lord, the ranks and hosts of Angels in the heavens,” etc.[^316]

And the Priest again before the great Throne proclaims: “Again and again, with compunction having bowed the knees, let us pray to the Lord,” and having bowed the knees, he saith the prayers: “O Lord God of hosts,” and “O God and Father Almighty,” etc.

After this the Priest sanctifies — if they be present — the new priestly vestments, and having vested in them, and having placed the Antimension, the Epistle, the Gospel, and the Holy Cross upon the holy Altar-Table, begins the Divine Litur-

=== LEAF p297 ===
[Book Page 273]

(p. 273)

-gy. — After the Gospel, however, or after the Ambo Prayer he reads the prayer: “Unoriginate, Unchangeable,” etc.

At the end is sung the thanksgiving hymn of Saint Ambrose: “We praise Thee, O God,” and thus the dismissal and Many Years.

§. 203.

Order of the Blessing of the Altar-Table Itself.

The Altar-Table is twofold: one immovable, and the other movable, that is: the Antimension. Both are consecrated by the Bishop, but for the blessing of an immovable Altar-Table a Priest also can have authority from the Bishop.

The Priest, wishing to bless an Altar-Table, vests in all the priestly vestments and, having come before the Altar-Table that is to be blessed, stands there before the placed Tetrapod. Upon this Tetrapod are placed the Cross, lighted candles, holy water, a sprinkler, and likewise also the Antimension, which must be placed upon the new Altar-Table.

After this he performs this rite thus: First, standing before the Tetrapod, he proclaims: “Blessed is our God,” etc. “Heavenly King,” etc., “Trisagion,” etc., “Our Father.” “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (three times). Psalm 83: (“How lovely are Thy dwellings, O Lord,” etc.) During the Psalm the Priest sprinkles the Altar-Table with holy water, saying: “Thou shalt sprinkle me with hyssop, and I shall be cleansed; Thou shalt wash me, and I shall be made whiter than snow.” The cantor, after the Psalm, sings the troparion of the Saint whose image is above the Altar-Table.

The Priest, after the sprinkling, places the Antimension upon the Altar-Table, and censes the table. After this he proclaims: “With compunction, having bowed our knees, let us pray to the Lord,” and the Priest, having bowed his knees, in a loud voice reads the prayer: “O Lord God of hosts,” etc. At the end the usual dismissal of the day, and the Divine Liturgy is celebrated upon the newly-blessed Altar-Table.

§. 204.

Order of the Blessing of Altar Linens.

The linens are placed upon the Tetrapod before the Royal Doors. The Priest, vested in epitrachelion and phelonion, goes forth from the Sanctuary and, having censed them cruciformly, proclaims in a loud voice: “Blessed is our God.” After this: Heavenly King. Trisagion. Our Father. Lord, have mercy (12 times). Come, let us worship (three times). And Psalm 92: (“The Lord reigns.”)

=== LEAF p298 ===
[Book Page 274]

(p. 274)

After the Psalm the Priest proclaims: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and reads the prayer: “O Lord God, Father Almighty,” etc. Unto this: “Peace be unto all.” “Bow your heads unto the Lord,” and the Exclamation.

After the Exclamation the Priest sprinkles the coverings, saying: “These coverings are sanctified by the grace of the All-Holy Spirit, by the sprinkling of this holy water, in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, Amen.”

And having made the usual Dismissal, he takes them himself and covers with them the Altar-Table and the Table of Oblation.

§. 205.

Order of the Blessing and Sanctification of Sacred Vessels, that is: the Diskos, Chalice, Star, and Spoon.

To bless and sanctify the Chalice, Diskos, Star, Spoon, and Ciborium pertains properly unto the Bishop alone; but if a Priest receives authority for this from the Bishop, he performs this in this manner:

Before the Royal Doors a Tetrapod is placed, and upon it the vessels. The Priest, vested in epitrachelion and phelonion, comes hither and first censes them cruciformly, and after this proclaims: “Blessed is our God.” Heavenly King. Trisagion. Our Father. Lord, have mercy (12 times). Come, let us worship (12 times). And thus is read Psalm 22: “The Lord is my shepherd.”

After the Psalm the Priest proclaims: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and in a loud voice recites the prayer: “O Master Almighty,” etc.

After this prayer he first sanctifies the chalice and cup, proclaiming: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and saying the prayer: “O Master Almighty, unto Thee we pray,” etc., and immediately: “Peace be unto all.” “Bow your heads,” and the exclamation: “For Thou art the fountain,” etc. And upon this he sprinkles the vessels with holy water, saying: “These vessels are sanctified by the grace of the All-Holy Spirit, by the sprinkling of this holy water, in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, Amen.”

After this, wishing to sanctify the Star, he again proclaims: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and reads the prayer: “O Lord Jesus Christ our God,” etc. After the prayer he sprinkles the Star with holy water, saying: “This Star is sanctified by the grace of the All-Holy Spirit, by the sprinkling of this holy water: in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, Amen.”

Finally he sanctifies the Spoon, proclaiming: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and reads the prayer: “O Lord God Almighty, with the coal taken with tongs by the Seraph,” etc. Then he sprinkles the Spoon with holy water, saying: “This Spoon is sanctified by the grace

=== LEAF p299 ===
[Book Page 275]

(p. 275)

of the All-Holy Spirit, by the sprinkling of this holy water, in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, Amen,” and thus he makes the dismissal of the day.

Note: The blessing and sanctification of the Ciborium, that is, the Tabernacle, is performed in the likeness of the blessing and sanctification of the Chalice.

§. 206.

Order of the Blessing of New Priestly Vestments.[^317]

Before the Royal Doors upon the prepared Tetrapod are placed the new vestments brought for blessing. The Priest, vested in epitrachelion and phelonion, first censes them, saying as usual: “Blessed is our God,” etc. After this: “Amen.” “Heavenly King,” etc. Trisagion. Our Father. Lord, have mercy (12 times). “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (three times). And Psalm 92. After the Psalm the Priest proclaims: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and reads the prayer: “O Lord God Almighty,” etc. After this: “Peace be unto all.” “Bow your heads unto the Lord,” and the exclamation. After the exclamation the Priest sprinkles the vestments set forth with holy water, saying: “These vestments are sanctified (he names them) by the grace of the All-Holy Spirit, by the sprinkling of this holy water, in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit,” etc. And the dismissal of the day and Many Years.

§. 207.

Order of the Blessing of Church Banners.

The Priest, vested in epitrachelion and phelonion, having censed the banners, begins: “Blessed is our God,” etc., unto which the choir answers: “Amen,” and saith: “Trisagion,” and “Our Father.” After the exclamation the Priest saith: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and in a loud voice recites the prayer: “O Eternal God without beginning.” And he sprinkles the banners with holy water, saying: “These banners are sanctified by the grace of the All-Holy Spirit, by the sprinkling of this holy water: in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, Amen,” and the Dismissal.

§. 208.

Order of the Blessing of the Iconostasis.

An Iconostasis can be erected either in a blessed church or in an unblessed church.

=== LEAF p300 ===
[Book Page 276]

(p. 276)

a) If the Iconostasis is erected in a blessed church, then it is blessed in this manner: The Priest, vested in epitrachelion and phelonion, begins before the Royal Doors, saying: “Blessed is our God.” Heavenly King. Trisagion. Our Father. Come, let us worship (three times). And immediately the cantor reads Psalm 131: (“Lord, remember David”), during which the Priest censes, and after this, having said “Let us pray to the Lord,” in a loud voice recites the prayer: “O Lord our God, God of gods,” etc., after the prayer: “Peace be unto all,” “Bow your heads,” and the exclamation: “For Thou art the God of mercy.” After this he sprinkles with holy water the whole Iconostasis from the bottom even unto the top, saying: “These Icons are sanctified by the sprinkling of this holy water in honor and glory of the great God and our Savior Jesus Christ, and in memory of His Saints: in the name of the Father, † and of the Son, † and of the Holy Spirit, † Amen.” — After this the cantor reads the troparia: “Unto the Most Pure Image.” Glory: “Apostles, Prophets,” etc. And Now: “With love, O Pure Virgin,” etc. And thus he makes the usual dismissal.

b) But if the Iconostasis is erected in an unblessed church, and it is to be blessed by a Priest, then first the church is blessed, and so before the dismissal the Iconostasis is blessed. That is: the Priest, having stood before the Royal Doors, after he hath first said: “Let us pray to the Lord,” reads the prayer: “O Lord our God, God of gods,” etc., and after the prayer continues as was said above, and only thus makes the dismissal.

§. 209.

Order of the Blessing of Bells (Cymbals).

The bell that is to be blessed is suspended in a prepared place, and that in such a manner that it may be sprinkled both from without and from within. The Priest, having taken upon himself the epitrachelion and phelonion, begins: “Glory to the Holy,” etc. And immediately: “Trisagion,” and “Our Father.” After the exclamation the choir sings the troparion: “Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God,” with the kontakion: “When He came down and confused the tongues.”

After this the Priest with his hand blesses the bell three times, saying: “In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit,” etc., and in a loud voice proclaims the Litany: “In peace let us pray to the Lord.”

After the Litany the Priest gives a name unto the bell, saying: “In the name of the Lord Jesus Christ, let this bell be named (N.), according to the grace given unto us from the Life-Creating Spirit, Amen,” and having taken the censer, he censes the bell from four sides from without and from within. The choir unto this reads Psalm 28.

---

=== LEAF p301 ===
[Book Page 277]

(p. 277)

After the Psalm the Priest proclaims in a loud voice: “Again and again, bending our knees, let us pray to the Lord.” And having bent his knees and laid his hand upon the bell, he proclaims in a loud voice the prayer: “O Lord our God,” etc. After this the Priest, having stood up, sprinkles the bell with holy water on four sides on the exterior and on four sides from within, saying: “This bell is blessed and sanctified,” etc., and having lifted up his hands he saith: “Glory to Thee, O Holy Trinity, our God, glory to Thee!”
After this he anoints the bell with holy myron, saying: “Unto the glory of the Holy Trinity. Alleluia” (three times). — The choir unto this reads Psalm 142, and the Priest after the Psalm proclaims in a loud voice the prayer: “O God, the Only One,” etc., and immediately makes the Dismissal.

§. 210.

Order of the Blessing of a Bell Tower.

The Priest — having received authority for this blessing — comes unto the newly dedicated bell tower and stands on the western side facing the east. Before the Priest stands a Tetrapod, upon which are placed: burning candles, the Cross, the Gospel, and in a vessel holy water with a sprinkler. — Having taken upon himself the epitrachelion and phelonion, he censes round about the bell tower and begins the rite: “Blessed is our God,” etc. (“Heavenly King,” “Trisagion,” “Come, let us worship,” Psalm 60). After the Psalm is sung: the troparion of the church with its kontakion.
After this the Priest proclaims: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and in a loud voice reads the prayer: “O Lord our God,” etc.; after the prayer he sprinkles round about the bell tower, saying: “By the sprinkling of this holy water this tower is sanctified unto the glory of the Lord,” etc. After this he stands before the tower, looking toward the east, and begins: “And that we may be accounted worthy,” etc., and reads the Gospel, after which follows the Litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and the Dismissal.

§. 211.

Order of the Blessing of a Cross to be Erected upon the Roof of a Newly Built Church.

The Priest in epitrachelion censes the cross and begins as usual: “Blessed is our God,” etc., “Trisagion,” “Our Father,” etc. And immediately the troparia are sung: “Save, O Lord, Thy people,” “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): Kontakion: “Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross of Thine own will,” “Both now” (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): “Protection of Christians.” After this the Priest, having said “Let us pray to the Lord,” proclaims in a loud voice the prayer: “O Lord Almighty,” after which

=== LEAF p302 ===
[Book Page 278]

(p. 278)

he sprinkles the holy cross with holy water, saying: “This sign of the Cross is blessed and sanctified by the grace of the Holy Spirit, and by the sprinkling of this holy water,” etc. Unto this is sung the kontakion (“Thou Who wast lifted up”), and the Priest makes the usual dismissal, while the workmen erect the holy cross upon the top of the church.

§. 212.

Order of the Blessing of a Cemetery.

On the day preceding the blessing of the cemetery the Priest erects upon it a tall wooden cross, according to the height of a man, and beside that same cross he erects a single wooden candlestick for the placement of three candles.
On the following day in the morning the Priest, vested in priestly vestments, with other priests, preceded by the cross-bearer, goes unto the cross erected in the cemetery, carrying with them the Trebnik, three candles, holy water, and a censer. Having arrived at the place of the erected cross and the candles having been lit and set upon the three-branched candlestick, he begins: “Blessed is our God,” etc. The choir unto this saith: “Amen,” and “Heavenly King,” “Trisagion,” “Our Father,” “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (three times), Psalm 50.
After the Psalm the Priest intones the Litany of Peace, and after this he sprinkles the erected cross and the entire place of the cemetery, saying: “Thou shalt sprinkle me with hyssop, and I shall be cleansed; Thou shalt wash me, and I shall be made whiter than snow.” And during this the choir sings the troparia: “Thee we implore,” etc., after which the Priest proclaims in a loud voice the prayer: “O God Almighty.”
And immediately follows: the Prokimenon, the Apostle, and the Gospel. After this the Priest saith the prayer: “O God of all the world,” and “Peace be unto all,” “Bow your heads unto the Lord,” etc., and the exclamation: “For Thou art the Resurrection.” After this the Priest, of the three lighted candles, affixes one upon the top, and the other two upon the arms of the cross, and censes and sprinkles the Holy Cross. — At the end there is a litany for the departed (“Have mercy on us, O God” (*Помилуй насъ Боже*)) and thus the Dismissal.

§. 213.

Order of the Blessing and Sanctification of a Newly Erected Cross.

The Priest, vested in epitrachelion and phelonion, having come unto the cross begins: “Blessed is our God,” unto which the choir sings: “Amen,” and there follows: “Trisagion,” “Our Father,” “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (three times).

=== LEAF p303 ===
[Book Page 279]

(p. 279)

Psalm 98, and the cantors sing the troparia: “Save, O Lord, Thy people,” “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): Kontakion: “Thou Who wast lifted up,” “Both now” (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): “The Life-Creating Cross.”
After this all sing three times the troparion: “Send down Thy grace.”
After the singing of the troparia the Priest proclaims in a loud voice the prayer: “O Lord God,” and “Peace be unto all,” “Bow your heads,” etc., and the exclamation: “For Thou art a God of mercy and compassions,” etc., and then he sprinkles the cross with holy water, saying: “This sign of the Cross is sanctified,” etc., and censes it crosswise unto the stichera: “The Cross is the guardian of the whole inhabited earth.”
At the end is sung: “We bow before Thy Cross,” etc., three times, and then while the choir sings “Come, ye faithful,” first the Priest, and then the rest bow down and kiss the holy cross, after which follows the Dismissal.

§. 214.

Order of the Blessing of a New House.[^318]

The Priest coming into the house that is to be blessed brings holy water with him in a vessel, likewise also oil blessed at the All-Night Vigil in a small vessel. The house that is to be blessed must be prepared in this manner: that upon the four walls the sign of the cross be drawn, and in the midst of the house a Tetrapod covered with an embroidered cloth, and upon it are placed the holy cross and burning candles.
The Priest, having come into the house, having taken upon himself the epitrachelion and phelonion, begins as usual: “Blessed is our God,” etc., “Heavenly King,” “Trisagion,” “Our Father,” “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (12 times), “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (three times), etc. And Psalms 126, 127, and 132 are read. After the Psalms the troparion: “As unto the house of Zacchaeus,” etc., unto which the Priest proclaims: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and in a loud voice reads the prayers: “To Thee we fervently pray,” etc., and “Visit, we pray Thee,” etc.
After these prayers the Priest first sprinkles the walls of the house with holy water, saying first: a) “In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, Amen”; b) after this: “By this sprinkling of holy water may the Lord grant unto this house and unto the things found therein His blessing and a peaceful dwelling”; c) at the end: “May the blessing of God the Almighty, the Father, † and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, † descend upon this place and abide forever. Amen.”

=== LEAF p304 ===
[Book Page 280]

(p. 280)

After this, secondly, he anoints with oil the walls of the house, having drawn the sign of the cross, and that in the center of the drawn cross, beginning from the eastern wall, then the western, northern, and finally the southern, at each anointing saying: “This house is blessed by the anointing of this holy oil in the name of the Father, † and of the Son, † and of the Holy Spirit, † Amen.”[^319] — After this anointing he lights the candles prepared before each sign of the cross. The choir during this sprinkling and during this anointing sings the sticheron: “Bless, O Lord, this house.” When the anointing is completed, the Priest, standing toward the east, reads Psalm 100, and during this the Priest censes the whole house, and immediately sings the Litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” after the Litany makes the Dismissal, and proclaims Many Years.

§. 215.

What the Priest Performs on the First Day of the Holy Great Fast.[^320]

On this day (that is, on the First Monday of the Great Fast), after the divine service of the day, the Priest instructs his spiritual children how they ought to prepare themselves through the holy Fast for the Passions of Christ. And having instructed them sufficiently, he bids them bend their knees before the Lord and proclaims in a loud voice the prayer: “O Lord Jesus Christ our God, the Hope of all the ends of the earth,” etc. And after the prayer he blesses them with his right hand, saying: “The blessing of the Lord be upon you, by His grace and love for mankind, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages.”

§. 216.

What is the Dread Prayer?

The dread prayer is a service over those who suffer from demons. It is the adjuration of the devil. It consists of Psalms 142, 22, 26, 64, and 50, further of the canon to Jesus Christ, to the Most Pure Virgin, to the Bodiless Powers, to the Apostles, and to All Saints, and of the exorcistic prayers of Saint Basil the Great and Saint John Chrysostom.[^321]

=== LEAF p305 ===
[Book Page 281]

(p. 281)

It is the most important exorcism. This service is not permitted to be celebrated without permission obtained for each individual case from the Bishop.
Besides this exorcism, there is in the Church also: $\alpha$) the adjuration of the devil over the Catechumen before Holy Baptism; and $\beta$) over the water at the blessing of the same; $\gamma$) there is a prayer over a dwelling disturbed by evil spirits; and $\delta$) there is a prayer for the driving away of insects and other noxious vermin harmful to fields and vineyards.

§. 217.

Order of the Akathist to the Dormition of the Most Holy Virgin Mary.[^322]

The Priest, in epitrachelion, standing before the Royal Doors, proclaims: “Blessed is our God.” The choir: “Amen,” and immediately “Heavenly King,” “Trisagion,” “Our Father,” and the exclamation. After the exclamation the Priest enters into the Sanctuary, and the cantors sing the troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord,” etc. “Glory” (*Сла́ва*): “Lord, have mercy on us,” “Both now” (*И҆ ны́нѣ*): “Open unto us the doors of compassion.” After this: Lord, have mercy (12 times), “Glory, Both now,” “Come, let us worship” (*Прїиди́те поклони́мся*) (three times), and immediately Psalm 142: “Lord, hear.” After the Psalm, “Glory, Both now,” and the troparion: “In giving birth, O Virgin.” After the troparion: Lord, have mercy (three times), “Glory, Both now,” and Psalm 50: “Have mercy on me, O God.”
After this the Priest comes forth, and before the Tetrapod, vested in phelonion, sings alternately 13 kontakia and 13 ikoi. (The kontakia are: “To the chosen from all generations,” “Seeing the Apostles,” “The power of the Most High,” “Having within a storm,” “The God-guided stars,” “The God-bearing heralds, the Apostles,” “Desiring all,” “Seeing Thee strange in the highest,” “All nature,” “Desiring to save the world,” “All singing,” “The grace of high-enthroned glory,” and “O all-hymned Mother.” — And the ikoi are: “The foremost Angel,” “All rational creation,” “The disciple having within a thought,” “The initiates having heard,” “The God-beholders seeing their Master,” “Unto us the Sun of righteousness hath shone,” “Showing a new wonder,” “All being on high,” “The oratory of the God-enlightened,” “A rampart art Thou unto the faithful,” “The light-receiving lamp,” “Singing Thine all-venerable dormition,” and “The foremost Angel.”[^323])

=== LEAF p306 ===
[Book Page 282]

(p. 282)

At the end the Priest again sings once more the kontakion: “To the chosen from all generations,” etc.
Unto this the cantors sing the Exaposteilarion of the feast three times: “Ye Apostles from the ends of the earth.”
After the Exaposteilarion, “Lord, have mercy” (*Господи помилуй*) (three times), and immediately the Priest, standing before the Tetrapod, proclaims: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and kneeling, reads two prayers. The first: “O Most Holy Theotokos, Virgin Lady,” etc. After the first prayer he again proclaims: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and only then reads the second prayer: “O my Most Gracious Queen,” etc.
After the prayers the cantors sing: “It is truly proper”; and immediately “Trisagion,” “Our Father,” the troparion: “In giving birth, virginity,” the Litany: “Have mercy on us, O God.” “Again we pray that this city may be preserved,” etc., the exclamation: “Hear us, O God,” etc. “Glory to Thee, O Christ God.” The Dismissal of the Dormition: “The blessing of the Lord be upon you,” etc.[^324]

## APPENDIX

§. 218.

When We Use the Censer.[^325]

We use the censer in our divine services many times, namely:
1.
